# CLAUDE.md

The Admin Panel is the web app Shor's staff use to administer the Discord server: a Discord login for the staff, blocked words kept in a Discord AutoMod rule (ADR 0002), and an audit log (ADR 0004). It is a Next.js app (App Router) in TypeScript under `src/app/`, hosted on Vercel's free Hobby plan, at https://shor-admin-panel.vercel.app, a `vercel.app` domain with no domain bought for it (Gilles, 2026-10-24, while grilling #51), which Hobby allows since Shor makes no money (the POs, relayed by Gilles on 2026-10-29, on #13), from this repository, public since 2026-10-27 so that Hobby can deploy it (ADR 0007, #100), that talks to Discord's REST API only (ADR 0003). As of 2026-10-28 it serves, to the staff alone, through the Discord login of `src/staff/` and its pages under `/login` (#10), beside a sidebar from 1,024 px wide, a rail of icons from 768 to 1,023 px, or under a bar that sticks to the top of the page, and its menu, on a screen narrower than 768 px (#224, #356), that names the member, beside their avatar, a link to their own record for those who moderate (#140, #320), and links each section, `/blocked-words`, whose link counts the blocked words (#226), to which `/` redirects (#71), where they list, add and remove the blocked words of the panel's AutoMod rules (#11), one for each action a blocked word carries, « Bloquer seulement » to every staff member and, to those who also hold a moderation role, a timeout of one hour or of one day that the Discord server gives by itself to the member who writes the word, an automatic sanction (#325), and change the action of a word from its tile, through a pencil and a dialog (#326), the list filtered by what a word contains (#229), and `/audit-log`, where they read who changed those rules, or timed out, kicked or banned an account or lifted its timeout or ban, or which account AutoMod timed out, and when, from Discord's audit log, 50 changes a page, of every type or of the one they choose, the blocked words, the timeouts, AutoMod's included, the kicks or the bans (#231, #327), and of the author and the account they search, by name, ID or mention, of a text found in the reason of a change or in its word, and of a period of days of Paris, from one day to another, all five combined with the type (#321, #393), grouped by day of Paris, each at its time, after the icon of its kind, beside the avatar of the member who made it, or under « AutoMod », with no avatar and no link, a sanction with its reason, or an automatic timeout with the rule that gave it, and, for those who moderate, a link to its account's record, as the avatar and the name of the member who made it are, for them, a link to theirs (#12, #141, #182, #230, #320, #327), and `/game-log`, where those who view the game server's channel on Discord read the lines its webhook posts there, searched by a word and by a period, and the others get neither the link nor the page (#394, `.claude/rules/game-log.md`), and `/support-tickets`, where they read the support tickets still open that they view on Discord, oldest first, each with its request kind, who opened it, when it opened and was last active, its number of messages, the staff who answered, an extract of its last message and a link to its channel (#388, ADR 0009), then the archives of the closed ones, newest first, 25 a page, each with its request kind, who opened it and who closed it, when and why, which the Bot posts in the archives channel and the panel reads there through Discord's search of the Discord server's messages, the opener and the closer being, for those who moderate, a link to their record (#385, ADR 0010), filtered by request kind, by opener and closer, by a period on the closing or on the opening and by a word of the reason, newest first or oldest first, with the filters, the order and the page in the URL (#386), and, to those who also hold a moderation role, `/records`, where they read every account, members and banned accounts mixed, ranked by name, or by arrival or by highest role, 25 a page with their total, the search, the filters, the sort and the page in the URL (#322, #324), each member with the badge of their highest role, in the color of that role on Discord (#323), search them by the start of a username, a global name or a nickname, filter them by role, standing, arrival on the Discord server and age of the Discord account, with the bot accounts hidden unless they ask (#324), find an account by its ID or by its mention, and open its record, `/records/{userId}`, with its names, dates, roles and standing on the Discord server (#176), where they time out a member for one of seven durations, or lift their timeout, with a reason that Discord's audit log keeps in the format of `src/audit-log/`, and a direct message to the member, through `src/sanctions/`, which refuses beforehand what the role hierarchy forbids (#179), where they kick a member, with a reason and a direct message sent before the kick (#180), where those who also hold a role of `BAN_ROLE_IDS` ban an account, a member with a direct message sent before the ban, or an account absent from the Discord server by its ID, deleting its messages of up to the last 7 days, and lift a ban, each with a reason (#181), a kick and a ban once a dialog confirms it (#235), and where they read, under every record, the history of the account's sanctions and their lifting that Discord's audit log keeps, given through the panel, in Discord or by AutoMod (#177), and, under that, the archives of the support tickets that account opened, which the Bot posts in the archives channel and the panel reads there (#197, ADR 0009), in the Hydromel palette of the shadcn/ui theme, light or dark as the system is set, or as each staff member chooses through a toggle (#70, #222, #225, **Interface**), and, for a page that does not exist or fails to show, French error pages (#61, **Error pages**), and, to anyone, with or without a session, its privacy policy, `/privacy`, which the login pages and the navigation link (#186, **Privacy policy**); `src/discord/` holds the client of Discord's REST API, which the login and the staff check call, its in-memory fake and the HTTP adapter that answers for Discord over a fake (#59, (#63).

## How we work

The process lives in Matt Pocock's skills (`mattpocock/skills`). This file only adds what the skills can't know about this repo.

Reach for these yourself:

- **tdd** for every behaviour change.
- **diagnosing-bugs** for any bug that survives a first look: a tight loop that goes red on this bug comes before any theory.
- **domain-modeling** the moment a term is fuzzy, overloaded, or disagrees with `GLOSSARY.md`, and whenever a decision clears the ADR bar.
- **code-review** against the originating ticket before any commit that closes one.
- **groom-backlog** before your final report, when the session changed the repo or leaves loose ends, or once a burst of tracker writes is over: an issue created, edited, labelled, closed or re-parented, a PR opened or merged, work started on an issue. A hook reminds you after each write.

The orchestrating skills are run by the user. When you see the moment for one, suggest it in a single line, then carry on:

- non-trivial change, not grilled yet → `/grill-with-docs`
- grilled change too big for one session → `/to-spec`, then `/to-tickets`
- effort too foggy to scope → `/wayfinder`
- the answer lives in a teammate's head (the POs, the staff, loanspac) → `/to-questionnaire`
- an epic of `ready-for-agent` and `skill:implement` sub-tickets, to deliver without Gilles, its `ready-for-human` ones walked through with him in guide sessions → `/deliver-epic <n>`
- every `ready-for-agent` and `skill:implement` ticket of the backlog, to deliver without Gilles, one at a time in board order → `/deliver-backlog`

## Language

Three things are in **French**: chat with the user, GitHub issues and their board (title, body, comments, and the board's descriptions and view names; see `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`), and the panel's interface, which is French only, with no translation layer. Everything else is in **English**: docs, `GLOSSARY.md`, ADRs, PR text and PR comments, commit messages, and every word written into the codebase: file names, identifiers, logs. URLs count as file names, since each segment is a folder under `src/app/`: `/blocked-words`, not `/mots-interdits`.

## No comments

The repository holds no comments, in any file that supports them: code, tests, scripts, CSS, HTML, Markdown, SQL, YAML, Python, PowerShell, `.env.example`, whose variables are documented in the README. Two exceptions only: a shebang, and a tool directive the code needs to work, which stands alone in its comment, with no text of its own, in the forms **Directives** allows and in a script file only.

What a comment would say goes elsewhere: the why of a change in its commit message, a decision in an ADR, a term in `GLOSSARY.md`, an invariant in a test and its name. Code copied from elsewhere, such as shadcn/ui or EsportCards, loses its comments on the way in, and comes in only as its license allows (**Copying code from another project**).

`pnpm check:comments` checks every tracked or untracked, non-ignored file of the repository that holds the folder it runs from (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`), so a subfolder or a worktree checks its own repository. A hook runs it before `gh pr create` and its alias `gh pr new`, and CI on every PR and every push to `main`. The hook recognises those two with the repository chosen by `-R` or `--repo`, before `pr` or between `pr` and the verb, spelled `-R o/r`, `-R=o/r`, `-Ro/r`, `--repo o/r` or `--repo=o/r`: gh 2.101.0 answered `gh pr view` with each of them in both places, and `gh pr create --help` lists `-R, --repo` under « INHERITED FLAGS » (2026-10-30, #293). It recognises them too when a backslash, or a backtick for PowerShell, at the end of a line splits the command, since it joins those lines before it looks (`test/check-comments.test.mjs`; the PowerShell backtick is inferred from PowerShell's rule, not tried; decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #293, for Gilles to review). The hook runs it from the `cwd` of its input, which follows Claude into a worktree, while `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, where the hook's own copy of the script sits, stays at the project root where the session started (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, read 2026-10-30), so a PR from a worktree gets that worktree checked (inferred from those docs and from tests in temporary repositories, since no session in a worktree has tried it). From a folder in no repository it refuses the PR, since it has nothing to check, rather than check the repository of its own copy (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #19, for Gilles to review).

It reads these types (`familyOf`, in `scripts/check-comments.mjs`): script files (`.js`, `.mjs`, `.cjs`, `.jsx`, `.ts`, `.mts`, `.cts`, `.tsx`), JSON (`.json`, `.jsonc`: `//` and `/* */`), CSS (`.css`, `.scss`), markup (`.html`, `.htm`, `.svg`, `.xml`), Markdown (`.md`: `<!-- -->`, a link definition that comments, and `#` in its YAML header), SQL (`.sql`: `--` and `/* */`, a `'` or `"` string running over several lines, and a backslash that escapes only in an `E'…'` string, as PostgreSQL has read it since 9.1 (https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/sql-syntax-lexical.html, §4.1.2.2, read 2026-10-30); a `$$…$$` or `$tag$…$tag$` body is read as SQL up to its closing delimiter, the first one after the opening one, with the tag case sensitive, as PostgreSQL ends the string there (the same section, §4.1.2.4, read 2026-10-30), so its comments are reported, its strings are read as elsewhere, and a nested body with another tag is read to its own delimiter; a `'` or `"` left open in a body, and a delimiter never closed, make the file unreadable, so a body of text with a lone apostrophe, `$$don't$$`, where PostgreSQL needs no escape (« single quotes can be used without needing to be escaped », the same section), is reported as unreadable and is written as a `'don''t'` string instead (#309); a `$` opens no delimiter right after an identifier character, since PostgreSQL asks a dollar-quoted string that follows a keyword or an identifier to be separated from it by whitespace (the same section), nor when it is a parameter, `$1`, or has no closing `$`, and a tag starts with a letter, an underscore or a character outside ASCII (`test/check-comments.test.mjs`, #309); the check reads only the character before the `$`, where PostgreSQL's lexer follows whole identifiers (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #309, for Gilles to review)), YAML (`.yml`, `.yaml`), Python (`.py`: `#`), PowerShell (`.ps1`: any `#` outside a string, and `<# #>`), and the files with `#` comments: `.toml`, `.sh`, `.env*`, `.gitignore`, `.gitattributes`, `.prettierignore`, `.git-blame-ignore-revs`, `.npmrc`, `.editorconfig` and `Dockerfile`. In Markdown, a fenced code block (` ``` ` or `~~~`) and an inline code span are code, not comments, so a passage may quote `<!-- -->` in either, as **The browser's console** and a line of `.claude/rules/e2e.md` do; an indented code block is not recognised, so a comment in one is reported, and a line that opens an HTML comment ends an inline code span, as a blank line does (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #22, for Gilles to review). A link definition whose destination is `#` or `<>` and that carries a title, such as `[//]: # (why)`, `[//]: <> (why)` or `[comment]: # "why"`, is a comment too, since it shows nothing in a rendering and its title is all it holds (inferred, not checked against CommonMark's specification). An ordinary definition, such as `[docs]: https://example.com "Title"`, is not reported. The check reports a definition that fills a line, whatever its label, its indentation or the quote or list marker before it, as long as the label holds a character that is neither a space nor a bracket, and also right after a line of a paragraph, where CommonMark would render it as text (inferred, not checked against its specification). So it reports a link to `#` with a title, such as `[top]: # "Top"`, a false positive that shows where a missed comment would hide. It does not recognise a definition cut over several lines, with its destination or its title on the next line, its title across lines, or a `\]` in its label, so a comment written so goes unreported (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #297, for Gilles to review). A Markdown file's YAML header is the lines between a `---` on its very first line, which a byte order mark may precede, and the next `---` line, provided that at least one of them is a `key:` line, one that starts at its first column with anything but `#` and holds a colon followed by a space or by the end of the line. Without one, both `---` are rules and the file is plain Markdown: `---`, a `# Title` and `---` read as a heading between two rules, and a `<!-- -->` between them is reported. Ten tracked files open with a header, the three `SKILL.md` of `.claude/skills/` and the seven ADRs (`git ls-files '*.md'` and the first line of each file, `ef968f8`, 2026-10-30). A header is read as a `.yml` file is, so a `#` there is a comment, while the Markdown after it is read as before, where a `# Title` is a heading, and a `---` that is not on the first line opens no header. Nine of the ten headers parse as YAML; `groom-backlog`'s `SKILL.md` does not, its `description` holding `work: settle`, and `parseAllDocuments` of `yaml` 2.9.1 answers `BLOCK_AS_IMPLICIT_KEY` (a throwaway script over those ten headers, 2026-10-30). That copy is shared with EsportCards and cannot change (**Code style**), so a header that is not valid YAML is read line by line with its quotes ignored, rather than reported as unparsable: a `#` after a space counts as a comment there even inside quotes, a false positive that shows where a missed comment would hide. A `<!-- -->` in a header is YAML text, not a comment, and only `---` closes a header, not YAML's `...`. A file that opens with a `---` rule, holds a line such as `Note: text` and has a second `---` further down is read as a header, and the same file without a second one is reported as unparsable, the price of telling a header from a rule by its `key:` line (inferred from the code) (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #298, for Gilles to review). A `#!` on the first line of a `.py` or `.ps1` file is a shebang, not a comment. In PowerShell, telling a bare word from an expression takes PowerShell's own parser, so the check takes every `#` outside a string for a comment, but for one escaped with a backtick: `echo a#b` is reported, and so is `$s = "a $("b # c") d"`, where a subexpression holds quotes, both false positives that show where a missed comment would hide (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #22, for Gilles to review).

A `.sh`, `.toml` or `.env*` file goes through a reader of its own, since a string, a here-document or a quoted value can run over many lines and hold a `#` that is no comment (#33); YAML goes through its parser first, which masks a quoted scalar that runs over many lines, and the other files with `#` comments are read line by line, where a quote ends with its line. In shell, a `#` starts a comment at the start of the file or after whitespace or one of `; & | ( ) < >`, and not inside a string (`'…'`, `"…"` or `$'…'`, over many lines) or the body of a here-document; `$(…)` and backtick substitutions are shell again, inside double quotes too, and a `#!` on the first line is a shebang. `$((…))` and `((…))` are skipped as arithmetic, so that their `<<` is a shift and not a here-document (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #33, for Gilles to review). A `${…}` is read as one unit, up to its closing `}`, with the quotes, `$(…)`, backticks and `${…}` it holds, so that the `)` of a `${y#a)}` does not end the `$(…)` around it and the `#` after a blank in `${y:-a # b}` is not a comment, as bash reads them: `y='a)b'; x="$(echo ${y#a)} # hidden`, a line break, `)"; printf '[%s]\n' "$x"` prints `[b]`, and `printf '[%s]\n' ${y:-a # b}`, with `y` unset, prints `[a]`, `[#]` and `[b]` (GNU bash 5.3.15, `bash -n -c` then `bash -c`, 2026-10-30, #302). A `$$` is one token, so that the `{` after it opens no parameter and a `#` after a blank is a comment: `echo $${x # c}` exits 0 and prints the process ID then `{x`, and `x="$(echo $${y # c`, a line break, `)"` leaves `x` ending in `{y` (GNU bash 5.3.15, `bash -n` then `bash`, 2026-10-30, #302); inside double quotes the reader takes it for one token too, with no exactness claimed, since `x="$${y # c"` passes `bash -n` and fails at run time on a bad substitution (the same run). A `'` in it opens a quote even inside double quotes, so that `"${y:-'}"` and a `${y` that never closes, which exit 2 in bash on `unexpected EOF while looking for matching`, make the file unreadable here (the same run). A `case` in a `$(…)` is followed too, so that the `)` which ends one of its patterns, such as `a)`, `a|b)`, `(a)` or `@(a|b))`, does not end the substitution and the comments after it are found: `case` is a keyword when a blank follows it where a command starts, `in` opens it, and `esac` closes it where a command starts, which is at the start of the file or of a substitution, after a newline, `;`, `&`, `|`, `(`, `)`, `{` or `!` followed by a blank, or after the `in` that opens a `case`, `time`, `then`, `do`, `else`, `elif`, `if`, `while` or `until` (`test/check-comments.test.mjs`, #302), so that `echo case a in b)` opens no `case` and a pattern spelled `case` with no blank after it, which runs in bash, is a pattern: `case case in case) echo pattern;; esac` prints `pattern` (the same run; the words that start a command decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #302, for Gilles to review). In TOML, any `#` outside a string is a comment, so `a = 1#c` is reported (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #33, for Gilles to review); a `"""` or `'''` string runs over many lines, and a `"` or `'` one must close on its line, or the file is unreadable. In a `.env*` file, a quote (`"`, `'` or a backtick) opens a value only right after `KEY=`, `export KEY=` or `KEY: `, and closes where the `LINE` expression of dotenv 17.4.2 closes it (`node_modules/.pnpm/dotenv@17.4.2/node_modules/dotenv/lib/main.js:38`; the bundle of `@next/env` 16.4.2, `node_modules/.pnpm/@next+env@16.4.2/node_modules/@next/env/dist/index.js`, holds the same expression, compared by a script on 2026-10-30), so a quoted value may run over many lines and a quote anywhere else is a plain character: the `#` of `KEY=a "b # c"` is reported (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #33, for Gilles to review). There, a `#` starts a comment at the start of a line, after whitespace or right after a closing quote: `KEY=a #b` and `KEY="a"#b` hold one, `KEY=a#b` none. `#!` is a comment in TOML and in `.env*`. A quote that never closes in a `.sh` or `.toml` file, like a here-document or a `$(` or backtick substitution that never closes, makes the file unreadable rather than hiding the rest of it, as for YAML, while in a `.env*` file it is a plain character, as dotenv takes it, and the lines after it are still read (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #33, for Gilles to review).

Three limits are known. The `)` of the first pattern of a `case` that the reader does not take for one ends a `$(…)` inside double quotes early, as it did for every `case` before #302, and the comments after it, up to the closing quote, are missed, where the same without quotes reports them: `x="$(time -p case a in a) echo ;; # hidden\n esac)"` reports nothing, with `\n` for a line break. It happens in three cases: a `case` after a word the reader does not know to leave a command starting, such as the `-p` of `time -p`; a pattern spelled `case` with a blank after it, as in `x="$(case case in\n case ) echo p ;; # hidden\n esac)"`, which the reader takes for the start of a second `case`; and a pattern spelled `esac` in parentheses, `(esac)`, which it takes for the end of the first, so that the `)` of the next pattern ends the substitution (`checkFile`, 2026-10-30; #302). All three are valid in bash: `x="$(time -p case a in a) echo t;; esac)"; printf '[%s]\n' "$x"` prints `[t]`, `x="$(case case in`, a line break, ` case ) echo p ;;`, a line break, ` esac)"; printf '[%s]\n' "$x"` prints `[p]`, and a pattern `(esac)` runs (GNU bash 5.3.15, `bash -n` then `bash`, 2026-10-30, #302). A redirection before `case`, as in `>/dev/null case a in a) echo t;; esac`, is a syntax error in bash, so it is no fourth case (the same run). The reader leaves these three unfixed, since telling a pattern from a command takes a state that follows each `;;`, for forms nothing in the repository meets (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #302, for Gilles to review). Neither `$[…]` nor `.envrc` is read as shell (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #33, for Gilles to review). The old arithmetic `$[…]` opens a here-document with its `<<`, which never closes, so a `.sh` file that holds `x=$[1 << 2]` is reported unreadable, which shows (`checkFile`, 2026-10-30; #302). A `.envrc` stays on the `.env*` reader, since `familyOf` and `hashFileComments` take every name that starts with `.env` (`:84` and `:414` at 80cc714), though direnv loads it into a bash sub-shell (https://direnv.net/man/direnv.1.html, read 2026-10-30): the `#` of a here-document in it is reported, and a comment inside a `KEY="$(…)"` that spans lines is missed (`checkFile`, 2026-10-30). The repository holds no `.envrc`, `.sh` or `.toml` file, and its one `.env.example` has no comment (`git ls-files`, 2026-10-30), so none of this shows today.

A file of any other type is reported as « unknown file type » until the script reads it or lists it as commentless, which it does for images (`.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.gif`, `.webp`, `.avif`, `.ico`, `.bmp`), fonts (`.woff`, `.woff2`, `.ttf`, `.otf`, `.eot`) and `pnpm-lock.yaml`: it does not tell text from binary, so a new type of either fails the check until it is listed (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #22, for Gilles to review). A file it cannot parse is reported too, including a Markdown code fence or YAML header, a Python `'''` or `"""`, a SQL `'` or `"` string, a PowerShell `<#`, `'`, `"` or here-string, a shell or TOML quote, a shell here-document, `$(` or backtick substitution that never closes, so that the rest of the file is never skipped. The script's footer counts the comments, the files it cannot parse and the files of an unknown type each on a line of its own, none for a count of 0, and gives the advice to move the why elsewhere only when it found a comment, and the PR hook's refusal asks to remove each comment, to fix each file that cannot be parsed, and to teach the check each unknown file type or list it as a type without comments, since it quotes all three kinds of line (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #299, for Gilles to review).

## Facts and docs

- **Facts apart from inferences.** Everything you write to docs, tickets or reports keeps verified facts, with their source (`file:line`, commit, URL and date, command output), visibly separate from inferences.
- **Challenge with evidence.** When the code, the docs, `GLOSSARY.md`, an ADR and the user's request disagree, name the contradiction, quote both sides, and ask which one wins. Record the answer through domain-modeling.
- **Agent decisions.** An agent running `deliver-epic` or `deliver-backlog`, as those of #221's run did under its prompt, decides where this file or a skill says to ask Gilles: it applies the most acceptable option and records it under `Décisions de l'agent (à revoir par Gilles)` in the ticket and in the commit message, for him to review; the docs' mark, `(decided by the agent on <date>, on #<n>, for Gilles to review)`, goes in a file of `.claude/rules/`, an ADR or `docs/`, never in this file (`.claude/skills/deliver-epic/rules.md`, #257).
- **Unrecoverable why.** When nothing explains a decision or a fact the work needs, append it to `docs/agents/open-questions.md`: the question, where it shows up, who is likely to know, what is blocked on it.
- **Docs as you go.** Docs are updated the moment they become true, in the same change. This file holds only what every session needs, in its shortest form: a rule that matters only when touching some files goes to `.claude/rules/<area>.md`, scoped by `paths:`, a decision to an ADR, reference to `docs/` behind a pointer line, and a trial or a ticket trail to the commit message and the ticket. `GLOSSARY.md` is a glossary and nothing else; ADRs follow domain-modeling's bar. When docs contradict the code, fix them in the same change if it takes a few lines, otherwise list them in your report.
- **Scope.** Each diff stays inside its ticket. Problems you spot nearby are loose ends: groom-backlog files them as tickets.
- **Gotchas.** When a session hits a trap the environment doesn't reveal, add one line to the rule of the area it concerns, `.claude/rules/<area>.md`, as for a warning silenced until something changes (**Reasons**), and under **Gotchas** only a trap of the machine or the tooling that no path scopes. Delete lines that stop being true.

## Costs

No third party the panel relies on may ever send a bill or take a hidden fee, as Gilles decided on 2026-10-27, calling it a golden rule, as #9 was about to open the Vercel account (#123). It covers every service, account and tool the panel, its CI and its tooling use, today's and any to come. [`docs/costs.md`](docs/costs.md) says, for each, what the panel uses, why it cannot bill, what would make it bill, and what it can cost otherwise, with its sources.

- **No way to pay.** Every account the panel relies on stays on a free plan with no payment method on file, so that a limit reached blocks or pauses the service instead of billing it, as Vercel's Hobby and GitHub Free do (`docs/costs.md`). So nobody adds a payment method, starts a trial, upgrades a plan, or buys a domain, credits or an add-on, nor installs an integration or app that bills, on an account the panel relies on, not even for a free first month: a card added for one purchase may stay on file (inferred from one unofficial report, `docs/costs.md`, **Vercel**).
- **A paid plan** would reopen this rule: should the panel need one, as it would if Shor turned commercial, Gilles decides it in an ADR before anything is signed.
- **A new tool.** Before a new third party comes in, whether a service, an account, an integration, an app, or a package, an action or a registry that reaches a hosted service when it installs or runs, its entry goes into `docs/costs.md`, from its own pricing and terms: whether it asks for a payment method, what happens at each limit the panel could reach, and what would make it bill. One that needs a card before it can be used, or that bills past its free quota even without one, stays out. A package that only runs locally needs no entry, since the npm registry has its own (**Adding a dependency**). Neon, the store ADR 0004 picks after the MVP, gets its entry before its account opens, from the store's grilling (#175).
- **Agents** create no account, type no payment method, accept no trial or upgrade, buy nothing, and install no integration or app that bills, by any means: a terminal, a dashboard, an MCP server. Before Gilles takes such a step, they tell him what it costs, with its source, and they stop at a screen that asks for a card or offers a trial in place of the free plan.
- **Rechecked every month.** Free plans change: the monthly session of **Dependencies** reads again the pages `docs/costs.md` cites, and an entry whose facts moved is updated before anything else, or its service leaves (#97).

## Architecture

How a feature is written end to end, as Gilles decided while grilling #47 on 2026-10-23, then #49, #54, #52 and #50 for the lines that point to **Errors**, **Testing**, **Interface** and **Monitoring**. It was written before the code: each ticket that writes the code follows it.

- **Folders.** `src/app/` only routes: its `page.tsx`, `layout.tsx` and `route.ts` call a domain folder named after a `GLOSSARY.md` term, such as `src/staff/` (login, session, staff role), `src/blocked-words/` or `src/audit-log/`. A domain folder holds its logic, its components, its `actions.ts` and their tests. `src/discord/` is the adapter to Discord's REST API, `src/ui/` holds what the interface shares (**Interface**), and `src/monitoring/` the logs and the alerts (**Monitoring**). The error pages are the one exception (**Errors**).
- **The Discord seam.** `src/discord/` exposes two interfaces, one method per endpoint the panel calls but the alerts' webhook (**Monitoring**), typed with `discord-api-types`. `DiscordApi` uses the panel's application token and is bound at construction to one Discord server, so no method takes its ID: AutoMod rules, audit log, reading a member, listing the members, 1,000 at most a call, from the highest ID of the previous page, which asks for the privileged Server Members Intent (**The application's permissions**, #322) and which the search by name reads too, since the list searches it in memory and `searchMembers` left `DiscordApi` (#324), reading a user, a ban, the bans and the roles (#176), then the Discord server itself, for its owner and its roles, the application's own user, read from `/users/@me` with that token, modifying a member, for a timeout, opening a DM channel and sending a message in it (#179), and removing a member, for a kick (#180), then creating and removing a ban (#181), and listing a channel's messages, 100 at most a call, from the newest, before a given message, which no page of the panel calls since #387 moved the record's ticket archives (#197) to the search, and which the support tickets in progress (#380) are inferred to read for their summary, then listing the Discord server's channels, each with its type, its name, its parent category and its permission overwrites, which `canViewChannel` reads (**Channel visibility**, #384). Discord's change log of 2026-08-12 says that `GET /guilds/{guild.id}/channels` will "omit those channels from the response entirely", those that the application lacks View Channel on, from November 16, 2026, with no early opt-in over HTTP (https://docs.discord.com/developers/change-log, "Channel Obfuscation for Users and Bots", read 2026-11-05), where the same entry says "Today, bots receive every channel in a guild, including ones they can't view", and on 2026-11-05 the route still listed them, with their overwrites: the test Discord server's private channel, which denies View Channel to @everyone and whose messages the application read with a 403, came back from it (a read-only probe of `GET /guilds/{guild.id}/channels` and of that channel's messages, #384). So `canViewChannel` reads a channel that `listChannels` does not give as not viewable, for every staff member (**Channel visibility**). Each returns the fields of `discord-api-types`' object that the panel uses, picked from it, where the method's result is bigger than the panel needs, such as `Guild`, `Message` or `GuildChannel` (#179, #384). `Guild` keeps `id`, `owner_id` and `roles` whole, each role with its `permissions` as the bitfield string Discord sends, which with the roles of `listRoles` is all that `canViewChannel` needs of the Discord server; `GuildChannel` makes `id`, `type`, `name`, `parent_id` and `permission_overwrites` of `APIGuildChannel` required, since Discord sends all five for a channel of the Discord server (the contract suite reads them on the test Discord server, #384). `searchMessages` (#383) reads Discord's search of the Discord server's messages and gives a `MessageSearchResult`, the `total_results` and the messages found, flat, since Discord nests each message in an array that once held its context and now holds the message alone (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/message, **Search Guild Messages**, read 2026-11-05; the seven results of `probe-383-2.mjs` on the test Discord server came in seven arrays of one message each, 2026-11-05). Its query keeps what the pages that follow need: the text, the channels, the authors, a message after and a message before which to read, the size of the page, its offset and the order, the lists sent as Discord asks for an array, one parameter for each value (https://docs.discord.com/developers/reference, **Array Query Strings**, read 2026-11-05). It needs the Message Content intent and Read Message History on the channels it searches (**The application's permissions**), and Discord answers `202`, with no result, while its index is not ready, which the real implementation retries once as it does a 429, then fails as Discord unavailable (**Retries**). What the search reads and serves, as the test Discord server answered, and what it costs, is in ADR 0010 (#383). It also exposes that server's `guildId`, a property rather than a method, from which `memberAvatarUrl`, in `src/discord/member-avatar-url.ts`, builds the URL of a member's avatar (**Avatars**, #140), as `userAvatarUrl` does an account's Discord avatar (#176). `DiscordOAuth` uses the client ID and secret: authorize URL, code exchange, current user. The real implementations call `fetch`, with neither `@discordjs/rest` nor `@discordjs/core`. `src/discord/fake-discord.ts` keeps a Discord server's state in memory and, like Discord, adds an audit log entry on every write. Only tests import it: `pnpm dev` runs against the panel's development application, "Shor Panel Dev", and Gilles' test Discord server (#7). A `no-restricted-imports` rule of `eslint.config.mjs` refuses it and its HTTP adapter (**Testing**) anywhere in `src/` outside `*.test.*` files.
- **Entry points only wire.** Pages, layouts, `actions.ts`, Route Handlers, `proxy.ts` and `instrumentation.ts` hold no logic of their own and never read `process.env`. Those that need Discord get the real clients from `createDiscordApi()` and `createDiscordOAuth()` and pass them on, as in `addBlockedWord(discord, staffMember, word)`; tests pass the fake instead, with no `vi.mock`. The login callback's Route Handler and `proxy.ts` pass `createMonitor()`'s `Monitor` the same way (**Monitoring**). The callback's Route Handler passes `createDiscordApi` and `createDiscordOAuth` themselves, which its domain function calls inside its `try`, so that a missing variable is a panel error it reports too, since the callback throws nothing (**Error pages**; Gilles, 2026-10-26, on #10). Only `src/discord/` reads Discord's variables, `src/staff/` the staff's, and `src/monitoring/` its own (**Security**, **Variables**). Vitest cannot render `async` Server Components (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/testing/vitest.md:9`), so what a page does lives in functions tested directly.
- **The staff check.** Every page but the login pages under `/login`, the privacy policy at `/privacy` (#186) and the error pages, and every Server Action that writes, starts with `requireStaffMember` from `src/staff/`, and every domain write takes the branded `StaffMember` it returns (ADR 0005). The pages under `/records` start with `requireModerationAccess` instead, which runs `requireStaffMember`, then calls `notFound()` for a staff member who holds no role of `MODERATION_ROLE_IDS`, so that they see the not-found page rather than `/login/refused` (ADR 0005, #176), and so does the Server Action that changes a sanction, whose domain functions also throw for a `StaffMember` who does not moderate (#179, #180), or, for a ban or its lifting, who does not ban: `StaffMember`'s `opensBans` says whether they also hold a role of `BAN_ROLE_IDS` (#181). Like the staff check's redirects, it takes effect in the part of the page under `<Suspense>`, so the page's static shell shows first, and the response keeps its `200` (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/04-functions/not-found.md:193`). The page of `/records` starts it in its body, through `moderatedDiscord`, after `connection()`, as the page of `/blocked-words` starts `requireStaffMember`, and `notFound()` is thrown where the promise is awaited, in the components of the form and of the list, each under its own `<Suspense>` (#324). The shell's static `metadata` stays too, so that the tab of that staff member reads « Modération – Panel de Shor » on `/records` and « Fiche – Panel de Shor » on a record, not « Page introuvable – Panel de Shor », and names the section, though the comment of #61 of 2026-10-28 asks that the page read « comme si la section n'existait pas » (`e2e/records.test.ts`, 2026-10-30, #61). This is intended (Gilles, 2026-10-30, on #269): until Discord answers, the shell's body names the section too, through the `h1` and the sentence of `/records`, whose form shows only once the staff check and the roles' read are done (#324), and the breadcrumb of a record, though no data from Discord shows (a throwaway Playwright probe over `holdDiscord`, 2026-10-30), and the one reason ADR 0005 gives for the not-found page is that `/login/refused` would tell a staff member they are not staff. A `generateMetadata` that awaited `connection()`, then ran `requireModerationAccess`, gave that staff member the not-found page's tab, and kept the static shell and the moderators' tabs, but left every staff member without a `<title>` until Discord answered, which `checkAccessibility` refused on a record's skeletons, and `error.tsx` without one once Discord failed (the same probe). The not-found page is the panel's own, in French (**Error pages**, #61). `/game-log` starts with `requireStaffMember` too, then `notFound()`s a staff member who does not view its channel on Discord, as `canViewChannel` works it out (**Channel visibility**; `.claude/rules/game-log.md`, #394). `requireStaffMember` never returns a failure: it redirects to `/login` without a readable session, and to `/login/refused` a member who is not on the Discord server or lacks the staff role. The login callback, which has no session yet, runs the same check by Discord user ID and redirects itself (ADR 0005). It uses `redirect` rather than `forbidden()`, which needs `experimental.authInterrupts` (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/04-functions/forbidden.md:17`) and gives a Route Handler an empty body (`node_modules/next/dist/server/route-modules/app-route/module.js:259-263`).
- **Channel visibility.** `canViewChannel(discord, staffMember, channelId)`, in `src/staff/can-view-channel.ts`, says whether a staff member views a channel of the Discord server, as Discord does, from the roles the staff check read in the `StaffMember`, the Discord server's owner and roles, and the channel's permission overwrites, in the order of the pseudocode of https://docs.discord.com/developers/topics/permissions (read 2026-11-05, #384): the owner views every channel; a staff member's base permissions are @everyone's role with the permissions of each role they hold, and with Administrator among them they view every channel; then the channel's overwrite for @everyone applies, its denies removed and its allows added, then the allows and denies of the staff member's roles, taken together, denies removed first, then the staff member's own overwrite, the same way, and the channel is viewed when View Channel is left. A timed-out staff member is computed like any other, since a timeout leaves View Channel (the same page, "Permissions For Timed Out Members": "Timed out members will temporarily lose all permissions except VIEW_CHANNEL and READ_MESSAGE_HISTORY"). An overwrite is matched by its ID alone, as the pseudocode does. The function reads a channel's own overwrites and never its category's, because Discord does: "Rather than inheritance, permissions are calculated by means of what we call Permission Syncing" (the same page), a child channel is synced to its category when it holds "the same permissions and overwrites", and changing the child de-syncs it. A deny on a category reaches a channel only as the overwrites the channel holds itself, copied from it while it was synced, and a channel de-synced from a category that denies is viewed or hidden by its own overwrites, which `src/staff/can-view-channel.test.ts` proves in both directions, so that the panel never shows a staff member more or less than Discord. #384 and the epic #380 word it as « surcharges de la catégorie et du salon », which assumes an inheritance that Discord's page does not describe (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #384, for Gilles to review). A category is a channel like any other: asked for its own ID, the function computes from its own overwrites. It reads the Discord server and the channels once each, through `getGuild` and `listChannels`; `viewsChannel(guild, channel, staffMember)` is the same computation over what a caller has already read, which `canViewChannel` calls, so that a page that checks a list of channels reads both once and asks `viewsChannel` for each. A channel that `listChannels` does not give is not viewed, whoever asks, since Discord lists only the channels the application can view from 2026-11-16 (**The Discord seam**), so that a channel the application cannot view reads as hidden from the staff members Discord shows it to: the application needs View Channel on each channel a page reads, and a grant on its category reaches the channel only while the channel is synced to the category (**The application's permissions**; inferred from the page's Permission Syncing, not tried).
- **Writes** are Server Actions, in their domain folder's `actions.ts`, marked `"use server"`, and end with `refresh()` once the write lands. Logging out is a Server Action too, which only ends the session, then sends the member to `/login/logged-out`, a static page that says so, with « Se connecter avec Discord » (Gilles, 2026-10-26, on #10; #236). Route Handlers serve OAuth2 only: `/login` and `/login/callback`.
- **Validation** uses zod 4 throughout the repo, config included. Domain functions validate what comes from the browser, such as an action's `FormData` or the callback's query string. An invalid URL parameter counts as absent. Discord's responses are typed, not validated.
- **Components** are Server Components. `"use client"` goes only on a leaf that needs browser state or events, in its domain folder, on some components shadcn/ui's CLI copies (**Interface**), and on the two error pages Next.js requires it of (**Errors**); never on a page, a layout or anything that reaches `src/discord/`. A client leaf may hold browser state, such as `useActionState`'s, but no logic: it hands that state to a component whose output follows from its props alone (**Testing**). There are two exceptions. One is the toast of a write that lands, which the leaf that holds `useActionState` adds, through `useSuccessToast` (**Forms**; Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #227). The other is the theme a staff member chooses: `StatefulThemeToggle`, in `src/staff/`, reads it from `data-theme` through `useSyncExternalStore`, whose server snapshot is « Auto », and on a click writes it to `data-theme` and `localStorage`, or removes both for « Auto », then hands the theme and that write to `ThemeToggle`, as #225 decided (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #225) (**Theme**).
- **Caching.** Nothing from Discord and nothing from the session goes through `use cache`, since the panel reads Discord back on every view (ADR 0002). Only the static shell prerenders; the part of a page that waits on Discord sits under `<Suspense>`, with a French fallback. That part, and a Route Handler, await `connection()` before they create a client: the build prerenders a page, and a `GET` Route Handler, until it reads the request (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/01-getting-started/15-route-handlers.md:89`, `:124`), and a client created there parses variables that a build outside Vercel lacks, which failed `pnpm build` on `/` and `/login/callback` (2026-10-26). It also keeps `/login` from prerendering one `state` for every visitor. `/records/[userId]` exports `instant = false`: its `userId` is unknown at build, so the navigation's `usePathname` suspends outside `<Suspense>` there (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/04-functions/use-pathname.md:75`), which failed `pnpm build`; the page then renders at each request, where nothing outside `<Suspense>` waits on Discord (2026-10-28, #176).
- **`proxy.ts`** sends a request without a readable session to `/login`, on every route but `/login`, everything under it, `/privacy`, which anyone reads and which reads nothing from Discord (#186), and static files: the callback opens no session for a member without the staff role, who would otherwise loop from `/login/refused` back through Discord. It authorizes nothing.
- **Imports** that leave their folder use `@/*`, mapped to `src/*`; imports within a folder stay relative, except in `src/ui/` (**Interface**). Vitest resolves the alias only with `resolve.tsconfigPaths: true`, which Vite 8.3.5 leaves off by default.

## Errors

How the panel fails, as Gilles decided while grilling #49 on 2026-10-23, then #53 for what a successful write returns. Like **Architecture**, it was written before the code.

- **Four kinds of failure.** _Refused input_: the staff entered something the panel refuses, or the Discord server is in a state they can fix in Discord, such as already holding 6 keyword rules. _Refused access_: no readable session, or not staff (**The staff check**). _Discord unavailable_: a 429 the panel does not retry or that comes back, a 5xx, a network error or a timeout, and a 202 from the search of the Discord server's messages that comes back or whose delay is over 2 s, because Discord's index is not ready (**Retries**, #383). _Panel error_: everything else, a misconfiguration included, such as a revoked token, a missing permission or the Server Members Intent turned off, which Discord answers on the list of members with a 403 of code 50001 (#322); the staff can only report it.
- **`DiscordError`** is the one class every method of `DiscordApi` and `DiscordOAuth` throws, network errors and timeouts included. It carries the HTTP status, Discord's JSON `code` and `message`, the delay of a 429, the method and the route's pattern, as Discord's docs write it, such as `/guilds/{guild.id}/members/{user.id}`, never the path filled in, since Next.js prints its properties in the logs (**Monitoring**), and says whether Discord is unavailable. One absence is an answer rather than an error: `getMember` returns `null` on Unknown Member, code 10007 (https://docs.discord.com/developers/topics/opcodes-and-status-codes, read 2026-10-23).
- **Retries.** Through `DiscordApi` and `DiscordOAuth`, a 429 is retried once, after its `retry_after`, when that is 2 s or less. The search of the Discord server's messages (#383) is retried the same way when Discord answers it `202`, "Index not yet available. Try again later", code 110000, with a `retry_after`, after that delay or after 0.25 s when it is shorter, since the docs ask for "a short delay" at 0 (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/message, **Search Guild Messages**, read 2026-11-05); a `202` that comes back, or whose delay is over 2 s, is a `DiscordError` with the status 202, which counts as Discord unavailable. No `202` was seen on the test Discord server, whose index was ready, in the ten probes and the three full runs of the contract suite of 2026-11-05, so this follows the docs alone, and the 0.25 s is decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #383, for Gilles to review. The search's 429s carried a `retry_after` of up to 4.4 s there (`probe-383-5.mjs`, 2026-11-05), over the 2 s the client waits (ADR 0010), so two searches within seconds of each other can fail the second as Discord unavailable. A record makes from 1 to 4 of them (**Navigation**, #387). Nothing else is retried, since a create that got a 5xx may have landed. Every call they make gives up after 10 s, through `AbortSignal.timeout`. An alert follows its own rules (**Monitoring**). The panel tracks no rate-limit bucket: it only reacts to a 429.
- **Write results.** A domain write checks every limit Discord documents before calling it, so a 400 from Discord is a panel error. It re-reads what it changes right before writing and applies only its own change, with no conflict error: a form sends one word, never a list. It returns `{ ok: true, message }`, or `{ ok: false, message }` with each field the form sent, as typed and under its name, such as `word`: one French `message` per form, written by the domain, that says what landed, such as « « foo » est maintenant un mot interdit. », or what went wrong, for refused input and for Discord unavailable, and the fields so the form keeps them (**Interface**). A blocked word's write names its action, in its message and in its audit log reason, `ajout de « foo », action « Exclusion 1 heure »`, and hands back, when it fails, the `word` and the `action` it got, as sent, `""` for a removal, which sends none (#325). Its right follows the action: `addBlockedWord` throws for a `StaffMember` whom the action's right refuses, before it reads or writes anything, as a sanction does, and `removeBlockedWord` reads the right off the rules that hold the word, read back from Discord, the strongest action among them when a retouch in Discord has put the word in several, from which it removes the word with one write to each, each reason naming that rule's own action, and whose action its message names, the tile's, while a word that no rule holds any more is removed with no write and no right, as before (#325 sets the right of the strongest action and the removal from every rule that holds the word; the one write to each rule, each reason naming its own rule's action, and the message naming the action are decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #325, for Gilles to review). Two words that differ by their case are two words to read and to remove, as in one rule before, and the same word to add, since only a retouch in Discord can put both in the rules (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #325, for Gilles to review). `src/blocked-words/blocked-word-action.ts` lists the actions from the weakest to the strongest and holds each one's label, the name of its rule, its right, its timeout and its notice, which #328's kick and ban add an entry to, while `mayUse` says whether a staff member holds an action's right, `mayChange` whether a staff member may give a word that has an action another one, which takes a right on a second action too (#326), and `strongestHeld`, in `panel-rule.ts`, picks the strongest action among the rules that hold a word, which reading, refusing a duplicate and removing share; a rule that sanctions exempts `StaffMember.staffRoleId`, which `findStaffMember` fills from the `StaffRoles` it already receives, so that `src/blocked-words/` reads no environment and only `src/staff/` reads the staff's variables (#325). A blocked word's change of action (#326) is `changeActionOfBlockedWord`, which `changeBlockedWord` runs for a form whose hidden `change` is `action` and, for any other, hands to the removal, as `changeSanction` routes the sanctions. It takes the `word`, as the list holds it, spaces included, and the new `action`. It throws for a `StaffMember` whom the new action's right refuses, before it reads anything, and for one whom the right of the word's current action refuses, once it has read the rules back from Discord and before it writes, the current action being the strongest among the rules that hold the word. It refuses with `{ ok: false }`, the `word` and the `action` as sent, and writes nothing, for a word that no rule of the panel holds any more, `« foo » n’est plus un mot interdit.`, and for a new action whose rule is full or that the Discord server has no room to create, with #325's messages. It writes in this order: the word enters the rule of the new action, which the panel creates with that word, the staff role's exemption and the notice if the rule does not exist, then it leaves every other rule of the panel that holds it, the old one and any copy that an interrupted change left, with one write each, so that it is blocked at every moment. Every write carries the reason `« foo » passe de « Bloquer seulement » à « Exclusion 1 jour »`: the entering write names the strongest action among the rules the word leaves, and each leaving write its own rule's, so that a word held by one rule gets the same reason twice (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #326, for Gilles to review, where #326 gives the one reason). Its message says what landed, `« foo » passe à « Exclusion 1 jour ».`. Discord unavailable before the first write, as the panel reads the rules, returns `{ ok: false }` saying that the action has not changed, and from the first write on, whichever write fails, one saying that the word may be in two rules, the old action's and the new one's, that it stays blocked, and to change its action again or remove it, which the next change of its action or its removal does: the word then reads with the strongest action of the rules that hold it (**Interface**, #325). `src/blocked-words/change-action-of-blocked-word.test.ts` proves the order by a fake that fails the second write alone, derived in the test's file as the filtered audit log's is (#231), and reads the rules back through `DiscordApi`. Choosing the action a word has, when no other rule holds it, succeeds with `« foo » garde l’action « Exclusion 1 jour ».` and writes nothing; when a weaker rule still holds a copy of it, it removes that copy alone, in one write, and says the same, so that a change run again after an interrupted one cleans up, to the action the word shows as to any other (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #326, for Gilles to review, where #326 says that choosing the current action writes nothing). A word moved to a rule that holds a word differing from it only by its case lands beside it, since the comparison stays exact, as #325 decided for reading and removing; only a retouch in Discord can put the two in the rules, and a later change or removal takes each as the word it is (`src/blocked-words/change-action-of-blocked-word.test.ts`; a known limit, decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #326, for Gilles to review). The action hands that to the form as is, whose leaf then shows the message of `{ ok: true }` in a toast (**Forms**, #227). A timeout that lands returns `{ ok: true }` whatever its direct message then meets, since the sanction holds: its message says that the member did not receive the direct message when Discord refuses it, with 50007, 50278 or 40003, or may not have when Discord is unavailable, and a direct message that fails otherwise is thrown, as a bug of the panel, so that it alerts, though the timeout has landed (Gilles, 2026-10-28, on #179, whose « S'il échoue, […] le résultat dit que le membre ne l'a pas reçu » this narrows to Discord's refusals and to Discord unavailable). Discord unavailable while a timeout or its lifting is written returns `{ ok: false }`, saying that it may or may not have landed, while Discord unavailable before the write, as the panel reads the member and the role hierarchy, says it did not happen, since a read that fails changes nothing on the Discord server (#213). A kick sends its direct message first, since a kicked account no longer shares a Discord server with the application (#180): the kick then happens whatever the message met, and the result says what the message met, as for a timeout; Discord unavailable during the kick returns `{ ok: false }`, saying that the kick may or may not have landed, and that the message went, if it did, with a reminder to untick the direct message before retrying, since a retry would send it again, while Discord unavailable before the kick says it did not happen; and a panel error is thrown once the kick was tried, during the kick though the message went, or during the message though the kick landed (Gilles, 2026-10-28, on #180, whose « Si l'expulsion échoue ensuite, le résultat dit que le message est parti mais pas la sanction » this narrows to Discord unavailable, as for #179; that a message's panel error waits for the kick is inferred from #180's « Si le message échoue, l'expulsion a lieu quand même »). A ban follows the kick's rules, through the same `sanctionAfterDirectMessage` of `src/sanctions/`, its direct message sent first to a member, and none to an account absent from the Discord server, which shares none with the application; lifting a ban sends no message, since the account shares no Discord server with the application either, and Discord unavailable there says that the lift did not happen when it comes before the write and that it may or may not have landed during it, as for a timeout's (#181, #213). Anything else is thrown, a `DiscordError` from the action's `requireStaffMember` included. Reads never write: what a page needs created, the first write creates.
- **Read failures.** What a page reads from Discord is thrown on failure, with no catch on the way, and `error.tsx` replaces the page, and the layout of `(staff)` with it, since `error.tsx` sits at the root of `src/app/`, above that layout, so that no navigation shows (**Error pages**, #53). In production it gets a generic message and the error's `digest`, never the original message (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/error.md:106`, `:111-115`), so it shows one message for Discord unavailable and panel error alike, a « Réessayer » button that calls `retry`, and the `digest` as a reference. An error without a `digest`, as one a Client Component throws in the browser (`error.md:104`), shows no reference, and the message then ends on the channel (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #61, for Gilles to review). Three reads are exempt. One is a lookup of a member's avatar in the audit log: one that throws a `DiscordError` shows the member's initials instead, as for a member no longer on the Discord server, since an avatar is decorative, while any other error it throws is thrown (**Avatars**, #129, #141). So a panel error there alerts no one, against **Alerts**: the page's staff check reads its own member first, through the same `getMember` and the same token (`src/app/(staff)/audit-log/page.tsx:48`, `:52`), so a revoked token or a lost permission fails there and alerts, and only a failure tied to one member's ID, such as an ID that names no account, goes unreported (inferred; Gilles, 2026-10-28, on #141). Another is the count of blocked words beside « Mots interdits » in the navigation (**Navigation**): `readBlockedWordCount`, in `src/blocked-words/`, gives none when reading the AutoMod rules throws a `DiscordError`, and the navigation then shows no badge, while any other error it throws is thrown, so that Discord unavailable never replaces a page with `error.tsx` for a number (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #226). So a panel error there alerts no one either: the count waits for the layout's staff check, which reads the member through the same token first (`src/blocked-words/read-blocked-word-count.ts:12-16`), so a revoked token fails there and alerts, and a permission lost on AutoMod, which only the rules' read needs, goes unreported until a staff member opens `/blocked-words`, whose own read of the rules then fails and alerts (inferred, #226). The third is the count of each request kind in the pills of `/support-tickets` (**The filters**, #386): `readKindCounts`, in `src/ticket-archives/`, gives none when one of its searches throws a `DiscordError` that says Discord is unavailable, a 429 over the 2 s the client waits included, and the pills then show without their numbers while the list stays, whereas any other error it throws is thrown, a `DiscordError` that is a panel error included, so that it alerts, unlike the two above. The list of accounts of `/records` is a read like any other, with no exemption: a failure of its read of the members, a 403 of code 50001 for want of the Server Members Intent included, is thrown, `error.tsx` replaces the whole page, the search field with it, and a panel error alerts (#322, #317), as it does when the read of the roles, which the form and the list share, fails (#324). The ticket archives of a record are a read like any other too: a failure of the search of the archives channel, on any of its pages, or of a closer's name, a `DiscordError` included, is thrown and `error.tsx` replaces the whole record, its sanctions with it, so that a permission the application lacks on the archives channel, a 403 of code 50001, fails every record and alerts until it is granted (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #197, for Gilles to review, the ticket saying nothing of a failed read). A 429 of the search over the 2 s the client waits for, which is Discord unavailable (**Retries**), does the same with no alert, and « Réessayer » is inferred to work a few seconds later, once the search's rate has passed (ADR 0010; not tried on a record): a record whose search is rate limited shows no card, where a card that said so was set aside, since #387 asks that the card not change (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #387, for Gilles to review). An archives channel left out of the configuration is no failure: the card says that it is not configured (**Interface**). The page `/support-tickets` is a read like any other too: a failure of its search or of a name, a `DiscordError` included, is thrown and `error.tsx` replaces the page, and a 429 of the search that outlasts the 2 s the client waits is such a failure, which the staff retry with « Réessayer » (**Interface**, **The support tickets' page**, #385). The tickets in progress of the same page are a read like any other too, but for one exemption: a ticket whose channel is gone when its messages are read, because it closed in the seconds between the two reads (Unknown Channel, code 10003), is left out, and any other failure, a 403 of code 50001 for want of View Channel on one ticket's channel included, is thrown and `error.tsx` replaces the whole page, the archives with it (#388; the exemption decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #388, for Gilles to review). The game log is a read like any other, but for its link in the navigation, which gives no link when the read of the channels throws a `DiscordError` (`.claude/rules/game-log.md`, #394).
- **Error pages.** `src/app/` holds `not-found.tsx`, `error.tsx` and `global-error.tsx` with their own markup, since they belong to no domain, and beside them `error-notice.tsx`, the frame the first two share. Next.js requires `error.tsx` and `global-error.tsx` to be Client Components, and `global-error.tsx` to bring its own `<html>` and `<body>` (`error.md:21`, `:163`), here with `lang="fr"`. `error.tsx` and `global-error.tsx` show no navigation, nor does `not-found.tsx` for a URL that no route matches, which renders under the root layout alone; when a page of `(staff)` calls `notFound()`, as a section does for a staff member who does not hold its role (ADR 0005), the not-found page renders inside `(staff)/layout.tsx`, beside the navigation, which then lacks that section (`e2e/records.test.ts`, 2026-10-30, #61; #61's earlier inference was that it replaced the navigation too). `ErrorNotice` frames `not-found.tsx` and `error.tsx`, with a `PageHeader` with no overline, the rule with a diamond and the page's one button, in a `<main>` of at most 672 px that takes `px-4 py-12 sm:px-12` and no height of its own, since it sits either alone or in that column, whose margin it then adds to (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #61, for Gilles to review). Under `sm` the button fills the width, because the column that holds it, `flex-col sm:flex-row sm:flex-wrap`, stretches it, and the reference of `error.tsx`'s page takes `wrap-anywhere` (#362). `not-found.tsx` says « Page introuvable », as its `h1` and in its tab through `metadata`, « Page introuvable – Panel de Shor » (#53), then « Cette page n’existe pas. », and links to `/` through « Revenir à l’accueil », a plain `<a>` since `/` redirects (**Gotchas**); it says nothing more to a staff member who does not moderate and opens `/records`, as if the section did not exist (the comment of #61 of 2026-10-28). `error.tsx` says « Page indisponible », then the message of #49 that **Read failures** describes, as #53 worded it: « Le panel n’a pas pu afficher cette page. Discord ne répond peut-être pas : réessaie dans un instant. Si ça continue, signale-le dans #créer-ticket avec la référence <digest>. ». « #créer-ticket » is the channel of Shor's Discord server where the staff report a problem of the panel, named by one constant, `REPORT_CHANNEL`, in `ReportChannelLink`, in `src/ui/report-channel-link.tsx` since `/login/failed` names it too (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #61; `/login/failed` was out of #61's scope, as #10's, but its text waited on the same answer in `docs/agents/open-questions.md`, so it takes it here, as decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #61, for Gilles to review). It links to `https://discord.com/channels/1545540661774913626/1550835033311412224`, its server's ID then its own, in a new tab, so that the error's reference stays on screen while the staff report it, and « Page indisponible » is the `h1` that #61 leaves unnamed (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #61, for Gilles to review). `global-error.tsx` renders `error.tsx`'s page in its own document, which imports `@/ui/globals.css`, applies the stored theme in its `<head>` as the root layout does and takes `suppressHydrationWarning` on `<html>`, so that it follows a theme a staff member chose and not the system's alone when the server renders it (**Theme**, #225; inferred, since a root layout that throws on the server was not provoked), and, since it can export no `metadata` (`error.md:167`), sets its tab, « Page indisponible – Panel de Shor », through React's `<title>`, and the favicon through a `<link rel="icon">` to `/icon.svg`, the route that the build lists (`pnpm build`, 2026-10-30). When a client component of the root layout throws in the browser instead, React renders `global-error.tsx` over the same document, in French, in light and in dark, with its tab, and removes the `data-theme` that the layout's script had set, so that the page follows the system's scheme: a throwaway client component of the root layout that threw on `?boom`, under `next start`, with « Sombre » stored under a light system, showed `data-theme` set to `dark`, then removed, and the body's background light (a `MutationObserver` on `data-theme` and the body's computed background, 2026-10-30, #61), since React's commit of `<html>` removes every attribute it holds before it sets its own (`node_modules/next/dist/compiled/react-dom/cjs/react-dom-client.production.js:9366`). The system's scheme stands for that case (Gilles, 2026-10-30, on #270). The root layout holds no client component and `error.tsx` wraps everything below it (`error.md:96`), so nothing reaches that case today (inferred from the code). In #270's probe, a `useLayoutEffect` in `global-error.tsx` that copied the stored theme kept `dark`, but Vitest runs no effect (**No DOM**), and end to end could provoke the error only through a way to throw added to the code (inferred). This is revisited with the first client component of the root layout. None of the three checks the staff role, since they show nothing from Discord (ADR 0005). There is no `global-not-found`, which is experimental and serves only several root layouts (#49). The login callback throws nothing: a member who is not staff goes to `/login/refused`, any other failure to `/login/failed`.
- **Messages** are the panel's own French sentences, never Discord's message, a stack or a token. A failure's message says what to do next: correct the input, retry, or report the problem in « #créer-ticket », the channel of Shor's Discord server where the staff report a problem of the panel, which `error.tsx` and `/login/failed` name and link (**Error pages**; Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #61). A success's message says what landed (**Write results**).

## Testing

What the panel's tests prove and where, as Gilles decided while grilling #54 on 2026-10-23, then #53 for the theme and accessibility, and #50 for monitoring. Like **Architecture**, it was written before the code.

- **Layers.** Domain functions carry most of the tests, in Vitest against the fake Discord. The real implementations of `src/discord/` are tested on their own (**The real implementations**). Entry points only wire (**Architecture**): Playwright covers the flows where a wiring mistake shows nowhere else (**End to end**), and nothing but the typecheck and the build checks the rest. No coverage tool, no threshold, no snapshot: a test names the text or the attribute it proves, never a class, but for `sr-only`, the one class that says a text is for assistive technologies alone, as `StaffHeader`'s test does for the menu button's « Ouvrir le menu » (Gilles, 2026-10-27, on #137).
- **The fake.** `fake-discord.ts` implements `DiscordApi` and `DiscordOAuth`. Its constructor takes a Discord server's starting state: members, with their roles, their avatar on the server, their arrival, which may be none as Discord's `joined_at` may be (`node_modules/discord-api-types/payloads/v10/guild.d.ts:708`, #324), and the end of their timeout; accounts that are not on it; bans, with their reason, to which the state gives no audit log entry, so that a record reads such a ban as given in Discord unless the state poses its entry too (#285); roles, beside the `@everyone` it always holds, with no permission unless a role is given for the Discord server's own ID, which then is that `@everyone` (#384); AutoMod rules; audit log entries (#176); the Discord server's owner, the roles of the application, which is always a member, and the members who refuse direct messages (#179). Its list of members gives the application and the members of its state, by ascending ID, after an ID if one is given, and one member unless asked for more, as Discord's List Guild Members does: its `limit` goes from 1 to 1,000, 1 by default, and its `after` is « the highest user id in the previous page » (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/guild, read 2026-11-02; #322). It refuses no `limit`, since the panel always asks for 1,000 (inferred, not tried against Discord). A test that stands for the intent turned off makes `listMembers` fail once with the 403 of code 50001 that Discord answered « Shor Panel Dev » before #318 (#317, 2026-10-30). What happens in Discord outside the panel has methods of its own, named as in Discord, such as a member losing a role, or a member creating or editing an AutoMod rule by hand: the audit log records that rule change under the member's name, with no reason from the panel. So does a member who times out a member, lifts their timeout or changes their nickname, kicks one, or bans an account or lifts its ban, by hand, as `MEMBER_UPDATE`, `MEMBER_KICK`, `MEMBER_BAN_ADD` or `MEMBER_BAN_REMOVE`, the events Discord's docs name for them, whose `user_id` is the "User or app that made the changes" (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/audit-log, read 2026-10-28), with the reason the member typed, if any (inferred for a change by hand), which stays that member's change even when it follows the panel's format, since the entry's `user_id` is the member's and not the application's (#210); a kick or a ban's lifting leaves an account that is not on the Discord server (#177). AutoMod times out a member through a rule whose action is a timeout, for that action's duration, as `AUTO_MODERATION_USER_COMMUNICATION_DISABLED`, whose optional info holds the rule's name and trigger type (the same page). That page says neither what such an entry's `user_id` holds nor whether AutoMod's timeout also adds a `MEMBER_UPDATE`, which a trial on the test Discord server answered: a keyword rule that timed out an account for 60 s left one such entry, whose `target_id` and `user_id` are both the member, the latter of which the panel does not read, whose `options` also hold `channel_id`, with no `changes`, so no end, and with a `reason` of Discord's own, "Message contained keyword defined in AutoMod rule", and no `MEMBER_UPDATE` (`GET /guilds/{guild.id}/audit-logs`, 2026-10-30, #209), which would have had the history and the audit log each show the timeout twice (inferred). #182 left AutoMod's actions out of the audit log, since « le journal d'audit dit ce qu'a fait le staff », and #327 lifted that: the audit log shows this entry as an « Exclusion temporaire automatique », under the author « AutoMod », which the panel writes itself, never the member that the entry's `user_id` names, who is the member timed out (**Navigation**, **Avatars**). `src/sanctions/sanction-events.ts` holds what `readAuditLog` and `readSanctionHistory` share of it: `AUTOMATIC_TIMEOUT_EVENT`, whose action type both read, while the history alone takes its `changeOf`, `{ kind: "timeout", end: null }`, since the audit log builds its own change from the entry; `automaticTimeoutRuleNameOf`, so that the rule's name is read in one place; and `AUTO_MODERATION_NAME`, the label « AutoMod » that the audit log gives as the author and the history writes in « AutoMod, règle « <nom> » » (#327). The fake writes that entry and no `MEMBER_UPDATE`, for any rule, though only a keyword rule was tried, without `channel_id`, since no message is sent in a channel of the fake for a rule to act on. `readSanctionHistory` leaves that reason out, which keeps Discord's English off a French interface, inferred from Gilles' answer that the rule stands in for a reason on that line, given before anyone had seen the entry hold one (on #177, 2026-10-28). Its audit log's `users` hold every account an entry's `user_id` or `target_id` names, a former member included, as Discord's held the member a timeout targets (`pnpm test:contract`, 2026-10-29; inferred for a kick, a ban and a former member, #182). Another method issues the OAuth2 code a test logs in with, for the code challenge of that login, and the exchange refuses the code with another code verifier, or none, as Discord did (**OAuth2**). The fake refuses what Discord refuses among the limits the MVP touches: 6 keyword rules per Discord server and, per rule, 1,000 keywords of up to 60 characters (ADR 0002). It throws the `DiscordError` of a 400 with code 50035, so a limit the domain forgets to check fails a test. It refuses the same way, when a rule is created and when it is modified, an action that times out for more than 2,419,200 seconds, more than 20 exempt roles and a `custom_message` of more than 300 characters, with that 400, so that a notice of the panel that is too long fails a test (#325). Discord documents "150 characters" for the custom message and 2,419,200 seconds for the timeout (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/auto-moderation, read 2026-11-02), and the test Discord server measured otherwise for the first: a message of 150 characters, of 151 and of 300 was accepted, in a modify, and one of 301 was refused, in a modify and in a create, both with a 400 of code 50035, while 2,419,200 seconds were accepted and 2,419,201 refused, in a modify (`pnpm test:contract` and a throwaway probe of a rule's `custom_message`, 2026-11-02, #325). So the fake follows the 300 that Discord enforced and not the 150 that it documents, though #325 asked for 150, since a fake stricter than Discord would fail a test on a notice that Discord takes, while the panel's own, 120 and 118 characters, depends on neither (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #325, for Gilles to review). The 20 exempt roles stay the documentation's, which the test Discord server cannot try, since it probably holds fewer than 21 roles (inferred). A timeout it refuses as Discord does (#179): the owner, as Discord did, and a member with the Administrator permission, against whom Modify Guild Member "Will throw a 403 error" (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/guild, modified 2026-10-27), or whose highest role equals or exceeds the application's, since a bot acts only on "users whose highest role is lower than the bot's highest role" (https://docs.discord.com/developers/topics/permissions, modified 2026-08-13), each with a 403 of code 50013, whose code those two cases are inferred to share with the owner's, and an end more than 28 days ahead with that 400, as Discord did (`pnpm test:contract`, 2026-10-28). It records a timeout or its lifting in its audit log as an entry `MEMBER_UPDATE`, whose change `communication_disabled_until` holds the old end and the new one, the lifting no new one, as Discord did (the same run). A direct message it refuses with a 403 of code 50007, "Cannot send messages to this user", to a member who refuses them, and of code 50278, "Cannot send messages to this user due to having no mutual guilds", to an account that is not a member (https://docs.discord.com/developers/topics/opcodes-and-status-codes, modified 2026-10-27; the status 403 is inferred), and one over 2,000 characters, the limit Discord documents for a webhook's `content` (**What an alert says**), with that 400 (inferred for a direct message); `directMessagesTo(userId)` reads what an account received, which no method of `DiscordApi` reads.
