Its constructor takes the channels of the Discord server too, `channels` and `channelMessages`: a message posed in a channel, by the application unless another author is named, with its content and its embeds, gets the next ID, minted from the time of its `sentAt` when a test gives one, so that a search bounded by two IDs keeps the messages of a period (#386) and the tickets in progress state their dates (#388), and the channel lists its messages newest first, 50 a call by default, before a given message, as Discord's Get Channel Messages does (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/message, read 2026-11-04), and fails as Unknown Channel, a 404 of code 10003, when the fake was not given it, as Discord answered a channel that does not exist (`pnpm test:contract`, 2026-11-05, #197). A channel is given by its ID alone, a text channel with no parent and no overwrite, or in full, with its type, its name, its parent category and its permission overwrites, and `listChannels` gives all of them, in the order they were given, categories included (#384): it leaves none out, as Discord does until 2026-11-16 and not after (**The Discord seam**), so a test that stands for a channel the application cannot view leaves it out of `channels`. `createMessage` posts into a channel it was given as it does into a DM channel, and keeps the embeds it is sent. It refuses no `limit` over 100, since the panel always asks for 100 (inferred, not tried against Discord). `searchMessages` (#383) searches the channels it was given, never a direct message channel (inferred from the docs' name for the endpoint, a search of the guild), or the ones named: an ID it was not given is left out when another is named, and refused alone with a 403 of code 50001, Missing Access, as Discord answered a channel that does not exist (`pnpm test:contract`, 42 tests of 42, 2026-11-05) and a channel the application cannot view, which `probe-383-6.mjs` (2026-11-05) searched alone and beside « transcriptions », where it was left out. It keeps the messages of the authors named, strictly after `min_id` and strictly before `max_id` (`pnpm test:contract`, 2026-11-05), and those whose words the text names, newest first or oldest first, cut into pages by `limit`, 1 to 25 and 25 by default, and `offset`, 0 to 9,975; it refuses the rest, a `limit` or an `offset` that is no integer, and a text of more than 1,024 characters, with the 400 of code 50035 that Discord answered (`pnpm test:contract`, 2026-11-05, and `probe-383-10.mjs`, 2026-11-05, for `abc`, `NaN` and decimals); `total_results` counts every page (`pnpm test:contract`, 2026-11-05). A text is its words, found in the content or in one text of an embed at a time, its title, its description, its author's name, its footer, or the name or the value of a field. `probe-383-6.mjs` and `pnpm test:contract` showed on 2026-11-05 that Discord finds a word whole, whatever its case and with its accents, never cut short, two words swapped or one apart in one text, and no two words across two texts of a message or two messages; `probe-383-9.mjs` (2026-11-05) showed it for the end of a field's value and the next field's name, a description and a footer, and two embeds. The rest of the fake's rule is inferred, not shown: that words split at everything that is no letter, digit or emoji, where a hyphen, a number, an ID and an emoji alone were shown (`probe-383-6.mjs`, `probe-383-7.mjs`, `probe-383-8.mjs`, 2026-11-05), and that two words are found within 2 words of one another, in any order, which follows the docs' `slop` of 2, « Max number of words to skip between matching tokens » (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/message, **Search Guild Messages**, read 2026-11-05), the fake's window being the words asked plus 2, where no probe tried a gap of more than one word. A blank text filters nothing and a text with no word in it finds nothing, as Discord answered an empty text, a blank one and `«»` (`probe-383-6.mjs`, 2026-11-05). The fake has none of Discord's index lag, of tens of seconds (`probe-383-3.mjs` to `probe-383-9.mjs`, 2026-11-05), nor its 202 and its 429s: a test makes `searchMessages` fail once with `failNext`. Its state's `messageContentIntent: false` stands for the Message Content intent turned off: `listMessages` and `searchMessages` then give the content and the embeds of a message of another author emptied, unless the application wrote it or it mentions the application, by `<@id>` or `<@!id>`, as Discord says of an application without the intent (https://docs.discord.com/developers/events/gateway, **Message Content Intent**, read 2026-11-05), and no text matches an emptied message (inferred: the intent is on for both applications, so Discord's search with it off was not tried, 2026-11-05). It is on by default. The tests of `src/ticket-archives/` pose an archive through `archiveMessage`, whose embed `src/ticket-archives/archive-message.test.ts` holds to the one ShorBot builds (`archive_embed`, `shorbot/support_tickets.py` on `dev-gilles`, read 2026-11-05), and a `no-restricted-imports` rule keeps that helper in test files. The tests of `src/support-tickets/` and the end-to-end flows pose a ticket's channel through `supportTicketChannel`, which writes the overwrites ShorBot writes, with `channelIdAt` to date its ID, and the same rule keeps that helper in test files (#388). The tests of `src/game-log/` and the end-to-end flows pose the game server's lines through `gameLogMessage` and its channel through `gameLogChannel`, and the same rule keeps that helper in test files (`.claude/rules/game-log.md`, #394). It kicks a member as `MEMBER_KICK`, under the application and with the panel's reason, and keeps their account as one that is not on the Discord server, as Discord did (trial under `pnpm dev`, 2026-10-29, #215); it refuses, with that 403 of code 50013, to kick the owner or a member whose highest role equals or exceeds the application's, though not one with Administrator, whom Discord's rule for a bot does not name, and answers an account that is not a member with Unknown Member (inferred from the same pages, since the kick stays out of the contract suite, #180). It bans an account that is not on the Discord server, by its ID, as `MEMBER_BAN_ADD` under the application, with the panel's reason, which the ban keeps too, and lifts a ban as `MEMBER_BAN_REMOVE`, the account staying off the Discord server; it refuses a ban that deletes more than 7 days of messages, 604,800 seconds, with that 400, and answers the lift of an account that is not banned with Unknown Ban, as Discord did (`pnpm test:contract`, 2026-10-29, #181). It bans a member the same way, the member leaving the Discord server, as a trial in production found (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #218), and, like the kick, refuses with that 403 to ban the owner or a member whose highest role equals or exceeds the application's, though not one with Administrator (inferred from the pages the kick cites, since a member's ban stays out of the contract suite). It refuses with that 400 a ban that deletes fewer than 0 seconds, outside the 0 to 604,800 Discord documents (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/guild, modified 2026-10-27), and answers a ban of an ID that names no account with Unknown User (inferred, not tried). It deletes no message it holds, but keeps the seconds a ban asks to delete, which `deletedMessageSecondsOf(userId)` reads, as no method of `DiscordApi` does (#181). An audit log reason, which Discord documents as 1 to 512 characters (ADR 0004), it refuses neither way: it keeps a longer one's first 512 characters, and an empty one as no reason at all (`pnpm test:contract`, 2026-10-25). The fake does the same, so a test that reads its reason back from the audit log sees the cut (Gilles, 2026-10-25, on #59). `failNext(method, error)` makes that method's next call throw that `DiscordError`, once. A test that needs a method to fail with anything else, as a bug in the panel would, subclasses the fake in its own file and overrides that method, as the audit log's test of a member lookup does (#141), and so does a test that needs a method to answer some calls and fail the others, as the audit log's test of a filtered read does, whose fake answers only the entries of the type read and throws the `DiscordError` of a 503 for the rest (#231). Each test builds its own fake.
- **The HTTP adapter**, next to the fake in `src/discord/`, takes a `DiscordApi` and a `DiscordOAuth` as parameters and answers a `Request` addressed to Discord with a `Response`, in the shape of `fetch`. It imports no fake: tests and Playwright hand it one. `httpAdapter`, in `src/discord/http-adapter.ts`, answers a `DiscordError` with its status and Discord's JSON error body, `retry_after` included, and an absent member, user or ban with Unknown Member, Unknown User or Unknown Ban, as Discord does; it rejects, as `fetch` does on a network error, for a `DiscordError` without a status, and for any other error its `DiscordApi` or `DiscordOAuth` throws, and answers a route the panel does not call with Discord's 404, `{"message": "404: Not Found", "code": 0}` (`curl https://discord.com/api/v10/no-such-route`, 2026-10-25). It answers `/users/@me` by its `Authorization`: `Bot` with `DiscordApi`'s application user, `Bearer` with `DiscordOAuth`'s current user (#179), and a kick, a ban and a ban's lifting with Discord's 204 and no body (#180, #181), and a channel's messages, from the query's `before` and `limit` (#197), the Discord server's channels (#384), and the search of its messages, which it answers with Discord's shape, each message found in an array of its own (#383).
- **Assertions read state.** A test proves a write by what it leaves on the Discord server, read back through `DiscordApi`'s own methods, never by the calls the fake received.
- **Testing the staff check.** The check by Discord user ID (ADR 0005) is tested in Vitest against the fake, a staff role removed between two requests included. `requireStaffMember` itself is covered end to end, since it reads `cookies()`, which throws outside a request (`node_modules/next/dist/server/request/cookies.js:104`), and redirects. A `StaffMember` in a test comes only from that check, called on a fake that gives the member the staff role: no test writes `as StaffMember`. A type test in `src/staff/`, checked by `pnpm typecheck` rather than by Vitest's typecheck mode, proves that an object written by hand with the same fields is not a `StaffMember`.
- **The contract suite**, written against the `DiscordApi` interface, runs three times: on the fake, and on the real implementation over the HTTP adapter and a fake, both in `pnpm test`; then on the real implementation against Gilles' test Discord server, with "Shor Panel Dev"'s token, by `pnpm test:contract`, by hand and through `contract.yml` in CI (**Delivery**). That last run reads the same `.env.local` as `pnpm dev`, or in CI the environment `discord-contract`, deletes what it creates, never touches the rule `pnpm dev` manages, and passes before a PR that touches `src/discord/` merges: `contract.yml` runs it on such a PR, and until #102 lands, the PR says it ran by hand. It bans `CONTRACT_ABSENT_USER_ID`, an account absent from the test Discord server, by its ID, reads the reason back from the ban, lifts the ban, and checks the entries the audit log keeps of both and that the audit log's `users` name that account, refuses a ban that deletes 7 days of messages and a second, and lifts the ban of an account that is not banned as Unknown Ban; each test that bans that account lifts the ban once it ends, whatever its outcome (#181). Before #178 gave "Shor Panel Dev" Ban Members, View Audit Log was enough to read the bans (`pnpm test:contract`, 2026-10-28, #176). It lists the members, which needs the Server Members Intent that #318 turned on for « Shor Panel Dev » on 2026-11-02: the list, read 1,000 a call, holds the application and runs by ascending ID, and a page of one member followed by the page after its ID gives a member with a greater ID (`pnpm test:contract`, 2026-11-02, #322). It times out `CONTRACT_MEMBER_ID`, a member of the test Discord server below the application's role, replaces the timeout, lifts it, checks the entries the audit log keeps of them, which #177 reads, and that the audit log's `users` name that member, which #182 reads, gives that member 28 days less a minute and refuses 28 days and a minute, tries the owner, and sends that member a direct message, which it then deletes; each test that times the member out lifts the timeout once it ends, whatever its outcome (#179). It posts to that member's DM channel a message with a text, then one that carries an embed alone, lists the channel's messages and reads them newest first, each with its author, the application, and its embed, then lists those before the newer one, and fails a channel that does not exist as Unknown Channel (`pnpm test:contract`, 31 tests of 31 on the test Discord server, 2026-11-05, #197). It uses a DM channel, as it did before the search tests found a channel of the Discord server (#383): it proves the route's shape, not the application's right to read the archives channel, nor that it receives the embeds of its own messages in a guild channel, which Message Content would empty for another author's (inferred from ADR 0009's reading of Discord's page on the gateway), and the manual try of #197 on Shor, whose record reads the archives through the search since #387, proves both for that read. It reads the roles and checks that each carries a `colors.primary_color` that is a whole number from 0, which is no color, to 0xffffff, which the badge of a member's highest role reads, since the panel had never read `colors` before (`pnpm test:contract`, 2026-11-02, 31 tests of 31 on the test Discord server, #323). It reads the channels and checks that each carries an integer type, a name, a parent that is a listed category or none, and overwrites that are a role's or a member's, whose `allow` and `deny` are bitfield strings, and that each role carries its `permissions` as a bitfield string, listed or read with the Discord server, whose roles are the same, which `canViewChannel` reads (`pnpm test:contract`, 33 tests of 33 on the test Discord server, 2026-11-05, #384). It searches the Discord server's messages (#383) in the first text channel of the Discord server in which the application reads the history and posts, which it finds itself through `listChannels` and `listMessages`, since `contract.yml`'s environment holds no variable for a channel and adding one is a step for Gilles (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #383, for Gilles to review): a nested `describe` posts six messages there once, a content, an embed, both and three more, and deletes them at the end. It proves that a word of a content finds its message with its channel, its author and its content, that the title, the description, the author's name, a field's name and value and the footer of an embed find theirs, that a word is found whole, whatever its case, with its accents, and that an apostrophe, U+2019, joins the letters around it into one word (`pnpm test:contract`, 42 tests of 42 on the test Discord server, 2026-11-05, #386), that words close together in one text are found in any order and none across two texts, that the author filter keeps the application's messages and none of a member's, that `min_id` and `max_id` are strict, that `limit` and `offset` page newest first and `sort_order` reverses, and that a `limit` over 25, an `offset` over 9,975, a text of over 1,024 characters and a channel that does not exist are refused. Discord's index lags, a message being found 8 to 58 s after its post and its words later still (`probe-383-3.mjs` to `probe-383-9.mjs`, 2026-11-05), so each expectation of what a search finds is retried every 5 s for 4 minutes while it fails as an assertion, and only a negative that follows a positive of the same fixture is asserted at once, since a search can also give fewer messages than it should: the first full run of the suite, on 2026-11-05, got one message where two were expected for `min_id` and `max_id` (ADR 0010). The fake and the adapter pass at once, and Discord takes about 2 to 3 minutes for the 9 tests (`pnpm test:contract` on the test Discord server, 2026-11-05, #383: 41 tests of 42 in 196 s, then 42 of 42 in 220 s and again in 165 s once every expectation was retried). It creates a rule that blocks the message with a custom message and times the member out, exempting the application's own role, which Discord accepts, reads it back as created, and checks the edges of what Discord measured (**The fake**): 2,419,200 seconds pass and 2,419,201 are refused through a modify, and a custom message of 300 characters passes while one of 301 is refused through a modify and through a create (#325). That rule is disabled, named `Contract …`, holds a keyword nobody types and is deleted whatever the outcome, and the suite holds only that one rule at a time there, so that the test Discord server keeps room for the rules of `pnpm dev`, 6 keyword rules at most. The 28 days was once exactly 28 days from the local clock: on 2026-11-02, its run for #246 answered the test « a timeout ends at most 28 days later » with a 400 of code 50035, while `date -u` read 08:56:27 and 08:56:28 around a `Date` header of 08:56:24 GMT from `curl -I https://discord.com/api/v10/gateway`, so that the clock ran 3 to 4 s ahead of Discord's, measured right after the run. A clock ahead of Discord's by any margin pushes an end of exactly 28 days past the limit (inferred), so the accepted end is 28 days less a minute, which a clock up to a minute ahead passes, and the refused one stays 28 days and a minute (inferred; decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #246, for Gilles to review). The fake has no other tests but the kick's and a member's ban, which stay out of the suite, like `DiscordOAuth`, since only the account can undo either, by joining again through an invite, once the ban is lifted (#181): Vitest tests run the real implementation over the adapter and the fake, and, for the kick, a trial under `pnpm dev`, on a test account that Gilles then brings back to the test Discord server, proves the rest (#180), as the first did on 2026-10-29, with `CONTRACT_MEMBER_ID`, whose direct message went 0.45 s before its `MEMBER_KICK` (#215), and a trial in production proved it for a member's ban on 2026-10-29, where #218 had planned one under `pnpm dev`, of which the test Discord server's audit log holds no entry (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #218; `GET /guilds/{guild.id}/audit-logs?action_type=22`, 2026-10-29). `DiscordOAuth` stays out of the suite, since a code exchange needs a member's consent in a browser: its fake follows Discord's docs, a Vitest test runs its real implementation over the adapter, and the first logins with "Shor Panel Dev", on `pnpm dev` and on a preview, prove the rest.
- **The real implementations** take `fetch` as a parameter, which `createDiscordApi()` and `createDiscordOAuth()` fill with `globalThis.fetch`. Tests pass a function that returns hand-written `Response`s, for what the contract suite cannot trigger on demand: a 5xx, a 429, a timeout, a network error. Delays run on real timers, with no clock injected, since the fake timers of Vitest 5.0.5 do not fake `AbortSignal` (`FakeMethod`, `node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/index.aTZaPVhf.js:3915`): the test's `fetch` rejects with the `TimeoutError` that `AbortSignal.timeout` raises, and a 429's `retry_after` is a few milliseconds when it is retried, 2.5 s when it is not. The 10 s value itself goes untested.
- **No DOM.** Vitest keeps running without one (**Feedback loops**). The component a client leaf hands its state to (**Components**) renders with `react-dom/server`, or, for `StaffHeader`, whose « Modération » waits on a promise, with `react-dom/static`'s `prerender`, which waits for it (#176), given a `progressiveChunkSize` without limit: past its default 12,800 bytes, which the sidebar's and the bar's two logos exceed, the render moves a `<Suspense>` it has completed to the end of the document, behind a `<template>` that a script would fill (`node_modules/react-dom/cjs/react-dom-server.node.development.js:8603-8608`, react-dom 19.3.1; #224); the leaf's state and events are proven end to end, and so is the toast it adds once a write lands, its one piece of logic, for which Gilles kept this rule (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #227), and the write of the theme a staff member chooses, the theme toggle's leaf's one piece of logic, for which #225 kept it too (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #225; `e2e/theme.test.ts`). The client components copied from shadcn/ui hold no logic of the panel's: they wrap Base UI primitives, whose logic is Base UI's, as the Toast does Base UI's Toast and its manager, and the Tabs Base UI's Tabs, whose open tab stays Base UI's (#234), and the Dialog Base UI's Dialog, whose open state stays Base UI's too (#235), or, as the Label does, plain elements (**Interface**). This is revisited with the first client component of the panel's, copied ones included, that holds logic of its own beyond that toast and that write.
- **The theme.** `src/ui/globals.test.ts` reads `src/ui/globals.css`, the light values in its `:root` and the dark ones in the `:root` under `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)`, and proves each contrast pair that **Accessibility** sets (**Interface**), in light and in dark (#70): at 4.5:1, every text color on its surface, such as `primary-foreground` on `primary`, but `destructive` on its own tint, which the MVP does not use (**Buttons**), and also `muted-foreground` on `background`, on `sidebar` (#224), on `card` and `popover` (#238), and on `accent`, the fill of an account found on hover (#232), and on `sidebar-accent`, the current section's fill, under the blocked words' count (**Navigation**, #226), `primary-foreground` on `primary/90` on `background`, the default Button's hover fill (**Copied code**, #222), and on `card`, where the blocked words' form sets « Ajouter » (#228), `destructive-foreground` on `destructive`, the red buttons of a record's sanctions, and on `destructive/90` on `background` and on `card`, their hover fill (**Buttons**, #234), `foreground` on the tints of the card « Sanctions »'s notices, `warning/6` and `destructive/10` on `card` (**Theme**, #234), `foreground` on `muted`, the card « Exceptions » (#228), and, in dark, `foreground` on the `input/30` and `input/50` fills of the field and of the outline button, and `muted-foreground`, the search field's placeholder, on its `input/30` fill (#232), both also on the field's `input/30` fill over `card`, in the blocked words' form (#228), `foreground` on the outline button's `input/30` and `input/50` fills over `warning/6` over `card`, in a running timeout's notice, and on `input/30` over `muted`, the open tab of a record's sanctions (#234); at 3:1, the border of the lift's reason and the focus ring in a running timeout's notice, `input` and `ring/50` on `warning/6` on `card` (#234), the field's border, `input` on `background` and on `card` and, in dark, on the field's own `input/30` fill over each, the focus ring, `ring/50` on `background`, `card`, `popover` and `muted`, the theme toggle's fill (#225), and `sidebar-ring/50` on `sidebar`, the border of the theme chosen in the toggle, `primary` on `muted` and on `card` (**Theme**, #225), and the current section's icon, `primary` on `sidebar-accent` (**Navigation**, #224). `card` and `popover` stand out from `background` in both schemes since #222, where « parchemin et cuir » set them apart in dark alone (`src/ui/globals.css` before d78ca61) (**Theme**): a toast sits on `popover`, its « Fermer » in `muted-foreground` (`src/ui/toast.tsx:45`, `:130`), and the pages that #221 redesigns are to put their forms and their messages on `card`, to which #221 proposes to map the mockup's `--panel` (#238). Each test's name says its pair. `ornament` has none: the rule it draws is decorative and carries nothing, so 1.4.11 asks no contrast of it (inferred, #224). It computes WCAG's ratio from the `oklch()` values, through OKLab and linear sRGB, clipping what falls outside sRGB, and lays an opacity a class adds, such as `ring-ring/50`, over what lies below in gamma-encoded sRGB, as browsers composite (inferred). Two tests hold that measure to known ratios: Tailwind's `neutral-500`, `#737373`, at 4.74:1 on white, and black at 50%, `#808080`, at 3.95:1 (WCAG's formula worked by hand on those hex values, 2026-10-27). It covers what axe leaves out, since axe measures only the contrast of text (inferred, not checked against axe's rule list). It also proves `destructive`, the color of a failed write's message, and `success` at 4.5:1 on `background` and on `card` (**Forms**, #238), and `warning` likewise, as #232 decided, though the badge's dot it draws is `aria-hidden` and carries nothing, so 1.4.11 asks no contrast of it (inferred, as for `ornament`): with the handoff's `oklch(0.58 0.13 65)` in light, both pairs failed (#232), and that `src/app/icon.svg` draws the logo in dark's `primary` on a tile of light's `primary`, each written as the hex of the theme's `oklch()` value (**Theme**; #132). The dot of a role's badge is `aria-hidden` and carries nothing either, since the text names the role, so 1.4.11 asks no contrast of it, and the theme test proves none for a color that Discord sets (inferred, as for the status badge's dot; #323). Last, it proves that the blocks of a theme chosen, `:root[data-theme="light"]` and `:root[data-theme="dark"]`, set the same custom properties as the light `:root` and the dark one it measures, each under its own `color-scheme`, so that a value changed in one block and not in its copy fails it (**Theme**, #225).
- **Testing monitoring.** Vitest proves four things (**Monitoring**). `isPanelError` says yes to a panel error and no to Discord unavailable, a `DiscordError` from another copy of its class included. The alert built from a `DiscordError` of `getMember` holds the route's pattern and no member ID, and leaves out a `request.path` that carries `code` and `state`. Sending an alert, over a hand-written `fetch` and a `waitUntil` that keeps each send for the test to await, gives up after its delay, drops a 429 and never throws, and the test captures the one `console.error` line a failed send writes (**The tests' console**). The delay runs on real timers, as in **The real implementations**, so that test lasts its 2 s. The login callback's domain function, over an in-memory `Monitor`, reports a panel error at `error` and a missing `state` at `warn`; a report is its output, not a write to Discord, so the test reads what that `Monitor` holds (**Assertions read state**). End to end sends no alert: Playwright's `webServer` sets `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` empty, which counts as unset and wins over a value in `.env.local` (**End to end**).
- **`proxy.ts`** is the one entry point with a Vitest test. `unstable_doesMiddlewareMatch` and `getRedirectUrl`, from `next/experimental/testing/server`, check that everything under `/login`, `/privacy` and static files get through, and that any other route without a readable session goes to `/login`, `/privacy-notes` and `/privacy/archive` included. Next.js 16.4.2's docs name the first `unstable_doesProxyMatch` (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/proxy.md:732-743`), which the package does not export (`node_modules/next/dist/experimental/testing/server/index.d.ts`, `middleware-testing-utils.d.ts:13`). What `proxy.ts` calls, `redirectWithoutSession` in `src/staff/`, is tested on its own over an in-memory `Monitor`: an expired or foreign seal, and an error it reports.
- **End to end.** Playwright runs `e2e/*.test.ts` against `next start`, by `pnpm test:e2e`, in CI's job after `pnpm build`, on Chromium only, installed by `playwright install chromium` on every run, uncached (**Delivery**, **Caches**), in a step that gives up after 2 minutes. It installs no system package. On 2026-10-27, `--with-deps`'s `apt-get` stalled on an Ubuntu mirror: it waited 9 min 35 s before the job's 10-minute timeout cancelled it (run 40483910314), and 8 min 36 s on another run (40486946538), where the tests then took 13 s. The libraries Chromium needs were already on the runner, 8 of them in older versions, and only 9 font packages were missing (the install log of run 40483910314). Chromium runs without them: #116's first run passed end to end, with an install of 9 s (run 40488867775, 2026-10-27; Gilles, 2026-10-27, in place of the `--with-deps` of #63 and #57). A runner that lacks a library would fail end to end at Chromium's launch, so the pull request that moves the runner to a new Ubuntu shows it (inferred). With `experimental.testProxy` (`node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/router-server.js:663`), `next.onFetch` from `next/experimental/testmode/playwright` (`node_modules/next/dist/experimental/testmode/playwright/next-fixture.d.ts:5`) answers the server's calls to Discord by handing each request to the HTTP adapter over that test's own fake. The API is experimental and absent from `node_modules/next/dist/docs/`, and once it is on, a request carrying `next-test-proxy-port` sends the server's `fetch` calls to `localhost` (`node_modules/next/dist/experimental/testmode/context.js:26`, `node_modules/next/dist/experimental/testmode/fetch.js:102`): `next.config.ts` turns it on only from `E2E_TEST_PROXY`, which Playwright's `webServer` alone sets and the build refuses on Vercel (**Security**). So end to end runs over the last `pnpm build`, made without the variable: `next start` loads `next.config.ts` again (`node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/router-server.js:133`, `node_modules/next/dist/server/config.js:1306`). `next.config.ts` sets `experimental.testProxy` only when it is on, since Next.js lists every experimental key a config sets, `false` included, under « Experiments (use with caution) » (`node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/app-info-log.js:111-118`); a build without the variable records none (`.next/required-server-files.json`, 2026-10-25). `next.config.test.ts`, at the root, imports the config under each environment and proves it: no key without the variable, `testProxy` with it, and a refusal when `VERCEL` is set. The `webServer` runs `next start` on port 3100 and waits on the port alone, through a TCP connection rather than an HTTP request that would carry no `next-test-proxy-port`; it never reuses a server already running, such as `pnpm dev`'s, and gives `next start` placeholder Discord and staff variables, an archives channel and an empty `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` (**Testing monitoring**), which win over `.env.local`'s, since `@next/env` sets no variable the environment already holds (`processEnv`, `node_modules/.pnpm/@next+env@16.4.2/node_modules/@next/env/dist/index.js`). Next.js's `next` fixture marks only the requests of a test that uses it (`node_modules/next/dist/experimental/testmode/playwright/next-fixture.js:23-29`), so `e2e/fixtures.ts` gives every test, through automatic fixtures, its own `FakeDiscord` behind the adapter, built from the `discordState` option the test sets and given the archives channel of the placeholder, which a record reads and which stays empty unless the test poses archives in it, and the check of **The browser's console**. Its `holdDiscord` fixture holds every call the server then makes to Discord until the function it returns is called, through a `next.onFetch` handler that Next.js asks before the adapter's, since it asks the last one registered first and moves on when one returns `undefined` (`node_modules/next/dist/experimental/testmode/playwright/next-fixture.js:36-43`), so that a page shows its skeletons; a test releases it within the 10 s after which a call gives up (**Retries**) (#139); a `no-restricted-imports` rule of `eslint.config.mjs` has every other file of `e2e/` take `test` and `expect` from it. When the adapter rejects, the fixture answers `abort`, which the server's `fetch` throws as a network error would (`node_modules/next/dist/experimental/testmode/fetch.js:126-128`), and when the rejection is no `DiscordError`, a bug of the fake or of the adapter, it also fails the test. Playwright's `request` fixture carries no `next-test-proxy-port`, so a server call made for it would reach the real network, with the placeholder values: a flow drives `page`, but for the visitor without a session, whose `/blocked-words` only the proxy answers, for the `200` of `/privacy`, which proves that the proxy lets it through with no redirect (#186), for the 307 of `/`, which `next.config.ts` sends before the proxy runs (**Navigation**), and for the favicon a page links, a static file (#135), none calling Discord, through `page.request` with no redirect followed, since `page.goto` would follow `/login` on to the real Discord. Playwright calls a route handler "only for the first url if the response is a redirect" (`node_modules/.pnpm/playwright-core@1.64.1/node_modules/playwright-core/types/types.d.ts:4441`): on 2026-10-26, a probe whose page routed `discord.com` still reached the real Discord through a redirect, while Chromium kept the test headers over a redirect within one origin. So the browser's leg of OAuth never reaches `discord.com/oauth2/authorize`: the `logIn` fixture routes `/login` itself, fetches the panel's answer without following it, checks that it points to Discord's authorize URL, and fulfills it in place with its `state` cookie; the test then opens `/login/callback` with that `state` and a code the fake issued (Gilles, 2026-10-26, on #10, in place of the `page.route` on Discord that #54 had planned). E2E covers only the flows where a wiring mistake shows nowhere else: a staff member logs in and lands on `/blocked-words`, beside a sidebar that names them; a staff member with an avatar on the Discord server sees it in the sidebar, served by the local route below, beside their name, then at 360 px in the bar, beside the menu button, their name gone into the menu (#140); they move between sections through the sidebar, which marks the current one and prefetches none, and log out from it; at 1,024 px, the sidebar runs 252 px wide down the screen's left edge and stays there as the page scrolls, at 1,023 and 768 px a rail of 64 px takes its place and stays there too, and at 767 px the bar takes their place at the top of the page and stays there as the page scrolls (#224, #356); the rail puts a 24 px rule, hidden from assistive technologies, between each group and the one before it, and shows no group title, which the sidebar shows, « Journaux » among them (`e2e/responsive.test.ts`, #392; it failed on a throwaway build whose « Journaux » had no rule, `pnpm test:e2e e2e/responsive.test.ts -g "the navigation is a bar below md"`, 2026-11-05); at 360 px, they open the bar's menu, which shows the sections under « Administration » and « Journaux », which prefetches no section either, move to the other section, which closes the menu and moves `aria-current`, and log out from the menu, and the menu fades in, sliding only with motion (#137); moving between sections fades the old content out and the new one in, animating nothing but opacity, under reduced motion as with motion (#134), and slides the current section's bar over, only with motion (#137, #224); `/` redirects to `/blocked-words` with a 307; a member without the staff role is refused, with neither sidebar nor bar, under « Accès refusé », offered « Se reconnecter » on `primary` and « Se déconnecter » in `outline`, each button's computed colors read against those of the theme's variables, `primary` and `primary-foreground`, or `background`, `border` and `foreground` (#236); a visitor without a session goes to `/login`; a visitor without a session opens `/privacy`, which answers `200` with no redirect, in French, and reaches it from a login page's footer, whose « Confidentialité » it then marks current, while a staff member reaches it from the sidebar, and at 360 px from the menu, which closes (`e2e/privacy-policy.test.ts`, #186), the first flow failing with a `307` to `/login` on a throwaway build whose matcher of `proxy.ts` did not leave out `/privacy` (2026-11-02); a member who loses the staff role mid-session lands on `/login/refused` at the next page, and logs out from there to `/login/logged-out`, which a deletion of `__Host-session` without `Secure` would fail, where « Ta session est terminée. » offers « Se connecter avec Discord » on `primary` (#236); a blocked word is added, then removed, each write saying so in a toast (#227), the word fading into the list lit up, then fading out of it, with motion (#138), and the capacity gauge counting it, then no longer, out of 3,000 (#228, #325); a moderator adds a word with « Exclusion 1 heure », which its tile names and its toast announces, and which the fake holds in « Mots interdits du panel · exclusion 1 heure », sparing the staff role, then removes it, the toast naming the action again, and a word that the list already holds under another action is refused in the message that names it, the field keeping the word and the choice its action (#325); a staff member logs out from the page of the audit log that shows both pagination links, which are in view, and `__Host-session` is gone and no page is prefetched, in one flow for both links, where a failure cannot say which one prefetched since `prefetchedPaths` records paths alone, and with the scripts requested after the click held back for a second by `page.route`, so that the links stay mounted past the router's 300 ms cooldown (**Navigation**, **Gotchas**; #168). On the build that still prefetched them, `pnpm test:e2e e2e/audit-log.test.ts -g "both pagination links" --repeat-each 8` failed 8 runs of 8 with the hold, each on `prefetchedPaths` at `["/audit-log", "/audit-log", "/login", "/login"]` and on two `ERR_SSL_PROTOCOL_ERROR` console lines, and the same command failed 2 runs of 8 without it, under a CPU slowdown of ×50 set through the Chrome DevTools Protocol's `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate` (a throwaway edit of the flow, 2026-10-30). The flow reads whether both links are visible when the first script is released, a second after its request: that proves the window the red needs, the links on screen past the cooldown, and fails the flow when no script is requested, but not that the router tried to prefetch in that window, which `prefetchedPaths` tells (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #168, for Gilles to review); a staff member who moderates chooses « Exclusions » from the audit log's pills, which opens `/audit-log?type=timeouts`, and logs out from there, with the five pills, « Réinitialiser », the form's two fields and its button and the links of a timeout's account and of its author all in the viewport, so that `__Host-session` is gone and no page is prefetched, the scripts requested after the click held back for a second as above (#268). It reaches the page through the pill rather than `goto`, as the staff member does: `goto` was not tried, so the flow proves nothing of a page loaded in full. Like the pagination's, it cannot say which link prefetched, since `prefetchedPaths` records paths alone, and the pills', « Réinitialiser »'s and the form's are all `/audit-log`. Three throwaway builds that each took out one `prefetch={false}` made it fail, `pnpm test:e2e e2e/audit-log.test.ts -g "moderates logs out" --repeat-each 8` failing 8 runs of 8 each time, each on `prefetchedPaths` and on `ERR_SSL_PROTOCOL_ERROR` console lines: without the pills' (`audit-log-type-filter.tsx`), `prefetchedPaths` held `/audit-log` five times and `/login` five times, with five console lines; without « Réinitialiser »'s, `["/audit-log", "/login"]` and one line; without the account link's (`audit-log.tsx`), `/records/<id>` three times and `/login` once, and one line (2026-10-30). A fourth, without the filter form's `prefetch={false}` (`audit-log-filter-form.tsx`), made it fail 8 runs of 8, the three whose output was read each on `prefetchedPaths` at `["/audit-log", "/login"]`, two entries, and on one `ERR_SSL_PROTOCOL_ERROR` console line, which the fixture lists once however often Chromium logged it (2026-11-02, #321). The trials ran before the flow asserted its links in the viewport once more right before the logout and ran axe on the pages it crosses, two additions that only add checks. Every page these flows cross also passes `@axe-core/playwright`'s WCAG 2.2 A and AA rules, in light and in dark, and scrolls no wider than a 360 px viewport (#72), and reports no Content Security Policy violation (#76). For axe and the 360 px viewport, a test calls the `checkAccessibility` fixture of `e2e/fixtures.ts` on each page it crosses, but `/blocked-words` where `logIn` lands on the way to another page, which the login and blocked-word flows check, and after each write's toast or message, once what `<Suspense>` streams shows. A test finds a toast through the `toast` fixture, by its accessible name in the region « Notifications », or every toast when it names none, and reads a write's toast through `readToast`, which waits for that toast, then presses F6, which focuses that region and pauses the toasts' timers (`node_modules/@base-ui/react/toast/viewport/ToastViewport.js:61-67`), so that the checks that follow, which can outlast a toast's 5 s, still see it, and see its « Fermer », which Base UI hides from assistive technologies until the region is focused or hovered (**Forms**, #227). The fixture first waits for the page's `load`, since `toHaveURL` can resolve before it: after the reload in the flow of a member who loses the staff role, the browser loaded `/`, then requested `/login/refused` as a new document 17 ms after `/`'s `load`, and `toHaveURL` resolved 6 ms before that document's `load` (throwaway test, 2026-10-27). A test passes it `waitForLoad: false` to skip that wait, as the skeleton test does, since a page whose calls to Discord are held never fires `load`. It then runs axe on the tags `wcag2a`, `wcag2aa`, `wcag21a`, `wcag21aa` and `wcag22aa`, since axe-core 4.14.0 tags no rule `wcag22a` (`axe.getRules()`, 2026-10-27), under `page.emulateMedia` in light then in dark, each time once no animation of `document.getAnimations()` is still running, which waits out the transitions the switch starts, and a view transition still running, whose pseudo-elements' animations it lists until the view transition ends (throwaway test, 2026-10-27; #134), while a finished one may stay there for good (**Gotchas**, #211): without that wait, axe measured a throwaway test's 1 s color transition halfway, at 1.04:1 (2026-10-27), as it would the Button's `transition-all` and the Input's `transition-colors`, whose colors dark changes (inferred). Before each axe run, it also waits for the page to have a title, through `toHaveTitle(/\S/)` since axe trims the title before it checks it, because Next.js mounts the `<title>` anew with each response and the page has none for about 0.3 s now and then (**Gotchas**, #161): that wait goes with the **Gotchas** line once Next.js stops remounting the metadata with each response. `e2e/check-accessibility.test.ts` is the one end-to-end test of a fixture rather than of a flow, and it lives in Playwright because the wait it proves runs on a page: it takes the `<title>` away for 1 s, then calls `checkAccessibility`. In each scheme it also reads the body's background, which must differ between the two, so that the dark run never checks the light theme again: against a build whose dark values sat under shadcn/ui's `.dark` class, the body kept `lab(100 0 0)` in dark, and the check failed (#70, 2026-10-27). On a page whose theme a staff member chose, which both schemes show alike, it runs axe once, in that theme, under the scheme the test emulates, and compares no backgrounds (#225). Last, it narrows the viewport to 360 px, where the document's `scrollWidth` must not exceed its `clientWidth`, and restores the color scheme and the viewport. Every test runs under Playwright's `reducedMotion: "reduce"`, which the `use` of `playwright.config.ts` sets, and a test that needs the animations asks for them through `test.use({ reducedMotion: "no-preference" })`, as one run of the crossfade test, of the skeleton test, of the blocked words' slide (#166) and of the toast's motion (#227) do, and the blocked-word flow (#138), so that the animations' path meets **The browser's console** too (#134). `e2e/fixtures.ts` also routes `https://cdn.discordapp.com/**` to a local SVG, through an automatic fixture, `discordCdnRequests`, which records each URL it serves, since Chromium loads an image itself, through no `next.onFetch`, and would otherwise reach Discord's CDN (inferred): the avatar's test reads that record, and no test reaches the real CDN (#140). Its `prefetchedPaths` fixture records the path of each request the page sends with `next-router-prefetch`, which the sidebar's, the menu's and the audit log's flows that log out read, so that a section's prefetch, which could reach `/login` after the logout (**Navigation**), fails them on every run rather than now and then: both failed in 6 runs of 6 before the sections' links stopped prefetching (#153, 2026-10-28). The audit log's two flows that log out read it too. Their pagination links, the pills and « Réinitialiser » link to the page's own pathname, and the form's action is that pathname too, and none sends a prefetch before the logout (a probe on the pagination links, #168; inferred for the pills, « Réinitialiser » and the form), so those flows hold the scripts of the page they go to, as above (#168, #268). The sanction's link goes to `/records/<id>`: its throwaway build recorded that path three times, and whether before or after the logout is not established, so that link may be caught without the hold (inferred). `e2e/responsive.test.ts` is a file of its own: it crosses the staff pages and the public ones at four widths for what no flow above checks, a page that scrolls sideways and a control too small for a finger, and the navigation's three forms and a dialog docked to the bottom (**Responsive**, #355).

## Interface

What the panel's interface is built with, as Gilles decided while grilling #52 on 2026-10-23; the options set aside, and why, are in #52's decisions. Like **Architecture**, it was written before the code: #67 sets up the tools, then every ticket that writes a page follows it. What the tools carry, the theme's values, small screens, navigation, wording, dates, buttons, what a form shows and the accessibility level, Gilles settled while grilling #53 on 2026-10-24. #74 asked the POs to confirm three of his answers, Shor's visual identity, the use on phones and the tone, until Gilles took over what the POs decided (`docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`, **People**, 2026-10-27): those answers stand, and #74 asks the POs only for facts, such as a smaller version of Shor's logo. The identity itself, from Shor's logo and with Shor's site as a reference the panel must not look like (https://shor-rp.fr, read 2026-10-27), Gilles settled while grilling #129 on 2026-10-27, in place of #53's neutral defaults: the palette, the headings' font, the logo, **Motion**, the skeletons, the buttons' icons and **Avatars**. #129's tickets wrote it, #132 to #141, then #166, which landed last, on 2026-10-28 (ad7e999); the options set aside, and why, are in #129's decisions. Its palette, « parchemin et cuir », gave way to Hydromel, from a mockup Gilles made with Claude Design and settled while grilling #221 on 2026-10-29, which #222 wrote (**Theme**); #221's decisions say what else of the mockup the panel takes, ticket by ticket. `docs/design/hydromel/` holds the mockup's captures, the visual reference of those tickets, and where a capture and its ticket differ, the ticket wins (#221, #223; this pointer decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #223, for Gilles to review). **Code style** says how classes are sorted.

- **Framework.** React, through Next.js (#5), with no library for state, client-side fetching, forms, URL state or animation: Discord holds the state, Server Components read it, Server Actions write it, and a form sends one field. This is revisited when a client leaf must share state with another or fetch data itself. The theme toggles of the sidebar and of the Sheet share the theme chosen through the document rather than through React: each reads `data-theme`, which holds it, through `useSyncExternalStore`, and the one clicked dispatches `THEME_EVENT`, which wakes the others, so no state library comes in (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #225, for Gilles to review). No React Compiler either, until a client leaf grows heavy.
- **Styles.** Tailwind CSS 4, through `@tailwindcss/postcss`. `src/ui/globals.css` imports Tailwind and holds the theme; `src/app/layout.tsx` and `src/app/global-error.tsx` both import it, since `global-error` receives no global styles (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/error.md:163-165`). A class name is written whole, never built by concatenation, since Tailwind reads sources as plain text (https://tailwindcss.com/docs/detecting-classes-in-source-files, read 2026-10-23).
- **Component library.** shadcn/ui, on Base UI, in its `nova` style, with CSS variables, as `components.json` records. A ticket adds the components it is the first to use, through `pnpm exec shadcn add`. `shadcn` is a devDependency, since `shadcn init` makes `globals.css` import `shadcn/tailwind.css` from it (https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/cli, read 2026-10-23); Vercel installs devDependencies for the build (https://vercel.com/docs/builds/configure-a-build, **Install Command**, updated 2026-08-28). Components take a `className`, their variants use `cva`, and `cn` merges classes, which shadcn/ui's registry imports from the `cn` package (https://ui.shadcn.com/r/styles/base-nova/button.json, read 2026-10-23). The panel's own components follow the same shape.
- **Copied code** becomes the panel's own. Its English strings turn French, such as the Dialog's two « Close » (https://ui.shadcn.com/r/styles/base-nova/dialog.json, read 2026-10-23) and the Toast's « Close toast », « Fermer » (#227), and the rest stays as the CLI wrote it, but for the lines Prettier, the lint and the typecheck change (**Code style**), for a class the panel uses whose colors miss **Accessibility**'s level on the panel's theme, for the classes that have a toast only fade under reduced motion (**Motion**, #227), for the classes that adapt a copied component to a phone and to a finger, which it carries beside its own (**Responsive**, #355, #357; Gilles, 2026-11-03): the touch sizes, `touch:h-11` and its kin, and the offset that keeps a close button's glyph in its corner at that size; the 16 px of a field under `md`, as `text-base md:text-sm` on the NativeSelect; the `env(safe-area-inset-*)` paddings of what sticks to an edge, the Dialog, a Sheet's footer and the Toast's viewport; the Dialog's docking to the bottom under `sm`, with its slide up; and a Sheet from the bottom's `max-h-[88svh]`, with its `SheetBody`, the one component that `shadcn add` does not write, and for the toast's title, rendered as a `<p>` rather than Base UI's `h2` (**Forms**, #227), so that `shadcn add --diff` shows only what the panel changed. `pnpm exec shadcn add toast` also offered to overwrite `src/ui/button.tsx` with the registry's `hover:bg-primary/80`, which was declined (2026-10-29), as `pnpm exec shadcn add dialog` did, declined too, which then wrote the Dialog alone, with no new package (`git status`, 2026-10-29, #235). There are two such color classes. One is the default Button's hover, `hover:bg-primary/90` where the CLI wrote `hover:bg-primary/80` (`src/ui/button.tsx:10` at db79373): on Hydromel's light `primary`, `primary-foreground` fell to 3.81:1 at 80%, which axe refused end to end (`pnpm test:e2e`, 2026-10-29), and holds **Accessibility**'s ratio at 90% (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #222, for Gilles to review, since #222 fixes `primary`). The other is the text of a Tab that is not open, `text-muted-foreground` where the CLI wrote `text-foreground/60` (`src/ui/tabs.tsx`), which gave 4.29:1 on the list's `muted` in light, which axe refused end to end on the record's tabs (`pnpm test:e2e e2e/sanctions.test.ts`, 2026-10-29), where `muted-foreground` holds 5.74:1, as the mockup's `--fg2` draws it (M:480), while its `dark:text-muted-foreground` already did in dark (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #234, for Gilles to review). `pnpm exec shadcn add tabs` copied the Tabs with no new package and wrote nothing else (`git status`, 2026-10-29). The `destructive` variant, which misses it on hover too, stays as written, since the panel uses none (**Buttons**). It gets no tests of its own, though the theme test proves the palette on that hover's fill (**The theme**).
- **`src/ui/`** holds `globals.css`, what shadcn/ui's CLI copies, and the components more than one domain uses; a component moves there when a second domain needs it, but for one that renders a domain's own type, which stays in that domain for the other to import, since `src/ui/` imports nothing outside itself: `SanctionChangeSummary`, in `src/sanctions/`, says what a sanction or its lifting changed, in a record's history and in the audit log (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #182). It holds no domain logic. It also holds `theme.ts`, which the root layout and `src/staff/` share: the `Theme` type, the key under which `localStorage` keeps the choice, the event that says it changed, and `APPLY_STORED_THEME`, the script of the root layout's `<head>` (**Theme**, #225), and `dialog-motion.ts`, the classes that have a dialog only fade under reduced motion, which the sanctions' confirmation and the blocked words' dialog share (**Motion**, #326). Every alias of `components.json` points to `@/ui`, and the CLI rewrites imports through those aliases (https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/components-json, read 2026-10-23), so `src/ui/` imports itself through `@/ui/*`, never through a relative path, and nothing else through `@/`, which a `no-restricted-imports` rule of `eslint.config.mjs` enforces (Gilles, 2026-10-24, on #69). `"use client"` appears there only on a copied component the CLI writes it on, which follows no rule the panel can rely on: the Label carries it and wraps a plain `<label>`, while the Button and the Input wrap Base UI primitives without it (`pnpm exec shadcn add input label`, 2026-10-26), and the Table carried it and wrapped plain table elements (`pnpm exec shadcn add table`, 2026-10-27), until #230 removed it with the audit log's table, its one user, since a component comes in with the first ticket that uses it (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #230, for Gilles to review), and the Sheet carries it and wraps Base UI's Dialog (`pnpm exec shadcn add sheet`, 2026-10-27), while the Skeleton, a plain `<div>`, has none (`pnpm exec shadcn add skeleton`, 2026-10-27), and the Avatar carries it and wraps Base UI's Avatar (`pnpm exec shadcn add avatar`, 2026-10-28), while the Textarea, a plain `<textarea>`, has none (`pnpm exec shadcn add textarea`, 2026-10-28), and the Toast carries it and wraps Base UI's Toast (`pnpm exec shadcn add toast`, 2026-10-29), as the Tabs do Base UI's Tabs (`pnpm exec shadcn add tabs`, 2026-10-29), and the Dialog Base UI's Dialog (`pnpm exec shadcn add dialog`, 2026-10-29), while the NativeSelect, a plain `<select>`, has none, and its copy wrote that one file with no new package, the registry's placeholder icon already the `ChevronDownIcon` of `lucide-react` (`pnpm exec shadcn add native-select`, `git status`, 2026-11-02, #325); the panel's own components there have none and render from their props alone, `Overline` and `OrnamentRule` among them (#232), `AccountLink`, which an account's avatar and name sit in and which draws a link to the href it is given, or none (**Avatars**, #320), and takes the touch size, `touch:flex touch:min-h-11 touch:items-center`, on either (**Responsive**, #357), and `ReportChannelLink`, which `error.tsx` and `/login/failed` share (#61). Two more are the underlined link, which the audit log, the blocked words and `ReportChannelLink` each wrote for themselves until #256, and the frame of an empty list, which the audit log, the blocked words and the list of accounts each did: `TextLink`, a `next/link` to a page of the panel in `foreground`, underlined in `input` and in `primary` on hover, which never prefetches (**Navigation**), takes its font size from its caller and, standing alone by default, the touch size, `touch:inline-flex touch:min-h-11 touch:items-center`, where its `inSentence` prop keeps it content-sized for a link inside a sentence (**Responsive**, #357), with `TEXT_LINK_CLASSES` beside it for a link that is no `next/link`, as `ReportChannelLink`'s external one, which adds its own `whitespace-nowrap` to the constant, so the constant does not hold it; and `EmptyListFrame`, a `<div>` in a dashed frame bordered in `input`, whose sentence the caller writes as a `<p>` inside it, with only the caller's margin or stack in its `className`, as the audit log's holds a sentence and « Réinitialiser », the `wrap-anywhere` that the account search put on its inner `<p>` having gone with the text searched that its sentence repeated (#324) (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #256, for Gilles to review, since #256 asks for a shared definition and leaves open its form, its element and `prefetch`). `FilterSheet`, `FilterSheetTrigger`, `FilterSheetContent` and `FilterSheetForm`, with `FilterChips`, are what the audit log (#359) and the list of accounts (#360) fold their filters into under `md`: they moved here from `src/audit-log/` with #360, since a second domain needed them, and they import nothing outside `src/ui/` and know nothing of either page's filters (**Forms and filters** of **Responsive**). `FilterPills`, in `filter-pills.tsx`, is the row of pills that the audit log's types and the support tickets' kinds share, with an optional badge after a pill's label and « Réinitialiser » when it is given an address, and `filter-layout.ts` holds the constants of the filters' layout that the two pages and their skeletons share, the pills' row and size, a control's height, the form's `hidden md:flex` and the fold's `md:hidden`: they moved here from `src/audit-log/` with #386, for the same reason. `FilterFields`, in `filter-fields.tsx`, draws a log's text and day inputs from a list of specs, each under its `Label` and with an id that starts with the prefix it is given, beside `PERIOD_FIELDS`, « Du » and « Au », named `from` and `to`, `searchTextField`, « Texte », named `text`, `filterChipsOf`, which turns the fields set into the chips of `FilterChips`, and `formatDay`; `filter-params.ts` holds `dayParamOf`, which reads a day `yyyy-mm-dd` that is a calendar day from 2015 to 2099 and counts anything else as absent, and `textParamOf`; `paris-day-bounds.ts` holds `parisDayStart` and `parisDayAfter`, the instants a day of Paris starts and ends. `dayParamOf` and the two Paris day bounds moved here from `src/ticket-archives/` with #393, and `textParamOf`, `FilterFields`, `filterChipsOf` and `formatDay` are new, for the other logs of #381 to read their period and their text the same way. `useSuccessToast`, beside them, is no component: it is the hook through which a form's leaf adds the toast of a write that lands (**Forms**, #227). `frenchNumber`, in `french-number.ts`, is no component either: it writes a number as French does, with a narrow no-break space between the thousands, for the blocked words' counts and the list of accounts' ranks, and it moved there from `src/blocked-words/french-text.ts` when #322 needed it in a second domain. `Pagination`, in `pagination.tsx`, with `pageNumbersOf` in `page-numbers.ts`, are the page navigation of the list of accounts moved there from `src/records/` when #385 needed it for a second page: they take a `label` for the `<nav>`, a `hrefOf` that gives the address of a page, the current page and the number of pages, which `AccountPagination` and `SupportTicketArchives` each fill (#385). The audit log's page is built from components of `src/ui/` that know nothing of it, so that the game log and the Bot log take the same template (#392): `LogPage` (the page's `<main>`, in the section crossfade, with its `overline`, `title` and `description`, and its `skeleton`), `LogFrame` (the `filters`, then the days and the pagination), `LogDay` (a `date` and its rows), `LogRow` (a `date`, an `Icon` with the `tileClassName` of its tile, a `trailing` cell that it places itself, and its body as children), `LogPagination` (a `label`, the `oldest` date, a `newestHref` and an `olderHref`, each `null` where it leads nowhere), `LogEmpty` (a `message` and a `resetHref`) and `LogSkeleton` (`loading`, `pillWidths`, which may be empty, the `form` skeleton and an optional `trailing` cell for each row); the type pills are `FilterPills`, the form is `FilterForm` (an `action`, `hiddenFields`, a `submitLabel` and its fields as children, with `FilterFormSkeleton` for its skeleton), and the fold under `md` is `FilterFold` (`FilterSheet`'s props and its `chips`). Their classes are in `log-layout.ts`, and `paris-day.tsx` holds `ParisDay` and `parisDayOf`.
- **Theme.** shadcn/ui's semantic variables (`background`, `foreground`, `primary`, `muted`, `destructive`, `border`, `ring`, and the rest), set in `:root` and mapped through `@theme inline` (https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/theming, read 2026-10-23). The panel's code uses those, never a palette color such as `zinc-100`, which review checks. Tailwind's default palette stays, since copied components use it, such as the Dialog's `bg-black/10`. The panel follows the system's light or dark setting, until a staff member chooses another through the theme toggle, which revisits #70's « no toggle and no JavaScript » (#221, #225). `ThemeToggle`, in `src/staff/`, is a group named « Thème » of three buttons, « Auto », « Clair » and « Sombre », each after its lucide icon, `Monitor`, `Sun` and `Moon`, `aria-hidden` and, while the text shows, marked `data-icon="inline-start"` (**Buttons**), the theme chosen marked `aria-pressed`, on `muted`, the pressed one on `card` inside a border in `primary`. `card` on `muted` gives 1.17:1 in light and 1.11:1 in dark, under the 3:1 that 1.4.11 asks of what shows a control's state, since the pressed choice sits next to the others (https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/non-text-contrast.html, read 2026-10-29), where `primary` reaches 4.80:1 and 8.52:1 on `muted`, and 5.63:1 and 9.48:1 on `card`, while `input` gives 2.83:1 on `muted` in light (the measure of `src/ui/globals.test.ts`, run on a scratch copy, 2026-10-29): the mockup draws no such border (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #225, for Gilles to review). The buttons are plain `<button>`s rather than shadcn/ui's Toggle Group (https://ui.shadcn.com/r/styles/base-nova/toggle-group.json, read 2026-10-29), whose Base UI primitive empties its value when the pressed toggle is clicked again (`node_modules/@base-ui/react/toggle-group/ToggleGroup.js:60`), which a choice among three must refuse, and keeps a value of its own, where `ThemeToggle` renders from its props (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #225, for Gilles to review). The toggle sits in the sidebar's foot, above the member (M:115-119), in the Sheet's footer, and at the right of the login pages' top bar, across from « Shor » (#236), through `PublicPageFrame`, which the privacy policy shares (#186), with its icons alone and their names in `sr-only` (M:40-44); in the sidebar's foot it takes `rail` (#356): below `lg`, its three icons stack in a column, each 44 px, their names in `sr-only`, and from `lg` it is the row of three labelled buttons, the one markup serving both, as a toggle with no `rail` is what it was (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #356, for Gilles to review); and the server renders it on « Auto », which the browser corrects once it hydrates, since the server cannot read the choice. The choice lives in `localStorage`, under `shor-panel-theme`, rather than in a cookie, which the server would read and so break the pages' static shell (**Caching**); « Auto » removes the key and hands the page back to `prefers-color-scheme`. A theme chosen sets `data-theme` on `<html>`, `light` or `dark`, and `globals.css` holds, after the dark values, `:root[data-theme="light"]` and `:root[data-theme="dark"]`, copies of the light `:root` and of the dark one, under `color-scheme: light` and `color-scheme: dark`, which the theme test keeps identical to them (**The theme**), rather than `light-dark()`, which Tailwind 4.3.3 leaves as it is though it targets Chrome 111 and Safari 16.4 (`node_modules/.pnpm/@tailwindcss+node@4.3.3/node_modules/@tailwindcss/node/dist/index.js`, #225). The `dark` variant is the `@custom-variant dark` of `globals.css`, which holds under `[data-theme="dark"]`, and under `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` unless `data-theme` is `light`, so the `dark:` classes of the copied components follow the choice too; the dark values sit in a `:root` under that query; and `:root` declares `color-scheme: light dark`, so that the browser draws its own controls, such as scrollbars, in the same scheme (inferred, not checked against the CSS specification). The build puts each `dark:` class under `:where([data-theme=dark],[data-theme=dark] *)`, then under `@media (prefers-color-scheme:dark)` with `:where(:root:not([data-theme=light]),…)` (`.next/static/chunks/*.css`, 2026-10-29). The root layout's `<head>` holds a `<script>` whose text, `APPLY_STORED_THEME` from `src/ui/theme.ts`, copies the stored choice into `data-theme`, so the browser runs it before it reads the body, and a theme chosen shows from the first frame; `<html>` takes `suppressHydrationWarning`, since `data-theme` is set before React hydrates: without it, a throwaway run of `e2e/theme.test.ts` under `next dev` logged « A tree hydrated but some attributes of the server rendered HTML didn't match the client properties » twice, and with it no line about hydration, beside Next.js's own warning about server caches (2026-10-29), while under `next start`, where end to end fails on any console warning (**The browser's console**), React reports no attribute that differs: of Next.js's React DOM builds, only `react-dom-client.development.js` and `react-dom-profiling.development.js` hold that message (`grep` over `node_modules/next/dist/compiled/react-dom/cjs/`, 2026-10-29), so end to end cannot see it, and that run under `next dev` is the proof. React writes a script's text as it is, escaping `</script` alone (`renderToStaticMarkup`, 2026-10-29), so the script needs no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, which `react/no-danger` refuses, and the Content Security Policy runs it through its `'unsafe-inline'` (ADR 0006). #225 planned `next/script`'s `beforeInteractive` instead, whose App Router version renders, in the body, only a `self.__next_s.push(…)` that Next.js's bootstrap, an `async` chunk, runs before it hydrates (`node_modules/next/dist/client/script.js:274-292`, `node_modules/next/dist/client/app-bootstrap.js:23-49`): with « Sombre » stored, over 10 loads of `/login/logged-out`, `data-theme` was still unset at `DOMContentLoaded` in all 10 and at the first animation frame in 9, against none of 10 with the head's script (a throwaway Playwright probe, 2026-10-29), and the reload of `e2e/theme.test.ts` fails on it (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #225, for Gilles to review). The palette is Hydromel (#221, #222), which replaced « parchemin et cuir », the logo's brown and a parchment that #129 and #132 had set: its values are those of the mockup's handoff, `etape-1-palette/src/ui/globals.css` (#221), for the variables the panel already had, and none of the variables that file adds. Its neutrals, the surfaces, `border`, `input`, `foreground` and `muted-foreground`, with their `sidebar-*` counterparts, sit at 60° to 85° in oklch, with a chroma of 0.005 to 0.035: in light, the surfaces and `border` at 78°, but `sidebar-accent` at 70°, like `input` and `muted-foreground`, and `foreground` at 60°; in dark, the surfaces at 60°, but `sidebar-accent` at 68°, `input` at 65°, `muted-foreground` at 70°, the borders at 80° and `foreground` at 85°. `card` and `popover` stand out from `background`: `oklch(0.994 0.005 78)` against `oklch(0.975 0.01 78)` in light, and `oklch(0.205 0.017 60)` against `oklch(0.165 0.014 60)` in dark. `primary` is amber, `oklch(0.52 0.13 60)` in light, `#9d5300`, with `oklch(0.99 0.01 80)` as its `primary-foreground`, and `oklch(0.8 0.13 78)` in dark, `#ebb353`, with a dark brown, `oklch(0.22 0.04 60)`. The text on `secondary`, `accent` and `sidebar-accent` is `foreground`, and the borders in dark are a parchment, `oklch(0.9 0.035 80)`, at 10%. `sidebar-primary` and `sidebar-ring` follow `primary` and `ring`. `--success` joins the semantic variables, a green that nods to the site's sage, which drew the message of a write that lands until that message went into a toast, whose copied check takes no color (**Forms**, #227), and which now draws the dot of a member's standing badge (#232) and the diamond of a lifting in a record's history (#233), and `destructive`, which draws the diamond of a kick or a ban there, is warmed toward orange, both at 4.5:1 or more on `background` (**Accessibility**). `--warning` joins them with #232, with `--color-warning` in `@theme inline`, an amber for a timeout, in the standing badge of one that runs and in a record's history (#233), `oklch(0.55 0.13 65)` in light, the handoff's step 1 darkened until it holds 4.5:1 on `background` and `card`, where its `oklch(0.58 0.13 65)` gave 4.12:1 and 4.35:1, and `oklch(0.8 0.11 78)` in dark, the step's own value (#221, #232). It is a color, not the **Warning** of `GLOSSARY.md`, the sanction, which it does not draw (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #232, for Gilles to review, since #221 names the token). `--destructive-foreground` joins them with #234, with `--color-destructive-foreground` in `@theme inline`, at the mockup's `--danger-fg`, `oklch(0.98 0.01 60)` in light and `oklch(0.17 0.03 35)` in dark (#221, #234), in the four blocks of `globals.css`: the text of the red buttons, « Expulser » and « Bannir » (**Buttons**). A record's card « Sanctions » draws two notices (**Forms**): a running timeout's, bordered in `warning/40` on a `warning/6` tint, its `Timer` in `warning`, and a banned account's, bordered in `destructive/40` on a `destructive/10` tint, its `ShieldBan` in `destructive`, both icons `aria-hidden` and their text in `foreground`, as #234 asks of the first. `SanctionNotice`, in `src/sanctions/`, draws both from one table keyed by the sanction's kind, `timeout` or `ban`, which gives each its icon and its classes, so that no tone of `src/sanctions/` bears the name of the glossary's **Warning**. The timeout's tint is `warning/6` where the mockup's is 10% (M:488), since the lift's reason sits in that notice: on `warning/10` over `card`, the field's border, `input`, gave 2.91:1 in light, under the 3:1 of 1.4.11, and 3.07:1 on `warning/6`; and the field keeps `card` as its fill in both schemes, since its dark `input/30` over the tint left `input` at 2.73:1 (a scratch port of the measure of `src/ui/globals.test.ts`, 2026-10-29) (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #234, for Gilles to review). The standing badge is `StandingBadge`, in `src/records/`, which the list draws beside each account and a record's header beside the account's name (#233): a pill bordered in `border`, its text in `foreground`, after a dot, `aria-hidden` since the text says the standing, in `success` for « Membre du serveur », `destructive` for « Compte banni » and `warning` for « Exclu jusqu’au <date> », whose date `ParisTime` renders with its year, where the mockup leaves it out (**Dates**, #232), and `muted-foreground` for « Absent du serveur », where the mockup's dot takes `--fg3` (M:804), which #221's table maps to `muted-foreground` (#233), one `lookOf` giving each standing its tone, which a `cva` turns into the dot's color, and its label. « Exclu jusqu’au <date> » is #232's own wording, which stands though `GLOSSARY.md` avoids « exclusion » alone for a timeout: it avoids the word with no end, which could name a kick, and this label names the end (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #232, for Gilles to review, with no change to `GLOSSARY.md`). It takes an `AccountStanding`, in `src/records/account-standing.ts`, a member with the end of a timeout that runs, or `null`, a banned account, or an account absent from the Discord server, the shape of a record's `Standing`, which a record's header passes as it is (#233). The list holds no absent account, which only its ID or its mention reaches, and they lead straight to its record (#232), so only a record's header shows that standing (#233). `FoundAccount` carries it as `standing`, in place of the `banned` it held, a member's read from the `communication_disabled_until` that the members' list returns, through `timeoutEndOf`, which a record's standing reads too (the union decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #232, for Gilles to review). The search's field keeps its label « Compte », which the mockup drops, and takes the placeholder « Nom, pseudo, ID ou mention », after a `Search` drawn in the field, `aria-hidden` (#232, #324). The form that holds it, `AccountSearchForm`, is the page's one: a GET `next/form` with `prefetch={false}` to `/records`, whose field is followed by six native `<select>`s, each under a visible `Label`, « Rôle », « État », « Arrivée sur le serveur », « Âge du compte Discord », « Comptes de bot » and « Trier par », then by « Chercher », which sends no page and so leads to the first (#324). Under `md`, the six choices are `hidden`, kept in the form, so that a search sent from the page keeps the filters set, and « Filtres » and « Chercher » share a row in two equal columns (#360). « Filtres » is a `FilterSheetTrigger`, which counts the choices set, the search left out, in a pill, and in words for assistive technologies, « 3 filtres actifs ». It opens a Sheet from the bottom under `sm` and from the right from `sm`, 384 px at most, through the classes that `FilterSheetContent` takes for `rightFromSm`, important overrides of the bottom Sheet's own, which leave the copied Sheet as the CLI wrote it (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #360, for Gilles to review). The Sheet holds a GET form of its own to `/records`, `AccountFilterSheetForm`: a hidden `query` with the search text of the URL, always sent, even empty, as the inline field sends it, then the six choices, under ids of their own, `account-sheet-*`, « Réinitialiser », a link to the list with the search and no choice, and « Appliquer », its submit, so that it gives the address the inline form gives for the same choices, each parameter once. A search typed and not sent is lost when the Sheet is sent, since its hidden `query` is the URL's (Gilles, 2026-11-03, on #360). « Réinitialiser » keeps the search where the audit log's clears everything, since that page's form has no field to keep, and it resets the six choices, the sort included, which #360 lists among the Sheet's six (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #360, for Gilles to review). The root of the `<Sheet>` wraps the page's `<Form>`, whose row holds the trigger, and the Sheet's content is a sibling of that `<Form>`, not a child of it: React's synthetic `submit` bubbles through a portal to the React ancestors, so a Sheet form inside the page's would also run the page's `onSubmit` (**Gotchas**). The root is keyed by the address `accountListHref` writes, as the form was. The choices set show under the search, under `md`, as chips, `FilterChips`: each a link to `accountListHref` without that choice, on its first page, named « Retirer le filtre État : Membres » from the field's label then the option's, `choiceChipsOf`, in `src/records/account-choices.ts`, which also holds the six choices, so that the inline form, the Sheet and the chips read them from one place (#360; the labels are the fields', where a shorter name was not needed, since the longest chip, « Âge du compte Discord : Moins de 30 jours », fits a 360 px screen in a Playwright screenshot of 2026-11-03; decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #360, for Gilles to review, since #360 names « État : Membres » alone). The parameters are `query`, `role`, a role's ID or `none`, `standing`, `members`, `timed-out` or `banned`, `joined`, `7d`, `30d`, `6m` or `over-1y`, `created`, `7d` or `30d`, `bots`, `shown`, `sort`, `joined-recent`, `joined-old` or `role`, and `page`, in English since a URL counts as a file name (**Language**), and a value that is none of those, a repeated one, a role that the Discord server does not list, `@everyone` included, and a page past the last count as absent (**Validation**). Each select opens on the value of the URL, through `defaultValue`, and the `<Sheet>` that wraps the form is keyed by the address that `accountListHref` writes for its filters, as the blocked words' form is keyed by its query, whose field kept « xyz » across a navigation without its key (**Forms**), so that a move to other filters shows their values; that the browser's Back button needs the key too is inferred, not tried. The first choice of each, « Tous les rôles », « Tous les comptes », « Toutes les dates », « Tous les âges », « Masqués » and « Nom », has the empty value and stands for no filter: `next/form` writes every control it holds into the URL (`node_modules/next/dist/client/form-shared.js:77-90`), so a submit sends `role=` and the five others empty, which counts as absent, as the audit log's form does with `author=` (#321), while the links write the filters that are set alone, in the order `query`, `role`, `standing`, `joined`, `created`, `bots`, `sort`, then `page`. #324 asks that neither the form nor the links write the first choice, which no native GET form can without client logic, a `<select>` sending its selected option whatever its value (inferred from HTML's form submission), against **Components** and **Framework** (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #324, for Gilles to review). The role choice lists « Tous les rôles », « Aucun rôle » and then each role of the Discord server but `@everyone`, from the highest, through `rolesByHierarchy`, where #324 lists « Aucun rôle » after the roles, which with some forty roles leaves it at the foot of a long list (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #324, for Gilles to review). The choices take a basis of 11 rem from `md` up, where they come back, and grow, so that three share a row at 768 px and four at 1,280 px, the last ones sharing a row with the button, and under `md` the button takes its half of the row beside « Filtres » (`e2e/account-filters.test.ts`, #324, #360). « Comptes de bot » hides every account that Discord flags `bot`, the panel's own application among them, until « Affichés », `bots=shown`: these are the glossary's **Bot account**, not its **Bot**, which is Shor's alone, so the label says « Comptes de bot » where #324 writes « Bots », the code names them `botAccount` and `isBotAccount`, and the URL keeps `bots` (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #324, for Gilles to review, with the entry **Bot account** added to `GLOSSARY.md`). The accounts listed sit on `card`, one row each, `accent` on hover as the mockup's hover maps (#221): the avatar, the name, `@username` in a monospace font, the badges, which go under them under `sm`, then a `ChevronRight`, `aria-hidden`.
