A member's highest role shows in a `RoleBadge`, in `src/records/`, in place of « Membre du serveur » (#323). It has `StandingBadge`'s shape, which both take from `BADGE_CLASSES` and `BADGE_DOT_CLASSES`, in `src/records/badge-classes.ts`: a pill bordered in `border`, its text, the role's name, in `foreground` and wrapped where it does not fit, so that a long name stays in its row, after a dot, `aria-hidden` since the text names the role. The dot takes the role's `colors.primary_color` written as `#rrggbb`, through a `style` rather than a class, since Tailwind reads no class built at run time (**Styles**) and the Content Security Policy allows inline styles (**Headers**), and the neutral `muted-foreground`, as « Absent du serveur » does, when that color is 0, which Discord documents as a role with no color (https://docs.discord.com/developers/topics/permissions, read 2026-11-02; the neutral dot is #323's proposal). It ignores `secondary_color` and `tertiary_color`, so a gradient or holographic role shows its primary color. A role whose color is close to `card` leaves a dot that is hard to see, with nothing hidden, since the text names the role (inferred). `rolesByHierarchy`, in `src/records/role-hierarchy.ts`, ranks roles from the highest to the lowest by `position` and, for two of the same position, by the lower ID first: Discord's documentation says only that « roles with the same position are sorted by id » (the same page), and discord.js's `Role#position` counts the role with the lower ID as the higher (`packages/discord.js/src/structures/Role.js`, read 2026-11-02), which is inferred to be Discord's own order, since two roles of one position were not tried on the test Discord server (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #323, for Gilles to review). `highestRoleOf` takes the first role of that order that a member holds, so a member none of whose roles the Discord server lists has no highest role. It filters `@everyone` out of nothing: it relies on Discord not putting `@everyone`, whose ID is the Discord server's, in a member's `roles` (inferred, not tried), so a member whose `roles` held that ID would show an « @everyone » badge. The roles of a record's card « Détails » go through `rolesByHierarchy` too, where they were sorted by `position` alone and kept the order Discord listed two roles of one position in; #324's role filter and its sort by highest role reuse it (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #323, for Gilles to review). A row shows the role alone for a member who holds one and is not timed out, and the role then « Exclu jusqu’au <date> », side by side, for one who is timed out, while a member with no role, a timed-out one included, and a banned account keep `StandingBadge`, « Membre du serveur », the end of the timeout alone or « Compte banni »; `StandingBadge` and a record's header do not change. `FoundAccount` carries `highestRole`, null for a banned account and for a member with no role. The rows of a search by name show the role too, since #324 folds that search into the list (they kept « Membre du serveur » until then, which the agent decided on 2026-11-02, on #323, for Gilles to review, as #323 proposes). The rows, which `AccountRows` draws, list every account that the search and the filters keep, members and banned accounts mixed, 25 a page (#322, #324). `AccountList`, in `src/records/`, draws what `listAccounts` gave, and `AccountSearchForm` the choices, so that the page only wires the staff check, the roles and the two reads, and redirects for an ID or a mention, while what is shown renders for a test (**Entry points only wire**); `AccountsView`, which picked between the search's results and the list, went with those results (#324), and so did the count « 3 comptes commencent par « ly » », the capped list, « Plus de 25 comptes… », its sentence « Seuls les 25 premiers comptes s’affichent : précise ta recherche. » and « Aucun membre ni banni dont le nom commence par « … ». ». The list keeps no account for a search or a filter in an `EmptyListFrame`, a dashed box bordered in `input`, as the mockup draws it (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #232, for Gilles to review, since #232 gives the count's wording alone). By default they run by the name they show, a member's nickname, else global name, else username, a banned account's global name, else username, whatever its case but not its accents, through `Intl.Collator("fr-FR", { sensitivity: "accent" })` where a comparison of UTF-16 units would put « Éclair » after « Zed », then by ID, so that two accounts of one name keep their page from one view to the next (#322; the collator decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #322, for Gilles to review, since #322 says only « sans tenir compte de la casse »). « Arrivée récente » and « Arrivée ancienne » run by `joined_at`, the latest or the earliest first, and put after the others, by name, the banned accounts, which have none, and the members whose `joined_at` is null, which Discord allows (#324, for the end of those sorts, which #324 proposes). « Rôle le plus haut » runs by the rank, in `rolesByHierarchy`, of the highest role a member holds, then puts the members who hold no role the Discord server lists after them, then the banned accounts, each group by name (#324). Two accounts a sort does not tell apart keep the name, then the ID, so that the pages hold from one view to the next (#324). The search and the filters keep the accounts that pass all of them, in AND: the text starts the username, the global name or the nickname of a member, or the username or the global name of a banned account, whatever the case, through `toLocaleLowerCase()`, as the search of the banned accounts did (#324); a role keeps the members who hold it among all theirs, « Aucun rôle » the members who hold no role the Discord server lists, the ones whose rows show no role badge, and either drops the banned accounts, as a filter of arrival does; « Membres » keeps every member, timed out or not, since a timed-out member remains one (`GLOSSARY.md`, **Member**; #324's proposal), « Exclus en ce moment » those whose timeout runs and « Bannis » the banned accounts; the arrival, from `joined_at`, keeps the members who joined in the last 7 days or 30 days, or since the same date 6 calendar months earlier, the bound included, or before the same date a year earlier, the bound left out, a day the earlier month lacks becoming its last day, and drops a member with no `joined_at`; the age of the Discord account is read in the ID, as `snowflakeDate` does, and keeps those created less than 7 or 30 days ago, as « moins de 7 jours » says, the bound left out where the windows of arrival keep theirs, a banned account too; bot accounts are left out unless asked for (#324). « Exclus en ce moment » is #324's own wording, which stands though `GLOSSARY.md` gives **Timeout** « _Avoid_: mute (Discord's voice mute), exclusion (alone) » and warns under **Kick** that « exclusion » is, in French, Discord's word for a timeout: the label names the standing of a member whose timeout runs, as « Exclu jusqu’au <date> » does (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #324, for Gilles to review, with no change to `GLOSSARY.md`). Those bounds count in UTC, while the panel shows Paris time, so that a member who joined a few hours after a bound in Paris time may sit on either side of it (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #324, for Gilles to review, since #324 leaves the bounds to tests at them, `src/records/list-accounts-filters.test.ts` and `months-ago.test.ts`). The list's count, the overline « 26–50 sur 312 », names it: the ranks of the first and the last account of the page, an en dash between them, then the total, the numbers through `frenchNumber`, so that a page of one account gives its rank alone, « 312 sur 312 » (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #322, for Gilles to review). « Heures de Paris. » follows the rows once, through `ParisHoursNote`. Under them, `AccountPagination`, which gives the `Pagination` of `src/ui/` its label and its addresses (#385), draws a `<nav>` « Pages des comptes », none for one page: « Page précédente » and « Page suivante » in `outline` with `ArrowLeft` and `ArrowRight`, as the audit log's, each only where it leads somewhere, and between them the page numbers, each a `TextLink` with `sr-only` text « Page » before its number and 24 px at least each way, since 2.5.8 of **Accessibility** asks it, and the current page, which is no link, with `aria-current="page"` in `font-semibold`. Past 7 pages, only the first, the last, the current one and its neighbours show, with a « … » hidden from assistive technologies where two pages or more are left out and the page's number where a single one is (`pageNumbersOf`), so that no page shows more than 7 entries (the `sr-only` text, the 24 px and the page shown in place of a single one decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #322, for Gilles to review). Under `sm`, a second list of numbers replaces that one, from `pageNumbersOf(page, pages, { compact: true })`: every page up to 5, then the first, the last, the current one and its neighbours, a gap hiding a single page where the full list shows it, so that no more than 5 numbers show, each a `TextLink` of 44 px at least on a touch screen, `touch:min-w-11` beside the `touch:min-h-11` that `TextLink` has. Both lists are in the DOM, one `hidden` at each width, with the same links. The two arrows become squares of 44 px, their text `sr-only`, on one row with the numbers, and where there is no page to go to, an empty `aria-hidden` square keeps the arrow's place, so that the numbers do not move (#360; a gap that hides a single page, where the full list shows it, and that empty square are decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #360, for Gilles to review). At 360 px the column is 320 px wide and 5 numbers and 2 arrows at 44 px take 308 of it, so a « … » takes no width there: its `<li>` is 0 px wide and takes `pointer-events-none`, and its glyph, centered on it, sits on the boundary of the two numbers it separates and overlaps the edge of the number before it by a few pixels, which `e2e/account-list.test.ts` reads with `elementFromPoint` on the center and the four edges of each target at 360 px: it failed on a throwaway build without `pointer-events-none` (`pnpm test:e2e e2e/account-list.test.ts -g "past 7 pages"`, 2026-11-03) (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #360, for Gilles to review, since #360 asks for the first page, the current one, its neighbours and the last in one row of 44 px numbers, which leaves 12 px for two gaps; #360 and `docs/responsive.md` write `{ siblings: 1, compact: true }`, and `siblings` has no other value, so only `compact` stays). An empty list says « Aucun compte ne correspond à ta recherche. » in an `EmptyListFrame`, whatever hid the accounts, the bot accounts hidden by default included, in place of « Aucun compte à afficher. » and of the search's sentence (#322, #324; the one sentence decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #324, for Gilles to review, since #324 says « par exemple »). The page goes in the URL beside the search, the filters and the sort, `/records?page=2`, none for the first, and an invalid one counts as absent, so that a page that is no integer, is below 1, comes twice or lies past the last gives the first; the links of the pagination keep the rest, through `accountListHref`, and the form sends no page (#322, #324). The avatar keeps `MemberAvatar`'s 32 px, where the mockup draws 38 (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #232, for Gilles to review). The ring sits just over 3:1 at the 50% the copied classes draw it at, as #129 had asked of « parchemin et cuir »'s (« juste assez pour tenir 3:1 à 50 % »), though nothing says the mockup chose Hydromel's for that reason: in light, a dark brown, `oklch(0.26 0.06 58)`, where `primary` at 50% would give 2.13:1 on `background`, under 3:1; in dark, an amber a little less saturated than `primary`, `oklch(0.8 0.12 78)`, where `primary` at 50% gives 3.31:1 (a scratch port of the measure of `src/ui/globals.test.ts`, run on `src/ui/globals.css`, 2026-10-29). `--radius` is `0.25rem`, where `nova`'s was `0.625rem`. The `chart-*` variables, which nothing uses, keep `nova`'s grays. The site's forest green and sage stay the site's. #129's refusal of decoration is lifted for letter-spaced overlines and the rule with a diamond, and holds for a paper texture and the site's illustration (#221, #224): an overline is real text, written in normal case and set in capitals by `uppercase`, in `muted-foreground`, and the rule is `aria-hidden`. `Overline` and `OrnamentRule`, in `src/ui/` since the sidebar and the moderation section both draw them (#232), set the overline and draw the rule, which under the header of `/records`, the overline « Fiches des comptes », its `h1` and its sentence, takes `leadingLine`, a 24 px line before the diamond in `ornament`, then a line in `border` across the column, as the mockup does (#232), and so does a record's header, under the account's name (#233), and the header of `/blocked-words`, the overline « AutoMod · règles du panel », its `h1`, its sentence and the capacity gauge (#228, #325, where « règles » took the plural), and that of `/audit-log`, its overline, its `h1` and « Qui a changé quoi, et quand : les mots interdits, les sanctions et leurs levées, depuis le panel comme depuis Discord. » (#230), and so does a login page, under the overline « Panel du staff · Skyrim Roleplay », its `h1` and its sentence, above its buttons (#236). `PageHeading`, in `src/ui/`, sets the `h1` of those three headers in the headings' font at 38 px from `sm` and 32 px under it (#357), and `PageHeader`, there too, the overline, if any, the `h1` and the sentence of `/records`, of `/blocked-words`, of `/audit-log` (#230) and of a record that names no account, where each wrote its classes out before #228 (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #228, for Gilles to review, since the comments of #232 and #233 on #228 only proposed it). The sidebar's group titles take `Overline` as the pages' overlines do, at 11 px since #357, where they were 10 px and the pages' 10.5 px, which the mockup draws (#224, #232). `--ornament` colors the rule's diamond, with `--color-ornament` in `@theme inline`, at the values of the handoff's step 1, `oklch(0.5 0.09 35)` in light and `oklch(0.72 0.1 50)` in dark (#221, #224). The logo is `ShorLogo`, in `src/staff/` while the sidebar, the bar, the login pages and the privacy policy alone show it: one SVG holding the shapes of Shor's black logo, `Noir.svg` on #129, with `fill="currentColor"`, colored `primary`, so amber since #222, and `aria-hidden`, 28 by 30 px as the mockup draws it (#224), before « Shor » and « Panel du staff » in their link to `/` (**Navigation**), « Shor » in the headings' font (#133, #135, #224). The login pages draw it twice, 22 by 24 px before « Shor » in their top bar, which is no link, as in the mockup (M:37-40), and 44 by 48 px above their overline (M:48, #236). The favicon, `src/app/icon.svg`, which Next.js links as `<link rel="icon">` from the pages under the root layout (inferred from its file convention; end to end reads it on `/blocked-words` and `/login/logged-out`), is, until the POs give a smaller mark (#74), the whole logo in dark's `primary`, `#ebb353`, on a tile of light's `primary`, `#9d5300`, with rounded corners (#222), at 3.03:1, where « parchemin et cuir »'s `#efe3cf` on `#423020` gave 9.88:1 (WCAG's formula on those hex values, 2026-10-29): the whole logo reads at 32 px but not at 16 px (Playwright render, 2026-10-27, and again in Hydromel's colors, 2026-10-29), where a crop of its sword's pommel stays sharp (the first render). Its colors are fixed, since Next.js serves the file as it is, outside the theme's variables (#135), and `src/ui/globals.test.ts` holds them to light's and dark's `primary` (#132). Tailwind's preflight is the reset.
- **Fonts and icons.** Text uses the system's sans-serif stack, but for an account's `@username` in the list of accounts and in a record's header, its ID in the record's details, a blocked word in its tile and the keywords of another AutoMod rule (**Forms**), the time of each change in the audit log, the count of blocked words in the navigation (**Navigation**), the counts of the request kinds in the support tickets' pills (#386) and the reference of an error on `error.tsx`'s page (**Error pages**), and the session cookie's name on the privacy policy (#186), in Tailwind's `font-mono`, the system's monospace stack, as the mockup draws them, but for the reference, which #61 sets (#232, #233, #228, #230, #226). Headings, `h1` and `h2`, and the « Shor » of the panel's link and of the login pages' top bar use EB Garamond (#129, #133, #224, #236), as the error pages' do (#61), through the `font-heading` class, which `--font-heading` sets in the `@theme` of `src/ui/globals.css`, with Tailwind's `--font-serif` behind it. `globals.css` imports `@fontsource/eb-garamond/latin-500.css` (5.3.0, OFL-1.1, `npm view`, 2026-10-27), one face: the weight the headings use, 500, in the latin subset, whose range holds French's letters but Ÿ, the U+00A0, ’, « » and … (`node_modules/@fontsource/eb-garamond/500.css:68`). It declares no `unicode-range` (`latin-500.css`), so the browser takes its face for any character and falls back to `--font-serif` for one it lacks (inferred from CSS's font matching). That stack, `ui-serif` then Georgia (`node_modules/tailwindcss/theme.css:5`), also shows the headings while the font loads, under the package's `font-display: swap` (`latin-500.css:5`; inferred from swap's definition): Georgia stays set aside as the headings' font, not as their fallback. A heading in another weight would need that weight's file. The initials of a record's avatar, 68 px and 56 px under `sm`, at 28 px and 23 px, the second in proportion to the avatar (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #361, for Gilles to review), take it too, from the same face, as the mockup draws them (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #233, for Gilles to review, since #233 gives only the avatar's size and ring), and so does the count of the blocked words' capacity gauge (#228). `@tailwindcss/postcss` rewrites the `url()`s of a file it imports, through its `transformAssetUrls` option, on by default (`node_modules/.pnpm/@tailwindcss+postcss@4.3.3/node_modules/@tailwindcss/postcss/dist/index.js:10`), and the build serves the font from `/_next/static/media/` (`.next/static/media/`, 2026-10-27). The package installs from the npm registry, so no font service is called, neither at build nor in the browser, and `font-src 'self'` holds (**Headers**). Its OFL-1.1 notice stays out of `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`, like those of the other npm dependencies, which the repository does not copy: the font names its copyright and the OFL's URL in its own metadata, `name` IDs 0 and 14 (read on the package's `.woff`, 2026-10-27; the `.woff2` the build serves is inferred to match) (Gilles, 2026-10-27, on #133). The system's Georgia, which Shor's site uses, and Cinzel, Spectral and Fraunces were set aside. A font `shadcn init` adds is removed. Icons come from `lucide-react`, shadcn/ui's default, with the copied components that carry them, and on the panel's own buttons, beside their French text, which stays their name (**Buttons**). A control without visible text has a French `sr-only` text, but for the Toast's « Fermer », an `aria-label` as the CLI wrote it (**Copied code**, #227).
- **Small screens.** Every page works from 360 px wide, in one column, with no horizontal scroll: under `lg`, 1,024 px (`--breakpoint-lg: 64rem`, `node_modules/tailwindcss/theme.css:329`), the sidebar gives way to a rail of 64 px, and under `md`, 768 px (`--breakpoint-md: 48rem`, `node_modules/tailwindcss/theme.css:328`), the rail gives way to a bar that sticks to the top of the page, and its menu (**Navigation**), and under `sm`, 640 px (`--breakpoint-sm: 40rem`, `node_modules/tailwindcss/theme.css:327`), the rows of the audit log, which stacked their time, what changed and their staff member beside the tile of their icon (#230), put the tile, the time and the staff member on a first line, with the staff member's avatar after their name at the row's right edge, as from `sm` up, where the stack put it before the name, and what changed under that line, 10 px lower, where the stack's rows were 4 px apart (#319; the avatar's side and the 10 px were decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #319, for Gilles to review, since the ticket says only that what changed goes under), and an account's badges, its highest role and its standing, go under its name, the second wrapping under the first where they do not fit on one line, as at 360 px for a timed-out member who has a role (#232, #323), a record's avatar takes 56 px and each term of its details stacks above its value (#361), and the choices of the form of the list of accounts fold behind « Filtres », a Sheet, and show as chips under the search (#324, #360).
- **Navigation.** Every page the staff check guards (**The staff check**) has the panel's navigation, a sidebar from `lg` up, a rail of 64 px from `md` to `lg` and a bar below `md` (#224, #356), in a layout below the root one, so that `error.tsx`, `global-error.tsx` and the not-found page of a URL that no route matches, which Next.js renders under the root layout, show none, while the not-found page that a `notFound()` of a page of that layout shows renders inside it, beside the navigation (**Error pages**, #61). It holds Shor's logo, « Shor » and « Panel du staff », a link to `/`; one link per section the member can open, « Modération » only for those who hold a role of `MODERATION_ROLE_IDS` (ADR 0005, #176), the current one with `aria-current="page"`, on its own path and on every path below it, such as a record under `/records`; the member's Discord avatar and name (#140), and under the name « Staff », or « Staff · Modération » for those who moderate (#221, #224); « Se déconnecter » (#71). `/` redirects to `/blocked-words` with a 307, not a permanent redirect, so a home page can come back. Tab titles follow `%s – Panel de Shor`, and the default stays « Panel d’administration de Shor », through the root layout's `metadata.title.template`. `src/app/(staff)/layout.tsx` renders the navigation beside the pages it guards, or above them under `md`, through `StatefulStaffHeader`, a client leaf of `src/staff/` that reads `usePathname`, since a layout receives no path and stays mounted across the navigations between sections (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/layout.md:240-242`); the member's avatar and name come from the layout's own `requireStaffMember`, under one `<Suspense>`, so a page loaded in full reads the member from Discord twice, once for the layout and once for the page, where ADR 0005 counts one, and a navigation between sections once. Both reads stay, with no request-scoped `cache()` to share them, until Discord answers `getMember` with a 429 (Gilles, 2026-10-27, on #71). The audit log's avatars add one `getMember` per distinct member of its page, up to 50 at once, and count toward that same 429, with no ticket of their own: they turn their own 429 into initials (**Avatars**), and whether they could leave the next staff check a 429 longer than the 2 s it waits (**Retries**) is inferred, not tried (Gilles, 2026-10-28, on #141). A record searches the archives channel at each view, when the configuration names one (#197, #387): the application's own user once more, with no request-scoped `cache()` to share it, then the guild message search (ADR 0010) for the application's messages in that channel whose text holds the account's ID, 25 a page, newest first, one search after another for as many pages as `total_results` says there are, from 1 to 4 (`MAX_CITING_ARCHIVES_READ`, 100 messages), and for each distinct closer of the account's archives a `getMember` and, when that member has left, a `getUser`, in parallel; it keeps no copy of any of it, not even a cache (ADR 0009). Until #387 it paged the channel's history, with from 1 call to 10 of `listMessages` and one more for a single message when the tenth page was full. The account's ID is a word of the archive in its « Ouvert par » field, in its « Fermé par » field and in its reason if a closer quotes it, and Discord's search cannot tell the fields apart (ADR 0010), so it finds a superset of the account's archives: `readTicketArchives` keeps those whose opener, as `ticketArchiveOf` reads it from the plain-text ID of that field, is the account, and shows one archive once if the search shifted between two of its pages (`src/ticket-archives/read-ticket-archives.test.ts`). An account cited in 25 archives or fewer costs one search, one of the burst that the search allows (**Retries**), and one that is cited in more costs up to 4, which meets a staff member who closed many tickets (inferred; the number of archives on Shor is not known). A record's history adds, at each view, one call to the audit log for each of the five kinds of entry it reads, and one more for each further 100 entries of that kind within Discord's 45 days, one after another, since no parameter filters the audit log by the account it targets (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/audit-log, read 2026-10-28; #177). The audit log's page makes, at each view, one call to the audit log for each of the seven kinds of entry it reads, the panel rules' two only once one of those rules exists, since the changes of every rule of the panel are kept in memory by the rule IDs the AutoMod rules give (#325), and AutoMod's timeouts, `AUTO_MODERATION_USER_COMMUNICATION_DISABLED`, always, since they name no rule of the panel and are read whichever rule gave them (#327), and one more for each further 100 entries of a kind, one after another, until it holds more than the page's 50 changes of that kind, or more than 100 entries of one of the panel's rules, whose change of action writes two that the page reads as one, or reaches Discord's 45 days (#182, #326). Filtered by an author, an account, a text or a period, it makes the same calls for the kinds it reads, but counts toward the page's 50 only the changes that meet the filters, so that a rare author, account or text, or a period far back, can have it read every page of Discord's 45 days for each kind, where an unfiltered page stops at 50 changes of a kind (#321, #393; inferred, not tried against Discord's own pages). A period reads from the newest entry, and one that ends before it starts or lies outside the last 45 days reads nothing (`.claude/rules/audit-log.md`). Filtered by a type, it makes those calls for that type's kinds alone, the two of the panel's rules, whichever and however many the Discord server holds, for « Mots interdits », `MEMBER_UPDATE` and `AUTO_MODERATION_USER_COMMUNICATION_DISABLED` for « Exclusions », `MEMBER_KICK` for « Expulsions », and `MEMBER_BAN_ADD` and `MEMBER_BAN_REMOVE` for « Bannissements », and reads the AutoMod rules, which give it the IDs of the panel's rules, only for « Tout » and « Mots interdits », as a filtered read that Discord answers for its type alone shows (`src/audit-log/read-audit-log.test.ts`, #231): so the AutoMod reader runs under « Tout » and « Exclusions » and is skipped under « Mots interdits », « Expulsions » and « Bannissements », which the same test shows, its Discord answering for a type's kinds alone (#327); an account chosen leaves out the panel rules' two kinds and that read too, since no change of a rule targets an account, so that « Mots interdits » under an account reads nothing, and keeps the AutoMod reader, since its entry names an account (`src/audit-log/read-audit-log.test.ts`, #321, #327). Each of those two reads also makes one call to `GET /users/@me`, `getApplicationUser`, since `attributionOf`, in `src/audit-log/entry-attribution.ts`, counts an entry as made through the panel only if its `user_id` is the application's and its reason follows the panel's format (#210): the audit log's page makes it beside its other reads, the record's history before them, and a record loaded in full reads it a second time for `readSanctionRefusals` (`src/sanctions/sanction-refusal.ts:44`), with no request-scoped `cache()` to share it, as for `getMember` (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #210, for Gilles to review). A failed read of it is thrown, as one of the audit log is (**Read failures**), since an application left unknown would have every change read as made in Discord. The record of a banned account reads the audit log too, since `getBan` returns a reason and a user and no author (`node_modules/discord-api-types/payloads/v10/guild.d.ts:954-963`): `readRecord` reads the `MEMBER_BAN_ADD` entries page by page, 100 at a time and one after another, the newest first as `auditLogPages` relies on, until the first one that targets the account, which is the ban in force (inferred), so it reads every page within Discord's 45 days only when that entry has left the audit log, and then makes one `getApplicationUser` for `attributionOf`, since a ban reads as given through the panel, with « Banni par » and its reason without the panel's prefix, only if that entry counts as the panel's (#285). A ban with no entry, or whose entry is not the panel's, a ban given by hand with a reason in the panel's format included, reads as given in Discord, with its whole reason and no « Banni par » (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #285, for Gilles to review, as the ticket's own decision for a ban whose entry has left the audit log). That read comes before the history's, in the same render, and the history reads the same `MEMBER_BAN_ADD` entries again, so a record of a banned account reads them twice at each view, once for the author and once for the history, and `getApplicationUser` twice too, once in each, since `readSanctionRefusals` is not read for it (`src/sanctions/sanction-options.ts:38-39`), with no request-scoped `cache()` to share them, as for `getMember`, until Discord answers with a 429 (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #285, for Gilles to review). A failed read of either is thrown (**Read failures**). The contract suite checks that the entry of an AutoMod rule's creation, of a timeout's setting, and of the ban of an account absent from the Discord server and of its lifting carry the application's `user_id` (`src/discord/discord-api-contract.ts:288`, `:662`, `:751`, `:765`); that a timeout's replacement and lifting, a member's ban and a kick carry it too is inferred, since no check reads their `user_id` (**The contract suite**). A view of `/records` reads every member of the Discord server, 1,000 a call, one call after another from the highest ID of the previous page, and every banned account the same way, and the Discord server's roles in one call, the three in parallel, after the staff check's `getMember`: one call each for the members and the bans under 1,000, and one more each for every further 1,000 (#322, #323, #324). The search, the filters and the sort run on what those calls gave, in memory, so that a search by name costs no call of its own, where it called `searchMembers` once. The staff check and the roles are read once, through two promises that the form's `<Suspense>` and the list's share, as the layout shares the member's: the page creates them, so that the form, which lists the roles, shows before the members are read, and `listAccounts` awaits the roles with the members and the bans, but answers an ID or a mention before it awaits the roles or reads the list, and goes straight to the record. The page has started the roles' call all the same, for the form, which shows until that redirect, so that every view reads the roles once, an ID or a mention included (#324). The one promise, in place of a second `listRoles` call for the form, was decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #324, for Gilles to review. The roles give each member their highest role, which the list shows in a badge and sorts by (**Theme**): #322 left the `listRoles` call out, since nothing showed the roles before (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #322, for Gilles to review, where #322 asks `listAccounts` to read them), and #323 adds it along with its consumer. Above 10,000 accounts, reading them all at each view is to be reviewed (#317). The count of blocked words beside « Mots interdits » adds one read of the AutoMod rules at each full load of a staff page and after each write, whose `refresh()` renders the layout again, as the badge that follows an add and a removal shows (`e2e/blocked-words.test.ts`), and none on a navigation between sections, so a full load of `/blocked-words` reads the rules twice, once for the layout and once for the page, with no request-scoped `cache()` to share them, as for `getMember`, while a write by another staff member leaves the count stale until the next full load (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #226). `/` redirects through `redirects()` of `next.config.ts`, which runs before `proxy.ts` (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/proxy.md:239-241`), and the panel's link is a plain `<a>`, since a `next/link`'s `fetch` of `/` would follow that redirect (**Gotchas**). From `lg` up, the navigation is a sidebar, an `<aside>`, so a `complementary` landmark, that sticks to the screen's left edge, 252 px wide and as tall as the screen, a left safe-area inset more (#356), on `sidebar`, in 14 px text, as the mockup draws it (`docs/design/hydromel/blocked-words-light.png`, and the mockup's lines that #224 cites). From top to bottom, it holds the panel's link, the logo before « Shor », letter-spaced capitals in the headings' font, over « Panel du staff », an overline, so the link's name reads « Shor Panel du staff »; « Panel du staff » is the brand's label, as #221 and #224 set it, not a name of the panel, which keeps the glossary's (**Admin Panel**, `GLOSSARY.md`) in prose and code (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #224, for Gilles to review); « Shor » is written so in the source and set in capitals by `uppercase`, as an overline is, where #224 writes « SHOR » (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #224, for Gilles to review); the rule, a diamond in `ornament` before a line in `border` (**Theme**); the `<nav>` « Sections du panel », whose sections sit in groups, up to four, each under an overline, a `<p>` rather than a heading, so that the pages' `h1` and `h2` stay their only headings (**Fonts and icons**), which names its list through `aria-labelledby`, by an `id` that names the sidebar or the Sheet, since both render the groups, so that `StaffHeader` still renders from its props alone, where a `useId` would not (#224): « Administration », with « Mots interdits », then « Journaux », with « Journal d’audit », which every staff member sees, then « Journal du jeu », which only a staff member who views the game log's channel on Discord sees (`.claude/rules/game-log.md`, #394), and which the Bot log joins (#381, #392), then « Modération », with « Modération », which a staff member who holds no role of `MODERATION_ROLE_IDS` does not see, title included, where the mockup shows it to all and names its link « Fiches des comptes » (#221, #224), then « Support », with « Tickets de support », which every staff member sees (#385), shown together with « Modération » once the member's roles are read, so that « Support » does not move down when « Modération » arrives (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #385, for Gilles to review); and its foot, the theme toggle (**Theme**, #225), then the member's avatar and name, then the `ghost` icon button `LogOut`, named « Se déconnecter » in `sr-only`, as #221 accepted, then « Confidentialité », `PrivacyPolicyLink`'s `next/link` to the privacy policy, which does not prefetch, in `text-xs` and `muted-foreground` (**Privacy policy**, #186). Each section carries a lucide icon, `aria-hidden`, `Ban` for « Mots interdits », `ScrollText` for « Journal d’audit », `Gavel` for « Modération » and `Ticket` for « Tickets de support ». « Mots interdits » ends with a badge, `BlockedWordCountBadge` in `src/blocked-words/`, the number of blocked words, whatever their action, 0 included, in `font-mono` at 11 px and in `muted-foreground`, as the mockup draws it (M:96-97), at 5.31:1 on the current section's `sidebar-accent` in light and 6.55:1 in dark (the measure of `src/ui/globals.test.ts`, run on a scratch copy, 2026-10-29); the number is `aria-hidden`, and a `sr-only` text says it in words, « 12 mots », so that the link reads « Mots interdits 12 mots » (#226), where #226 writes « Mots interdits, 12 mots »: the `sr-only` text keeps the « 12 mots » that #226 gives it rather than taking a leading comma, which the name's computation would set apart with a space, as it sets the badge apart from the name (inferred) (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #226, for Gilles to review), and « 0 mot » or « 1 mot » in the singular, through the French plural rules that the list's count follows (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #226, for Gilles to review). `StaffHeader` takes the badge as one node, `blockedWordCount`, as it takes `member`, so that the navigation knows nothing of the blocked words, and renders it after « Mots interdits » in the sidebar and in the Sheet; the layout fills it with `BlockedWordCountBadge` under a `<Suspense>` of its own with no fallback, so the badge delays nothing and shows once Discord answers (#226), where a promise read through `use` in `StaffHeader` had the navigation import `src/blocked-words/` (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #226, for Gilles to review, after the code review of #226). The badge draws nothing for no count, which `readBlockedWordCount` gives when reading the rules throws a `DiscordError` (**Read failures**), and when the layout's staff check fails: it counts only once that check has read the member, since the member's node then redirects or throws, so that no count shows before a redirect, and a check that fails on Discord reports its error through the member's node alone, though the badge then waits for that check before it reads the rules (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #226, for Gilles to review). The count is the length of the list that `readBlockedWords` gives, so the badge and the page count the same words. The current one takes `sidebar-accent` as its fill, its icon in `primary`, at 4.43:1 in light, enough for an icon's 3:1 and not for text's 4.5:1, so its text stays `sidebar-accent-foreground` (#224), and a 2 px bar in `primary` against the sidebar's left edge, in a `<ViewTransition>` named `current-section-bar`, which slides to the next current one (**Motion**); another section takes `accent` on hover (#221). It is built from plain elements rather than shadcn/ui's Sidebar, a client component with logic of its own, a context, a `sidebar_state` cookie written through `document.cookie`, the Ctrl+B shortcut and `useIsMobile` (https://ui.shadcn.com/r/styles/base-nova/sidebar.json, read 2026-10-27), which would reopen **No DOM** and **Framework** (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #224). From `md` to `lg`, that same `<aside>` is a rail of 64 px, through `max-lg:` classes on the same markup and not a second render, so that the sections, the badge and the member are in the DOM once, under the same accessible names in the three forms, « Mots interdits 12 mots », « Journal d’audit », « Modération » and the account's name for the member's link (`e2e/responsive.test.ts`, #356; one markup (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #356, for Gilles to review, where `docs/responsive.md` already has it)). The panel's link shows the logo alone, its name in `sr-only`, through the `nameVisibility` of its variants; the ornament is a 32 px line with its diamond in the middle, `RailOrnament`, in `staff-header.tsx`, since no prop of `OrnamentRule` gives that form and a prop with one user would grow a shared component (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #356, for Gilles to review); the group titles go `sr-only`, still the names of their lists, and a 24 px rule in `border` stands before each group after the first; a section's link is a 44 px square, its icon centered, its name in `sr-only` and repeated in a `title`, which the one markup also gives the sidebar, where a visible name then gets a redundant description, as the identity's `title` and « Confidentialité »'s do (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #356, for Gilles to review, since #356 asks for the `title` in the rail alone and a `title` that only exists below `lg` would need a second markup); the current one keeps `sidebar-accent`, its icon in `primary` and its 2 px bar, at the rail's left edge; the badge of `BlockedWordCountBadge` sits under its icon, centered, a pill on `sidebar` ringed in `border`, still at 11 px, where `docs/responsive.md` pinned it to the top right: from there it grew to the left, over the icon, as the count grew, from three digits, and « 3 000 », the most that the gauge allows, is 41 px wide in a link of 44, so that under the icon it grows sideways and covers nothing (the flow sets its text to « 3 000 » and reads its box under the icon, inside the link, with the rail scrolling no wider) (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #356, for Gilles to review, after the review of #356 found the badge covering the icon); it takes `in-data-[slot=sidebar]:max-lg:` classes, the `<aside>` carrying `data-slot="sidebar"`, since the same node also renders in the Sheet, where `max-lg:` alone would place it as well (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #356, for Gilles to review); and the foot stacks the theme toggle's three icons, the member's avatar, the log-out icon button and « Confidentialité » as a `Lock` icon button, each 44 px (**Theme**). The member's name goes `sr-only` there, in place of `hidden` as in the bar, so that its link, which keeps going to their own record, is named by the account's name alone, from its own text, and never by the `title` of `StaffMemberIdentity`, « aldis · Staff · Modération », which at every width shows the name and the role to a mouse and describes nothing the link's name needs (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #356, for Gilles to review); the line under the name goes `sr-only` too, in place of `hidden`, so that a screen reader still reads it (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #356, for Gilles to review), and `src/staff/staff-role-label.ts` gives that line and the `title` the same label. `PrivacyPolicyLink` and `ThemeToggle` take a `rail` prop, `ThemeToggle` beside its `iconOnly` and not in its place, since a rail shows the names from `lg` where an icon-only toggle never does (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #356, for Gilles to review): the link draws the `Lock` below `lg`, leaves its name in `sr-only` below `lg` and takes a `title` at every width, and the toggle stacks its icons below `lg`; with no `rail`, as in the Sheet and on the login pages, each is what it was. On a screen too short for the rail, a phone in landscape say, the `<aside>` scrolls, `overflow-y-auto`, and its list of sections takes its own height in the rail, `max-lg:overflow-visible`, where `flex-1` with `shrink-0` left that list at no height at all, so that the foot stays reachable (a Playwright screenshot of the rail at 844 by 390 px, taken by a throwaway test with `pnpm test:e2e`, 2026-11-03, and `e2e/responsive.test.ts` at 1,023 by 300 px) (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #356, for Gilles to review). The pages sit beside it, in a column that holds the mockup's margin, `clamp(1.25rem, 4vw, 3.5rem)` on each side, `2.75rem` above and `6rem` below (#224), `1.5rem` above under `md`, where the bar already stands above it, and each side raised to the screen's safe-area inset when that is wider, the left one under `md` alone, since the rail and the sidebar take it from `md` and are as wide as that inset more (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #356, for Gilles to review, since #356 names the bar and the column, and a rail or a sidebar drawn under a notch would hide its controls), so their `<main>` lost its own `p-4`, and each page keeps its `max-w-*` until the ticket that redesigns it (#224; dropping `p-4` decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #224, for Gilles to review). `/records` lost its `mx-auto max-w-xl` with #232: its header holds `max-w-160` and its form and results `max-w-190`, the mockup's 640 and 760 px, against the left edge of its box. An account that opened no ticket says « Aucun ticket de support archivé pour ce compte. », and a panel given no archives channel « Le salon des archives n’est pas configuré pour ce serveur Discord. ». The record reads the 100 newest archives of the channel that cite the account, as opener or as closer, through the search (**Navigation**, #387), and a known limit follows from it. It meets an account cited in more than 100 archives, in practice a staff member who closed many tickets, since any other account is cited by the tickets it opened alone: the card ends on « Seules les 100 archives les plus récentes où ce compte apparaît, qu’il les ait ouvertes ou fermées, ont été lues : ses tickets plus anciens ne s’affichent peut-être pas. », and the tickets of that account older than the 100th archive that cites it are not shown. The 100, 4 searches of 25, is the agent's, against the ticket's « tous les tickets du compte »: the search allows about one call every 4 to 5 s once a burst of a few has passed, and 10 searches back to back were 7 answers and 3 429s of 1.2 s or less (ADR 0010, `probe-383-5.mjs`), so 4 searches fit the burst of a rested start, and a record that read every page the search reaches, 400 at 25 an offset up to 9,975, would take about 30 minutes at that rate (400 × 4.5 s) and meet a 429 over the 2 s the client waits for long before. The card's sentence says what was read, the 100 newest archives that cite the account, and what is missing, its older tickets, and replaces the 1,000 messages of the channel that the card named before. An account cited in more than 100 archives none of which it opened, a staff member who only closed tickets, reads « Aucun ticket de support de ce compte parmi les archives lues. » above that sentence, where « Aucun ticket de support archivé pour ce compte. » would say that it opened none, which the read cannot know (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #387, for Gilles to review). A record that names no account shows no card. `RecordColumns` gives it a third row, `grid-rows-[auto_auto_1fr]` with the sanctions on `row-span-3`, where the history's row was the `1fr` one: a sanctions card taller than the details and the history stretched that row and left the tickets 243 px under the history (`e2e/ticket-archives.test.ts`, throwaway build, 2026-11-05). At 360, 768 and 1,024 px, the page's column holds 320, 643 and 690 px, under the 50.75 rem of its container query, so the tickets follow the history in the one column, after the details and the sanctions, each entry stacked; from 1,157 px of screen, where the column reaches 812 px, they sit under the history on the left, beside the sanctions (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #197, for Gilles to review, the ticket's « Terminé quand » naming no width, which this adds to it). Under `md`, the bar, 56 px high, on `sidebar`, sticks to the top of the page, `sticky top-0 z-40`, so that the menu stays in reach on a long page: it scrolled away with the page until #356, as the header did, which #224 left for Gilles to review and #354 settled (Gilles, 2026-11-03). Its height is `3.5rem` plus the screen's top safe-area inset, which it pads, and its sides are `max(1rem, …)` of the left and right insets (#356). Under it, the document takes a `scroll-padding-top` of the same height, `html:has([data-slot="bar"])` in `src/ui/globals.css`, so that a link that Shift+Tab or a script focuses just under the bar scrolls in below it and not behind it, as WCAG 2.4.11, Focus Not Obscured (Minimum), a level AA criterion (https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/, Recommendation of 2024-12-12), asks: the review of #356 found it hidden, with `elementFromPoint` answering the header, and `e2e/responsive.test.ts` fails without the padding and reads, in its safe-area flow, that the padding equals the bar's height, which `staff-header.tsx` and `globals.css` both write. It holds the logo, with « Shor » and « Panel du staff » left as the link's `sr-only` name, and a `ghost` icon button, `Menu`, named « Ouvrir le menu » in `sr-only`, which opens shadcn/ui's Sheet from the right, the button's side, rather than from the left as #129 planned (Gilles, 2026-10-27, on #137), titled « Menu », on `sidebar` like the sidebar, with the same groups, the current section marked the same way, its bar included, though it slides in the sidebar alone, the theme toggle (#225), the member, and « Se déconnecter » with its text visible (#137, #224), then « Confidentialité », a `SheetClose` that renders `PrivacyPolicyLink`, as a section's renders its `next/link`, so that a click closes the Sheet (#186); the groups and the footer sit in the Sheet's `SheetBody`, under its header, which scrolls on a screen too short for them, a phone in landscape say, the footer's `mt-auto` keeping it at the bottom otherwise, so that the close button stays in view (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #356, for Gilles to review, since the footer inside the body keeps everything scrolling together under the header); the logo, the avatar and the menu button are 44 px, as every link of the Sheet is (**Touch targets** of **Responsive**); the member's avatar sits beside that button, and the Sheet's footer shows it again beside the name (#140). `StaffHeader` takes the avatar and the name as one node, `member`, which the layout fills with `StaffMemberIdentity` under `<Suspense>`, and renders it three times, in the sidebar's foot, in the bar and in the Sheet's footer. For a staff member who moderates, each of the three is one link to their own record, over the avatar and the name, which `StaffHeader` makes from `ownRecord`, a promise of `{ href, name }` or `null` that `ownRecordOnceRead`, in `src/records/own-record.ts`, gives from the same read as `opensModerationOnceRead`: `null` for a staff member who does not moderate, through `recordHrefForModerator`, and when the read fails, and that the layout hands to `StaffHeader` (#320). The type of its value is `StaffHeader`'s own, so that `src/staff/` imports no domain folder, which the cycle records → sanctions → staff → records would break (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #320, for Gilles to review). `StaffHeader` reads it through `use`, under a `<Suspense>` whose fallback is the node itself, so that the identity shows, unlinked, until Discord answers, and marks the link `aria-current="page"` on that record alone, as a section is on its path. The sidebar's and the bar's links are `AccountLink`s, which take `prefetch={false}`, and the Sheet's is a `SheetClose` that renders a `next/link` with its own `prefetch={false}`, as a section's is, so that a click closes the Sheet. `StaffHeader` makes the link, and not `StaffMemberIdentity`, because the one node renders three times and Base UI's `DialogClose` throws outside a `Dialog.Root` (`node_modules/@base-ui/react/dialog/root/DialogRootContext.js:15-20`), so a node that carried it could not stand in the sidebar or the bar (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #320, for Gilles to review). The line under the name comes from the read whose promise `opensModerationOnceRead` gives `StaffHeader`, so it costs no call to Discord, where #224 names the promise itself (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #224, for Gilles to review); since #320 it is not part of that node, but drawn by `StaffHeader` under the identity in the sidebar and in the Sheet, from that promise through `use`, under a `<Suspense>` of its own whose fallback is a bar, so that it sits outside the identity's link and the link is named by the account's name alone, which story 32 of #317 asks (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #320, for Gilles to review). In the bar, `**:data-[slot=staff-member-name]:hidden` hides every descendant marked `data-slot="staff-member-name"`, the name and its skeleton's bar, so the avatar alone stays beside the menu button, and the bar shows no line, while the Sheet, which Base UI portals out of the bar, keeps the name. The bar's link, which holds the avatar alone, keeps the name in an `sr-only` text before it, which names the link, where the sidebar's and the Sheet's add none, since their name shows and would be read twice, so that all three are named by the account's name alone (#320). One node holds one skeleton for all three, where an avatar of its own would need a second `<Suspense>` (#140). The layout reads the member once, through one `requireStaffMember` call whose promise it shares: it fills `member` from it, and hands `StaffHeader`, through `opensModerationOnceRead` in `src/staff/`, a promise of whether they open « Modération », false when the read fails, since the member's node then redirects or throws. `StaffHeader` reads that promise through `use`, under a `<Suspense>` of its own with no fallback, in the sidebar and in the Sheet, so the other sections show at once and the groups « Modération » and « Support » once Discord answers, both waiting on that promise (#176, #224, #385). The switch is `md` rather than the `sm` #129 planned, since at 640 px the wide header that came before the sidebar left the member's name about 10 px of width (Playwright screenshot, 2026-10-27; Gilles, 2026-10-27, on #137), and #224 kept it for the sidebar, which #356 moved to `lg` for a rail of 64 px from `md` to `lg` (Gilles, 2026-11-03, on #354). It happens in CSS, through `md:` and `lg:` variants, never `useIsMobile`, and the Sheet keeps Base UI's own open state, so `StaffHeader` still renders from its props alone, but for the theme toggle's leaf, `StatefulThemeToggle`, which it renders in the sidebar's foot and in the Sheet's footer, and which the server renders on « Auto » whatever was chosen (**Theme**); `StatefulStaffHeader` and that leaf are the navigation's two client leaves of the panel's own, beside the copied Sheet's (#224, #225). Each section's link in the Sheet is a `SheetClose` that renders a `next/link`, so a click closes the Sheet; its `nativeButton={false}` has Base UI add `role="button"` (`node_modules/@base-ui/react/internals/use-button/useButton.js:186`), which its `role={undefined}`, merged after it, takes off, so the link stays a link (the 360 px flow of `e2e/staff-header.test.ts` fails without it, 2026-10-27). Each section's `next/link`, in the sidebar and in the Sheet, takes `prefetch={false}`, since its prefetch could reach `/login` right after a logout, which `proxy.ts` sends a request without a session to (Gilles, 2026-10-28, on #153, over the matcher below). The logout's response deletes the session cookie, so the router invalidates its prefetch cache and prefetches every link still visible again once a cooldown of 300 ms ends (`node_modules/next/dist/client/components/router-reducer/reducers/server-action-reducer.js:228-233`, `node_modules/next/dist/client/components/segment-cache/scheduler.js:65`, `node_modules/next/dist/client/components/links.js:285`), while the navigation to `/login/logged-out` waited, behind a view transition of about 300 ms, to hide the header, whose unmounted links would cancel their prefetch (`links.js:185-193`); and a viewport prefetch still under way at the click sent its second request, a segment's, once the cookie was gone (a throwaway Playwright probe that logged the page's requests and view transitions, red in 6 runs of 40, 2026-10-28). Without the prefetch, the probe passed 40 runs of 40, and the header flow `--repeat-each=300` 300 of 300 (2026-10-28). A click on a section then fetches its page, a round trip before its skeleton shows (inferred). The matcher of `proxy.ts` could skip prefetches instead, through `missing` (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/proxy.md:666-672`), which passed the same probe in 40 runs of 40, but a request without a session would then read a staff page's static shell (inferred, not tried), where **`proxy.ts`** sends it to `/login`, and Next.js hides `next-router-prefetch` from Proxy's code so that an RSC request is handled as its HTML is (`proxy.md:474`).
- **Login pages.** `/login/logged-out`, `/login/refused` and `/login/failed` share `LoginNotice`, in `src/staff/`, which lays out the right column of the mockup's login screen alone, centered, at most 672 px wide, `max-w-2xl`, about the column's width at 1440 px (M:30-74; inferred from its `minmax(440px, 1fr)` beside `1.15fr`), with a 16 px gutter under `sm` and the mockup's 48 px from `sm` up, and without its left column, the empty slot of the site's illustration (M:32), which the panel does not take, since it must not look like the site (#221, #236). The column fills the screen's height: on top, a bar with the logo, « Shor » in letter-spaced capitals in the headings' font, and the theme toggle (**Theme**); in its middle, a `<main>` that takes the height the bar and the footer leave, and centers in it, against the column's left edge, a block at most 400 px wide: the logo again, the overline « Panel du staff · Skyrim Roleplay », an `h1` of 38 px under `sm` and 46 px from it in the headings' font (#362), a sentence in `muted-foreground`, the rule with a diamond, and the page's buttons (**Buttons**); in its `<footer>`, « Réservé au staff de Shor », the note `LoginNotice` gives `PublicPageFrame`, then « Confidentialité », `PrivacyPolicyLink`'s `next/link` to the privacy policy, which does not prefetch (**Privacy policy**, #186), and a link « shor-rp.fr » to https://shor-rp.fr, a plain `<a>` with `target="_blank"` and `rel="noopener noreferrer"`, before an `ExternalLink` icon, `aria-hidden`, all three in `muted-foreground`, wrapping onto a second line where the screen is too narrow for them, where the mockup's `--fg3` would miss 4.5:1 (#221). On a touch screen, each of the two links takes 44 px of height, through `touch:min-h-11`, which applies to both since each is a flex item of the footer's inner row, and, on « Confidentialité », `touch:inline-flex touch:items-center` as well, so that its text sits in the middle of the 44 px, as the `shor-rp.fr` link's does through its own `inline-flex items-center` (inferred from CSS, not measured without it), which `PublicPageFrame` gives it through `PrivacyPolicyLink`'s `className`, since `privacy-policy-link.tsx` stays as it is (#356 changes it), and the footer centers its items so that the note stays level with them (#362; the composition decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #362, for Gilles to review). The top bar and the footer are `PublicPageFrame`'s, beside it in `src/staff/`, which the privacy policy shares, so that both read alike (#186). The top bar is a `<div>` rather than a `<header>`, whose `banner` the login flows of **End to end** read as the staff's bar, which a login page lacks. The link needs no change of the Content Security Policy, which sets no directive on a navigation (inferred from **Headers**' list). Its name stays « shor-rp.fr », with no `sr-only` text saying it opens a new tab: that is WCAG's advisory technique G201, under 3.2.5, a level AAA criterion, beyond the AA of **Accessibility**. The link to « #créer-ticket » of the error pages and of `/login/failed` opens a new tab the same way, and has no such text either (**Error pages**, #61). `/login/logged-out` says « Ta session est terminée. », then « Reconnecte-toi avec ton compte Discord pour revenir au panel. », with « Se connecter avec Discord »; `/login/refused` keeps « Accès refusé » and its sentence, with « Se reconnecter » and « Se déconnecter »; `/login/failed` keeps « Connexion échouée » and its sentence, which names « #créer-ticket » as a link in place of « le salon du staff sur Discord » (**Error pages**, #61), with « Réessayer » (#236). Their tab titles stay « Déconnexion », « Accès refusé » and « Connexion échouée », though the first page's `h1` changed. The column's width and gutters, the block that keeps to the column's left edge as the mockup's does, the logo before « Shor » and the `ExternalLink` icon, both drawn by the mockup and not named by #236, the 40 px buttons (**Buttons**), the top bar as a `<div>`, the link with no word on its new tab, and the tab titles were decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #236, for Gilles to review.
- **Privacy policy.** `/privacy` renders `PrivacyPolicy`, in `src/privacy-policy/`, which reads nothing from Discord and runs no staff check, so the build prerenders it whole (`○` in the route list of `pnpm build`, 2026-11-02), and `proxy.ts` lets a visitor without a session through (**`proxy.ts`**, #186). It sits in `PublicPageFrame`, the login pages' top bar and footer, whose note it sets to « Panel du staff de Shor », since « Réservé au staff de Shor » would tell a member that the policy is not for them, and whose « Confidentialité » it marks `aria-current="page"`, over a `<main>` that holds `PageHeader`, with the overline « Panel du staff · Skyrim Roleplay », the `h1` « Politique de confidentialité » and a sentence, then « Mise à jour le » and the day of its last change, `LAST_UPDATED`, in a `<time>`, in `dateStyle: "medium"` and Paris time, such as « 2 nov. 2026 » (**Dates**), the rule with `leadingLine`, then one `<section>` per heading, each an `h2` in the headings' font at 22 px, as « La liste » of the blocked words is, over paragraphs and lists at 15 px. The sections take no name, so that, like the audit log's days, they add no landmark. The `<code>` that names the session cookie takes `wrap-anywhere` (#362). Its tab reads « Politique de confidentialité – Panel de Shor ». Its nine headings say what it covers, what the panel keeps, what it reads without keeping it, what it writes into Discord, why, who sees it, who else touches it, each one's rights, and whom to write to, and each section gives the durations of what it describes, which together say what #186 lists, and their text says in French what the **Today** of `docs/personal-data.md` says, so that a change to one changes the other, and the page's date, in the same change (**Personal data**). It covers « Shor Panel » and its test application, « Shor Panel Dev », whose Developer Portals are to link it, a step only Gilles can take (#341). « Responsable et contact » keeps the placeholder #186 asks for, « Le responsable de ces données et l’adresse où lui écrire restent à désigner. En attendant, adresse ta demande au staff de Shor, sur le serveur Discord de Shor. », until « Who is responsible for the panel's personal data, and who answers requests? » is answered (`docs/agents/open-questions.md`), and the page names no legal basis, which that question now asks too. The login pages link it from their footer, and the navigation from the sidebar's foot and the Sheet's footer, each through `PrivacyPolicyLink`, in `src/staff/`, a `next/link` with `prefetch={false}`: `proxy.ts` would send no such prefetch to `/login`, but the flows that log out read `prefetchedPaths` empty (**End to end**). The error pages do not link it, since they show the panel's failure and one way out of it. The headings, the date, the footer's note and its « Confidentialité », where the navigation shows it, and the error pages left without it, were decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #186, for Gilles to review, since #186 lists what the page says and not its form.
- **The support tickets' page** (#385). `/support-tickets`, under « Support » in the navigation (**Navigation**), is open to the whole staff, as the archives channel is, and lists the ticket archives that the Bot posts there (ADR 0009), newest first, 25 a page, each with its request kind, who opened it and who closed it, when, in Paris time, and why. « Heures de Paris » is said once, in the overline « Tickets · Heures de Paris ». Its `h1` is « Tickets de support », its tab « Tickets de support – Panel de Shor », and its `<ol>` is named by the ranks of the overline above it, « 26–50 sur 312 », as the list of accounts' is. A name, the opener's or the closer's, is a link to that account's record for a staff member who moderates and plain text for another (**Avatars**, `recordHrefForModerator`), each on a line of its own, since a link in a sentence is exempt from the 44 px that #385 asks (**Responsive**). Above the archives, under an `h2` « En cours » where the archives sit under « Archives », it lists the support tickets in progress (#388, ADR 0009). `readOpenSupportTickets`, in `src/support-tickets/`, keeps the text channels of the tickets category (`TICKET_CATEGORY_ID`, **Variables**) whose name starts with the emoji of one of ShorBot's four request kinds and the separator `・`, that carry exactly one overwrite of a member allowing View Channel, the opener's, as ShorBot reads them (ADR 0009 holds the contract and ShorBot's ADR 0008 its other side), and that the staff member views on Discord (`viewsChannel`, #384), oldest first, since the oldest has waited longest (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #388, for Gilles to review). It reads the last 100 messages of each. A ticket shows its request kind, as an archive writes it; its opener's name; when it opened, from the channel's ID; its last activity, from the newest message's ID; its number of messages, « 100+ » when the page is full, which includes a ticket of exactly 100 messages, since Discord returns 100 messages at most a call and 100 cannot be told from 101 without a second call for each full ticket (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #388, for Gilles to review, where the ticket says « au-delà de 100 »); the staff who answered, the authors of its messages other than the opener who are not bots and hold the staff role now, so that one who has left is not counted; the author of the last message and an extract of its text, 160 characters at most, an ellipsis included, a mention written as `@name` where the name is known, and a bot's embeds' descriptions following its content, where a member's embeds are left out; and « Ouvrir dans Discord », a plain `<a>` to `https://discord.com/channels/<server>/<channel>` in a new tab. The opener, the staff who answered and the last message's author are links to their records for a staff member who moderates, but a bot's. A category left out of the configuration says that it is not configured, and a category with no ticket that the staff member views says « Aucun ticket n’est en cours. », which tells nothing of the tickets they do not view (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #388, for Gilles to review, for the ticket asks for one sentence). Its filters, its order and its page are in the URL, and **The filters** says how they are read (#386).
  - **The reader.** `searchTicketArchives(discord, archivesChannelId, applicationId, offset)`, in `src/ticket-archives/search-ticket-archives.ts`, is what #386 and #387 build on, and #387's record reads through it. It makes one `searchMessages` of the archives channel for the application's messages alone, newest first unless it is asked for the oldest, `ARCHIVES_PER_PAGE` (25) from `offset`, and gives `{ total, messages, archives }`: `total` is Discord's `total_results`, `messages` the number of messages the page held, and `archives` those that follow the contract of `ticketArchiveOf` and whose author is the application, which it checks again beside `author_id`, so that a message a member forged stays out even if the search let it through (`src/ticket-archives/search-ticket-archives.test.ts`, through a fake that ignores `author_id`). It takes the application's ID instead of reading it, so that a caller that pages makes one `getApplicationUser`, and it takes the options of `SearchFilters`: a `text`, which the record sets to the account's ID (#387) and the page's filters to an ID, a reason or a kind's emoji, a `minId` and a `maxId`, which are strict bounds, `oldestFirst`, and a `limit`, 25 unless the counts of the kinds ask for 1 (#386). `readTicketArchivePage`, in `read-ticket-archive-page.ts`, takes the URL's search parameters, reads the filters they hold (**The filters**) and the `page`, a string of digits from 1 to 400 or else absent, and reads the archives in one of two ways, `readServedPage` or `readWindowPage`. Both name each opener and closer of the page through `accountNames`, in `account-names.ts`, which the record's reader took out of its own file: the nickname on the Discord server, else the account's name, else none, and the ID shows. `ticketArchivesHref` writes the filters that are set, then the `page` if it is not the first, into the URL, in the order `kind`, `opener`, `closer`, `reason`, `from`, `to`, `on`, `sort`, `page`.
  - **The filters** (#386). `ticketArchiveFiltersOf`, in `ticket-archive-filters.ts`, reads the URL's `kind`, `opener`, `closer`, `reason`, `from`, `to`, `on` and `sort`, and a value that is empty, blank, repeated, too long or not valid counts as absent (**Validation**): `kind` is `technical-issue`, `player-report`, `rp-request` or `other-request`, the four kinds of ShorBot's `REQUEST_KINDS` that `request-kinds.ts` mirrors with their emoji and label, a contract that joins ADR 0009 (ShorRP/ShorBot#91 carries it into ShorBot's ADR 0008); `opener` and `closer` are a name, an ID or a mention of 100 characters at most; `reason` holds a word and 1,024 characters at most, the limit of Discord's search (ADR 0010); `from` and `to` are days written `yyyy-mm-dd`, from 2015 to 2099, and count as days of Paris; `on` is `opened`, and absent means the closing; `sort` is `oldest`, and absent means the newest first. An `on` with neither `from` nor `to` is no filter. Discord's search serves one text per view, since it finds the words of one text of a message and never those of two (ADR 0010), and the account's ID, the reason and the kind sit in three fields. So the page reads in one of two ways (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #386, for Gilles to review, since #386 names the filters and not what Discord serves of them).
    - **Served.** With no opener, no closer, no reason and no period on the opening, `readServedPage` lets Discord page the archives, as before the filters: the kind, if any, is the search's text, its emoji, which the test Discord server found as a word of the search for each of the four (a throwaway probe of 2026-11-05, #386, where #383 had tried 🔧 alone); the period on the closing is `min_id` and `max_id`, and the order `sort_order`. The period is the time of the message, which the Bot posts seconds after the ticket closes, so an archive closed in the last seconds of a day may sit on the other side of it from its « Fermé le » (inferred). A day is a day of Paris: `parisDayStart`, in `src/ui/paris-day-bounds.ts` since #393, gives its midnight, `to` ends at the midnight after it, and `min_id` is the ID just under the first one of the first millisecond, since both bounds are strict (ADR 0010). The total is Discord's, so it counts the messages that hold the emoji, an archive of another kind that cites it in its reason included, which the page leaves out of its list and keeps in the count, as it does a message that follows no contract. That is 1 search.
    - **Window.** With an opener, a closer, a reason or a period on the opening, Discord's text can serve only one of the filters, so `readWindowPage` reads the first 100 messages of what Discord finds, 4 searches at most, and stops when a search holds no more. The text Discord is given is the first of the opener's ID, the closer's ID and the longest term of the reason as typed, never the kind's emoji, so that the window holds every kind and the pills count them all under the other filters. Every filter is then checked in memory over those messages, exactly: the opener and the closer by their IDs, the reason by every word of the text among the words of the reason, wherever they sit in it, the kind by its emoji, and the period on the opening, which Discord does not serve, by `openedAt`. A period on the opening reads from `from` on, the oldest first, since a ticket closes after it opened: `min_id` is the start of `from` and the 100 oldest archives closed since then are read, and with `to` alone the 100 oldest archives (inferred, since Discord offers no bound on the opening). The archives that pass are ordered by the sort and paged in memory, 25 a page, so that a window has 4 pages at most, a page past the last is the first, and the total and the ranks are those of the archives that pass among the 100 read. A reason is searched by one term because Discord's search skips two words at most between the words of one text, a `slop` of 2 that it takes up to 100 (ADR 0010, a throwaway probe of 2026-11-05): « un cinq » found nothing in « un deux trois quatre cinq », where a term has no distance. A word is Discord's, whole, whatever the case and the accents: an apostrophe, U+2019, joins the letters around it, so that « l’ouverture » is one word that « ouverture » does not find (the same probe, and the contract suite), which the fake and `reason-words.ts` follow; a typed apostrophe of ASCII is not known to join (inferred, not tried). (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #386, for Gilles to review). When Discord holds more, `complete` is false and the page says « Seules les 100 archives les plus récentes de cette recherche ont été lues : les plus anciennes ne s’affichent pas. Précise un filtre ou une période pour les atteindre. », the other way round when the oldest were read.
    - **Accounts.** An ID or a mention is the account, whether or not it is on the Discord server, and costs no call. A name is resolved by `resolveAccounts`, in `resolve-accounts.ts`, against the Discord server's members and its banned accounts (`readAllMembers` and `readAllBans`, routes other than the search's): the accounts whose username, global name or nickname starts with it, whatever the case, as the list of accounts finds them. One account is the account. None, or several, give no archive and say so, « Aucun membre ni compte banni ne commence par « zzz » (ouvreur) : un compte parti du serveur se cherche par son ID ou sa mention. » or « Plusieurs comptes commencent par « ly » (ouvreur) : précise le nom, ou colle l’ID ou la mention du compte. », with « Réinitialiser », unless exactly one account has a name equal to the text, which is then the account. A name costs 2 calls to those routes, 1 more for each further 1,000 accounts, beside the searches (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #386, for Gilles to review, where #386 says « par nom, ID ou mention, comme le journal d'audit » and the audit log gets its names from Discord's audit log, while an archive holds an ID alone; an account that left the Discord server without a ban has no name to look up, and is found by its ID).
    - **Counts.** The pills « Tout » and the four kinds sit above the list, and each kind shows how many archives it holds among those that pass the other filters; « Tout » shows no number, since the ranks above the list say the total, and an archive of no known kind is in the ranks and in no pill. In a window they are counted in the window, for free. In a served reading they are Discord's totals: `readKindCounts`, in `read-kind-counts.ts`, searches the emoji of each kind with a `limit` of 1 and the period, 4 searches in parallel, 3 under a kind, whose count is the total the list already holds.
    - **What the staff see of the counts and of the rate limit** (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #386, for Gilles to review). The counts are read after the list and never with it: the page awaits `readTicketArchivePage`, then starts `readKindCounts`, and the pills sit under a `<Suspense>` of their own whose fallback is the pills without numbers, no skeleton, so that the list and the pills show at once and the numbers come when the counts do. A `DiscordError` of the counts, a 429 over the 2 s the client waits included, gives no counts and the pills stay without numbers, while any other error is thrown (**Read failures**; as the count of the blocked words does). So a staff member who clicks a pill again within seconds loses the numbers and keeps the page, and the first view after a rest shows the list and the numbers. The list's own search can still meet a 429 over the 2 s when the counts of the views before it have spent the burst, and that is the error page and « Réessayer » of **The search's rate limit**. A view costs 1 search for the list and 4 for the counts when it is not filtered, 4 in all under a kind, from 1 to 4 for a window, each one more when the page asked for lies past the last of a served reading. `e2e/support-ticket-filters.test.ts` proves the pills without numbers and the list whole when the counts' searches are refused.
  - **What the staff see past three limits** (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #385, for Gilles to review, since #385 says nothing of any). Discord's search refuses an `offset` past 9,975 (`probe-383-6.mjs`, ADR 0010), so the page reaches the 10,000 newest messages, 400 pages at most: `?page=401` and every page that is no page count as absent, as the list of accounts' do, and show the first page, and when the channel holds more messages, `complete` is false and the page says, under its navigation, « Discord ne permet de lire que les 10 000 archives les plus récentes : les plus anciennes ne s’affichent pas. », the older ones showing on no page until a filter that finds fewer, a kind, a period or an account, reaches them, and once a filter is set the sentence says « … archives les plus récentes de cette recherche … » (#386). And the total and the ranks count messages, not archives: `total_results` counts every message the application posted in the channel, and a message that follows no contract, a text or an embed under another title, is left out of its page and not out of the count, so that « 1–25 sur 312 » may sit over 24 archives. The archives channel holds only what the Bot posts (ADR 0009), so none is expected (inferred). A page whose messages hold no archive says « Aucune archive de ticket à afficher. », and keeps its navigation to the others, while filters that keep none say « Aucune archive ne correspond à ces filtres. » over « Réinitialiser » (#386). And Discord's index lags: a message was found 8 to 58 s after its post and a page may hold fewer messages than it should (ADR 0010, `probe-383-3.mjs` to `probe-383-9.mjs`), so an archive closed seconds ago may be missing for a while, and one that appears between two views pushes the archives after it one place down, so that the last archive of a page may show again at the top of the next (inferred). The next view shows it, and the page says nothing of it.
  - **The search's rate limit.** Discord allows about one search every 4 to 5 s once a burst of a few has passed, and answers a search over it with a 429 whose `retry_after` reached 4.4 s on the test Discord server, where the client waits for one of 2 s or less (ADR 0010, **Retries**). A view's list makes one search, and one more only when the page asked for lies past the last, or up to 4 for a window, and its counts of the kinds make 4 more or none (**The filters**, #386), so that a page of `/support-tickets` costs 5 of that burst when it is not filtered, 4 under a kind and from 1 to 4 for a window, where a record costs one for an account cited in 25 archives or fewer, and up to 4 for one cited in more (#387), beside a `getApplicationUser` and up to 50 `getMember`, and a `getUser` for each of those who has left, which are other routes (inferred not to count against the search's limit).
