A staff member who pages faster than that, after the burst, meets a 429 over the 2 s: the client throws it as a `DiscordError` that says Discord is unavailable, nothing catches it (**Read failures**), `error.tsx` replaces the page with its « Réessayer », which works a few seconds later, and no alert goes out, since Discord unavailable alerts no one (**Alerts**). A 202 of an index not ready, which `searchMessages` retries once, ends the same way. `e2e/support-tickets.test.ts` proves the error page and its retry on a 429 of 4.4 s that the fake throws once, not what the real client does with one, which no test measured on this page. The tickets in progress cost, for N tickets that the staff member views and D other authors, a `getGuild` and a `listChannels`, N `listMessages`, one for each channel and so each in a bucket of its own, and N + D `getMember`, with a `getUser` for an opener who has left, at most 5 at a time (`mapWithConcurrency`), so about 110 calls for the 50 channels that ShorBot lets a category hold (`CATEGORY_LIMIT`, `shorbot/support_tickets.py:53` at 9915eb7), none of them through the search's limit. A 429 of 2 s or less is retried once; any other fails the page as Discord unavailable (**Read failures**). Discord's global limit, 50 requests a second, was not approached by any trial (inferred, #388). The page shares one `requireStaffMember` between its two sections through a promise, as `/records` does. The counts of the kinds spend the same burst, which is why the list is read before them and a refusal of theirs is no error page.
- **Wording.** The panel addresses the staff as « tu », and its buttons are infinitives: « Ajouter », « Retirer », « Se déconnecter », « Réessayer ». Its texts follow French typography: the apostrophe ’, « » with a no-break space U+00A0 inside, a U+00A0 before : ; ! ?, and « … » as one character, each typed as such in the source; `pnpm check:typography` is to refuse the rest (#73). Until then, the lint only refuses ' in JSX text, through `react/no-unescaped-entities` (`pnpm exec eslint --print-config src/app/page.tsx`, 2026-10-24), and sees neither strings nor spaces. A message that puts a name after « de » elides it, « d’Aldis » but « de Lyra »: `deName`, in `src/sanctions/de-name.ts`, which the four messages of `src/sanctions/` that name an account after « de » call (the kick, the ban, the lifting of a ban and the lifting of a timeout), writes « d’ » when the first character of the name, as Discord gives it, is a, e, i, o or u, accented or not, in either case, and « de » for anything else: « h » and « y », « æ » and « œ », and a first character that is no letter (#219; the helper sits in `src/sanctions/` since only that folder uses it, decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #219, for Gilles to review).
- **Dates** are absolute, on 24 hours and in Paris time, such as « 24 oct. 2026, 14:32 », from `Intl.DateTimeFormat("fr-FR", { dateStyle: "medium", timeStyle: "short", timeZone: "Europe/Paris" })` (Node 24.22.0). `timeZone` is always passed: the server renders dates, and it knows no browser's time zone. A date sits in a `<time>` whose `dateTime` holds the ISO date, which `ParisTime`, in `src/ui/`, renders (#176), and a page that shows dates says « Heures de Paris. » once, as the list of accounts does after its rows once an account shown is timed out, and only then (#232, #322, #324), and as a record does in the overline « Heures de Paris » of its card « Détails », which replaced its sentence (#233), and as the audit log does in its overline « 45 derniers jours · Heures de Paris », which replaced « L’historique couvre 45 jours. Heures de Paris. » (#230). The audit log groups each page's changes by the day of Paris they were made on, which `parisDayOf`, in `src/ui/paris-day.tsx` since #392, where the shared log components render `ParisDay`, gives as `yyyy-mm-dd`, the `dateTime` of a day's `<time>` (#230): each day holds the moment of its newest change and sits under an `h2` that `ParisDay`, beside it, writes from that moment in Paris time, with its weekday and its year, `dateStyle: "full"`, such as « jeudi 29 octobre 2026 », in lower case as #230 writes it, with no « Aujourd’hui », « Hier » or « Lundi » and no count of its changes, which would be wrong for a day cut between two pages (#221, #230). A day cut between two pages heads both, since each page groups its own 50 changes (`src/audit-log/read-audit-log.test.ts`). Each change then shows its time alone, `timeStyle: "short"`, such as « 09:12 », in a `<time>` whose `dateTime` keeps the full ISO moment, through `ParisTime`'s `timeOnly`, and the page ends on « Jusqu’au <date> », the day of its oldest change in `dateStyle: "medium"`, such as « 22 oct. 2026 » (#230). A write's message is a string the domain writes (**Write results**), so a date there, such as a timeout's end, is plain text in the same format, which `formatParisTime` gives, as in a direct message, which says « (heure de Paris) » itself (#179).
- **Buttons.** `default` for what a form is for, such as « Ajouter », « Chercher », « Filtrer », « Exclure temporairement », « Expulser », « Bannir » or « Réessayer », and for the main action of a login page, « Se connecter avec Discord », « Se reconnecter » or « Réessayer » (#221, #236); `outline` for « Lever l’exclusion temporaire » and « Lever le bannissement », which undo, and for the « Se déconnecter » of `/login/refused`, beside its « Se reconnecter » (#236); `ghost` for the « Se déconnecter » of the sidebar and of the Sheet, the sidebar's an icon button, `size="icon"`, whose text is `sr-only`, while the Sheet's keeps it visible (#221, #224), and for the removal of a blocked word, which undoes too, an `X` alone in `muted-foreground`, `size="icon-sm"`, named « Retirer « foo » » in `sr-only`, where it was an `outline` « Retirer » (#221, #228). The pencil that changes a word's action is a `ghost` icon button drawn the same, a `Pencil` alone in `muted-foreground`, `size="icon-sm"`, named « Changer l’action de « foo » » in `sr-only` (#326). The panel uses no `destructive` variant, whose red text on its own tint missed 4.5:1 with `nova`'s values (#70), and with Hydromel's still misses it on hover, on `destructive/20` in light and `destructive/30` in dark, at 3.94:1 and 4.17:1 (a scratch port of the theme test's measure, 2026-10-29). « Expulser » and « Bannir » are red all the same, as #221 decided: `default` Buttons that `RED_BUTTON_CLASS`, in `src/sanctions/sanction-form.ts`, gives `bg-destructive text-destructive-foreground hover:bg-destructive/90`, so that the copied Button does not change (#234); `destructive-foreground` holds 5.40:1 and 6.77:1 on `destructive`, and 4.56:1 and 5.72:1 on the hover's `destructive/90` over `card` (**Accessibility**). They, « Exclure temporairement » and « Lever le bannissement » span their card, 38 px high as the mockup draws them (M:512), while « Lever l’exclusion temporaire » keeps its width in its notice (**Forms**). A login page's buttons are 40 px high, as the mockup draws them (M:57-64), through `LOGIN_ACTION_CLASS`, in `src/staff/login-notice.tsx`, which `cn` merges over the copied Button's default size, so that the Button does not change, and which keeps the Button's own `touch:h-11`, so that the buttons take 44 px on a touch screen (#362), and which also holds `w-full sm:w-auto`, so that the buttons fill the width under `sm`, the log-out button inside its `<form>` included, where `LoginNotice` stacks the actions in a column, `flex-col sm:flex-row sm:flex-wrap` (#362; decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #362, for Gilles to review, since the column alone stretches a link and not a button inside a form), the width of the block of at most 400 px that holds them and not of the column, so that at 639 px they are 400 px wide where the not-found and error pages' action, which sits in the column itself, takes 607 (the block's `max-w-100` read in `login-notice.tsx`, and the review of d63d8ae's measures at 639 px; decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #362, for Gilles to review, since #362 says only that the actions take « toute la largeur »), and which `LoginLink`, in `src/staff/login-link.tsx`, gives the `default` link to `/login` that each of the three pages draws (#236). Each button carries a lucide icon before its text, `aria-hidden` and marked `data-icon="inline-start"`, which the copied Button's classes already make room for (`src/ui/button.tsx:23`): `Plus` on « Ajouter », `X` on a blocked word's removal, `ArrowLeft` on « Revenir aux plus récents » and « Page précédente » and `ArrowRight` on « Plus anciens » and « Page suivante » (#322), `LogIn` on « Se connecter avec Discord » and « Se reconnecter », which #136 proposed where #129 named none, `LogOut` on « Se déconnecter », `RotateCcw` on « Réessayer », `Search` on « Chercher » (#176), `ListFilter` on « Filtrer » (#229), `SlidersHorizontal` on the audit log's « Filtres » (#359, #393), `Timer` on « Exclure temporairement » and `TimerOff` on « Lever l’exclusion temporaire » (#179), `UserX` on « Expulser » (#180), `Hammer` on « Bannir » and `UserCheck` on « Lever le bannissement » (#181), and `Check` on the « Changer » of the dialog that changes the action of a word (#326). While « Ajouter » is `pending`, a `LoaderCircle` takes its icon's place, and spins only under `motion-safe` (**Motion**) (#129 and #136, in place of #53's buttons without icons). `error.tsx`'s « Réessayer » takes `RotateCcw` too, and the not-found page's « Revenir à l’accueil », a `default` link, `ArrowLeft` (#61). Removing a blocked word asks for no confirmation: adding it back undoes it, and the audit log keeps both. Nor does changing its action, which another change undoes (#326). Nor does lifting a timeout or a ban, while a kick and a ban do (#221, #235): their red « Expulser » and « Bannir » open a dialog (**Forms**), and keep their text with no « … », which this section keeps for a pending button, and the dialog offers « Annuler », `outline`, then its confirmation, « Expulser » or « Bannir », red through `RED_BUTTON_CLASS` too and with the same icon, where the captures of #239 draw it without one and the ban's trigger as « Bannir… » (#235). Under `touch:` every Button is 44 px high whatever its size and whatever `h-*` a caller passes unprefixed, since `touch:h-11` sits beside them in the copied Button and the compiled CSS puts `.touch\:h-11` after `.h-9\.5`, at the same specificity (`.next/static/chunks/*.css` of `pnpm build`, offsets 81,985 and 12,317, 2026-11-03), and a throwaway run of `e2e/responsive.test.ts` with the pages' fixmes off listed no « Chercher », which sets `h-9.5`, among the controls under 44 px at 360 px (**Responsive**, #357): the 38 and 40 px that this section gives are the desktop's.
- **Forms.** A form's client leaf calls `useActionState` with `null` as its initial state, and hands `state` and `pending` to a component whose output follows from its props alone. That component always renders a `role="alert"` region, empty for `null`, which the field points to through `aria-describedby`, with the message of `{ ok: false }`, and a list's and the sanctions' also render a `role="status"` one, for their pending text (below). The regions exist before their text does, since a live region inserted with its text already in is announced unreliably (inferred, not checked with a screen reader). The submit button is `disabled` while `pending`; a lone form's button then reads a noun with an ellipsis, such as « Ajout… ». A list has one client leaf, which takes the items as props and hands them, with `state` and `pending`, to the list's component, and one pair of regions, outside its items, since an item's leaf would leave with it once a removal lands; each item keeps its own forms, such as a blocked word's cross, with a hidden `change` of `remove` and a hidden `word`, and its pencil, with a hidden `change` of `action` and the same `word` (#326). While one is pending, every cross and every pencil is `disabled` and the status region reads « Envoi en cours… », where it read « Retrait en cours… » for the cross alone (#326). A failure's message sits in an element of its own inside the alert region, `WriteFailureMessage`'s, in `src/ui/` since the sanctions show theirs too (#179), keyed by the message, so that a new message enters again, while the same one twice in a row keeps its element and does not (inferred from React's keys): in `destructive`, warmed until it holds 4.5:1 on `background`, after a `CircleAlert`, `aria-hidden`, fading in through `tw-animate-css`'s classes and sliding down by `0.25rem` only with motion (**Motion**). « Retrait en cours… » takes neither icon nor color, nor any animation. A record's sanctions sit in the card « Sanctions », on `card`, bordered, its `h2` over a line in `border` as the card « Détails » has it, as the mockup draws it (M:474-478). For a member, they sit under shadcn/ui's Tabs on Base UI, which `pnpm exec shadcn add tabs` copied (#234), each tab named as its heading was: « Exclusion temporaire », with the timeout's two forms, one that times the member out and one that lifts the timeout, shown only while it runs (#179), « Expulsion », with the kick's (#180), and, for a staff member who bans, « Bannissement », with the ban's (#181), where the mockup names the first « Exclusion » and shows the third to all (#221, #234). Base UI keeps the open tab, the first at each load, and each panel stays mounted, `hidden` and `inert` while its tab is closed, so that what is typed in a form outlives a move to another tab (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #234, for Gilles to review). A tab's label wraps where the card is too narrow for the three, as at 360 px, each tab as wide as its label first (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #234, for Gilles to review). To that staff member, the record of an account absent from the Discord server offers the ban alone, without the direct message, after the mockup's sentence that only a ban remains possible (M:557-567), and that of a banned account the ban's lifting alone, after a notice « Compte banni », which says a ban has no end and only the staff can lift it, both with no tabs (#181, #234). Each sentence the mockup adds stays, in `muted-foreground`: the kick's, that the account can come back through an invite, and the ban's, that it cannot until the ban is lifted (#234). The reason of a timeout, a kick and a member's ban shows « Écris le motif tel que le membre le lira. » as its placeholder, where the mockup does, which a lift's reason and an absent account's ban, whom no direct message reaches, do not (#234). Like a list, they share one client leaf, one action and one pair of regions, after the tabs; each form says which change it makes through a hidden `change` field, from which the action's domain function times out, kicks, bans or lifts the ban, every button that sends one, or opens the dialog that confirms one (below), is `disabled` while any form is pending, while the tabs still open, since Base UI moves the selection off a disabled tab to the first enabled one, or to none when every tab is disabled (`node_modules/@base-ui/react/tabs/root/TabsRoot.js:160-168`, `:211-234`), which would close the open panel during the send and leave every panel closed after it (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #234, for Gilles to review, against the letter of #234's « Tous les boutons »), and the status region then reads « Envoi en cours… », with neither icon nor color (#179, #234). The regions sit after the tabs since the lift's form leaves once its write lands, and take no room while empty, through `not-empty:mt-4`, where the section's gap had left about 48 px under the card (#233, #234). A running timeout shows, at the top of its tab, a notice « Exclusion temporaire en cours », then « Jusqu’au » and its end through `ParisTime`, which holds the lift's form, its reason and « Lever l’exclusion temporaire », sent with no dialog (#234; **Theme** says its colors). The section is `hidden` while it offers no sanction, as once a kick lands, whose toast says so, and the leaf stays mounted (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #227, where #180 kept « Sanctions » and the kick's message until the staff member left the record). The timeout's duration is a group of native radio buttons, as are the messages a ban deletes, « Aucun » checked by default, and its direct message, like the kick's and the ban's, a native checkbox, checked by default, rather than Base UI's, since React resets a form through the DOM (**Keeping what was typed**) and neither Base UI's Checkbox nor its RadioGroup listens for a form's `reset` (`grep -i reset` in `node_modules/@base-ui/react/checkbox/root/CheckboxRoot.js` and `radio-group/`, 2026-10-28), so its state would outlive the reset (inferred). The timeout's radio buttons are drawn as the mockup's seven buttons by their labels (M:500-504), each `<input>` covering its label with no appearance of its own, so that a click anywhere on the label reaches it, the label taking `primary` while it `has-checked` and the focus ring while it `has-focus-visible`, and « 1 heure » is checked by default, so that a successful timeout's reset checks it again while a refused one keeps the duration chosen, as `e2e/sanctions.test.ts` proves (#221, #234). The messages a ban deletes keep the native look, which neither #234 nor the mockup draws (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #234, for Gilles to review). Under `touch`, each duration is 44 px high, and each message a ban deletes, like « Prévenir en message privé », stands in a label of 44 px at least through `CHOICE_CLASS`, the messages in `CHOICE_GROUP_CLASS`, of `src/sanctions/sanction-form.ts`, a grid of two columns under `sm` and of columns of 170 px at least from `sm`, with 8 px between the columns and none between the rows, while at 1,024 px and over with a mouse they keep the wrapping row they had (#361; the gaps (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #361, for Gilles to review, since #361 gives the columns and not the gaps)). A member the staff member cannot sanction shows, in « Sanctions », the one reason why, and no form (#179), and a member who cannot be timed out, such as one with Administrator, shows why under the tab « Exclusion temporaire », and the kick's form under its own (#180, #234). A kick and a ban, of a member or of an account absent from the Discord server, ask for a confirmation, and no other write does (#221, #235): their form holds no submit button, and its red « Expulser » or « Bannir » is the trigger of shadcn/ui's Dialog on Base UI, which `pnpm exec shadcn add dialog` copied, and opens it with no check beforehand, since the domain stays the one validator (**No browser validation**). The dialog, which `SanctionConfirmationDialog` in `src/sanctions/` renders around its content, `SanctionConfirmation`, asks « Expulser {nom} ? » or « Bannir {nom} ? », by the name the record's header shows, which `SanctionOptions` carries, says « Le compte quitte le serveur Discord tout de suite. Il pourra revenir par une invitation. » or « Le compte ne pourra plus rejoindre le serveur Discord tant que le staff ne lève pas le bannissement. » as its description, then offers « Annuler » and the confirmation (**Buttons**). Its title, in the headings' font at 23 px, and its description sit beside a `TriangleAlert`, `aria-hidden`, on a tint of `destructive`, in a dialog at most 440 px wide, and its two buttons sit side by side at every width, on its own surface rather than on the copied `DialogFooter`'s band of `muted/50`, as M:579-602, which #235 cites, and the captures of #239 draw them, where the copied `DialogFooter` stacks them under `sm`, the confirmation above « Annuler » (`e2e/sanctions.test.ts`, at 360 px), in two equal columns under `sm` since #357. The copied Backdrop stays, `bg-black/10` with `backdrop-blur-xs`, where the captures draw a veil with no blur, since #221 gives `--overlay` to the copied classes. Base UI keeps its open state, as the Sheet's, focuses « Annuler » as it opens and gives the focus back to the trigger as it closes, on Escape too (throwaway Playwright probe, 2026-10-29). It portals the dialog out of the form, so the confirmation, a `DialogClose` of `type="submit"`, sends the open tab's form through its `form` attribute, which names that form's `id`, `kick-form` or `ban-form`, each unique on a record, which shows one ban form at most, then closes the dialog, while « Annuler » closes it and writes nothing (`e2e/sanctions.test.ts`). With no submit button in the form, Enter on its checkbox sends nothing (the same probe). A missing reason comes back, once confirmed, in the alert region, and the form keeps what was typed (**Keeping what was typed**). `kickConfirmation` and `banConfirmation`, beside their forms, give a `Confirmation` from the account's name, its form's `id`, title, description, label and icon, which both the trigger and the content read, and `sanction-confirmation.test.tsx` renders the content of each inside a `Dialog` root, since a portal renders nothing on the server (`node_modules/@base-ui/react/floating-ui-react/components/FloatingPortal.js:89`, `:201`). The blocked words' form is a `card` labelled « Ajouter un mot ou une expression », whose field shows « Ex. : free nitro » as its placeholder, with neither a counter nor a lower-casing: the domain stays the one validator, and the word goes as typed (#221, #228). Under the field sits a native choice, « Action », drawn by `BlockedWordActionSelect` through shadcn/ui's NativeSelect, with its label above it and « Ajouter » beside it, which offers only the actions the staff member's roles allow, « Bloquer seulement » alone to one who holds no role of `MODERATION_ROLE_IDS`, and has that one chosen by default (#325). Under `sm`, « Ajouter » goes under the choice and both take the form's whole width (**Responsive**, #358). Its labels « Exclusion 1 heure » and « Exclusion 1 jour » stand though `GLOSSARY.md` gives **Timeout** « _Avoid_: mute […], exclusion (alone) »: they name a duration, as « Exclu jusqu’au <date> » names an end and #231's « Exclusions » a type, so they read as a timeout, and `GLOSSARY.md` does not change (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #325, for Gilles to review). It is a `<select>` and not Base UI's Select, as the timeout's radio buttons are not Base UI's (below), and React updates no `defaultValue` of a `<select>` once it is mounted, so the form keeps the action of a refused word through a `key` that follows the action sent, and a word that lands mounts the choice again on « Bloquer seulement »: without the `key`, the choice went back to « Bloquer seulement » after a refusal, which fails `e2e/blocked-words.test.ts` (2026-11-02, #325). The form depends on the staff member, so the page puts it under a `<Suspense>` whose fallback is `AddBlockedWordFormSkeleton`, in its own shape, rather than a form that would lose what was typed, as `/records` does for its search (#325). The list sits under its `h2`, « La liste », and its count, « 1 mot interdit » or « N mots interdits », as a grid of tiles at least 200 px wide, each word in the monospace font and cut with an ellipsis where it does not fit, over the name of its action in `muted-foreground`, the one that only blocks included, while its tile gives the word whole in its `title` and the DOM keeps it whole, and with a cross only where the staff member may use the word's action, so that a word that times out shows none to one who holds no role of `MODERATION_ROLE_IDS` (`mayUse`, #325), the tile 48 px high on two lines where it was 40 px high on one, since the action's name beside the word and the cross would leave the word little of a 200 px tile (inferred; decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #325, for Gilles to review); an empty list says « Aucun mot interdit pour l’instant. » in place of the count, where the mockup counts « 0 mots interdits » (M:910), in an `EmptyListFrame`, a dashed frame bordered in `input` (#228). The pencil that changes a word's action sits before the cross, in a `div` that holds both, only where the staff member may use the word's action and at least one other (`mayChange`, #326), which today is a moderator, whatever the word's action: a `ghost` button, `size="icon-sm"`, in `muted-foreground`, named « Changer l’action de « foo » » in `sr-only` after a hidden `Pencil`, which is also the title of the dialog it opens. That dialog is shadcn/ui's Dialog, the one the confirmation of a kick uses, with no confirmation over it: `BlockedWordActionDialog` renders the tile's form, of `id` `change-action-form-<index>`, around the Dialog, whose content, `BlockedWordActionChange`, holds the title, the choice « Action », the native choice of the add form, standing on the word's current action and offering only the actions the staff member may use, then « Annuler », `outline`, and « Changer », `default`, after a hidden `Check`. Base UI portals the dialog out of the tile, so its choice and its « Changer », a `DialogClose` of `type="submit"`, belong to the form through their `form` attribute, as the confirmation of a kick does: « Changer » sends the form then closes the dialog, « Annuler » and Escape close it and write nothing, and focus goes back to the pencil. The choice is mounted with the dialog, so it stands on the word's action each time the dialog opens (`e2e/blocked-words.test.ts`). The tile keeps its place and its key, the word, and only the name of its action changes, which `slide-into-place` is inferred to show without animation (inferred, not tried). A change that lands says so in a toast, and a refusal in the list's alert region, with the dialog closed (`e2e/blocked-words.test.ts`). Beside « La liste » and its count, and under them below `sm`, the filter is a GET form, `BlockedWordFilterForm`, through `next/form` with `prefetch={false}` as the form of the list of accounts of `/records`, which sends its one field, `query`, to `/blocked-words`: a field named « Filtrer la liste » in `sr-only`, which shows it as its placeholder after a `Search`, `aria-hidden`, as the mockup draws it with no visible label (M:213-216), and the button « Filtrer », as #229 asks, where the mockup filters at each keystroke and has none (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #229, for Gilles to review, since #229 names the field and the button alone). The parameter is `query`, as #229 reads « comme `/records?query=` », which also leaves « filter » to AutoMod, which filters the messages (ADR 0002) through the rule's `keyword_filter` (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #229, for Gilles to review). `filterBlockedWords`, in `src/blocked-words/`, keeps the words that contain the text searched, whatever its case, through `toLowerCase()`, as `add-blocked-word.ts` compares words, so that « éCL » finds « Éclair » while « eclair » does not, since #229 leaves out the case alone, and leaves out the spaces around that text, as the mockup does (M:839), while an invalid `query`, blank or repeated, counts as absent (**Validation**) (`src/blocked-words/filter-blocked-words.test.ts`). Its result is a union: every word, with no query, or the words that pass a query, with their total. The page reads `searchParams` in the list's `<Suspense>`, as `/records` does, so its static shell stays, and filters the words the gauge, the list and the other AutoMod rules share for the list alone: the gauge counts every word (#229). With a query, the count reads « k sur N », and a list that shows no word says « Aucun mot interdit ne contient « … ». » in the dashed frame, an empty rule included, counted « 0 sur 0 », as #229 writes both (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #229, for Gilles to review, where the mockup says « Aucun mot interdit pour l’instant. » for an empty rule, M:912), and « Tout afficher », after the count, is a `TextLink` to `/blocked-words`, which does not prefetch (**Navigation**), drawn as the audit log's link to a record. The list and its skeleton share `LIST_HEADER_CLASSES` and `FILTER_FORM_WIDTH_CLASSES`, in `src/blocked-words/blocked-word-list-layout.ts`, each in a `cn()` (**Classes**). The form is keyed by the query, so that its field, which keeps what was typed across navigations, empties once « Tout afficher » lands: without the key, `e2e/blocked-words.test.ts` found « xyz » there (2026-10-29). A write keeps the query, since its `refresh()` renders the URL again, so a word added that does not pass it stays out of the list, though its toast says it landed (`e2e/blocked-words.test.ts`, #229). Beside the page's header where both fit, and under its sentence where they do not (**Responsive**, #358), the capacity gauge, a `group` that its overline « Capacité » names, shows the count of words in large over « / 3 000 mots » and a 3 px bar, `aria-hidden` since the numbers say the same, from `MAX_WORDS_PER_RULE` times the number of actions the panel offers, 3 for every staff member whatever their roles, as Gilles decided (Gilles, 2026-11-02, on #325), with no call to Discord of its own (#221, #228, #325); the bar fills in strict proportion, empty for an empty rule, where the mockup never draws it under 1 % (M:903) (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #228, for Gilles to review, since #228 says neither). The page hands `BlockedWordsView`, in `src/blocked-words/`, the gauge, the form, the list and the other AutoMod rules, each under its `<Suspense>`, and only wires, so that what the page shows is tested through `react-dom/server` (**Entry points only wire**), as `RecordView` is. Its box of 1,080 px is centered, as the mockup's (M:174). The form and the list fill the page's main column, and, where a container of 780 px holds both columns, the side column sits beside them and sticks 24 px from the top as the page scrolls, and goes under the list below that (`e2e/blocked-words.test.ts`, #228): the card « Exceptions », on `muted`, a `section` that its overline names, then the other AutoMod rules, each opening its keywords as chips through a `<details>` whose `summary` gives its name and « N mots-clés », under the overline « Autres règles AutoMod » and « Elles se modifient dans Discord. ». That overline labels its `section` where an `h2` named it before #228, as the mockup draws it, and the side column is a `div` rather than an `<aside>`, so that the sidebar stays the page's one `complementary` landmark, which end to end reads (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #228, for Gilles to review). Each blocked word of the list sits in a `<ViewTransition>` keyed by the word, whose `enter` is `highlight-enter`, whose `exit` is `fade-exit`, whose `update` is `slide-into-place`, and whose `default` is `none`, so a word just added lights up for a moment, one removed fades out, and each word the write moves slides to its new place, only with motion (**Motion**), with nothing shared between the form's leaf and the list's: the `refresh()` that ends the write lands in the transition of `useActionState`, which starts the view transition (`e2e/blocked-words.test.ts`, #138). The words that move are those after the one added or removed, since the form shows no message once a word lands (#227), each to the next cell of the grid or to the one before, across the end of a row too (#228); a word that keeps its place does not animate, though React names it whenever its cross changes (**Gotchas**) (`e2e/blocked-words.test.ts`, #166). A refused word's message, which pushes the list down, moves it with no animation (throwaway Playwright test, 2026-10-28), since that write leaves the list alone, so that React names no word (inferred from React's code). The list keeps its `<ul>` while it holds no word, hidden by `empty:hidden`, so that the first word added and the last removed animate too (**Gotchas**). A write that lands says so in a toast rather than beside its form (#221, #227): the leaf that holds `useActionState` passes its state to `useSuccessToast`, in `src/ui/`, whose effect adds a toast for each new `{ ok: true }`, with the message as the toast's title and `success` as its type, and none for a failure, nor for a state it has already seen, as when `<Activity>` shows a page again and reruns its effects (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/preserving-ui-state.md:70`): a refused word or timeout adds no toast, and coming back to the blocked words after a write adds no second one, as `e2e/blocked-words.test.ts` and `e2e/sanctions.test.ts` prove, both red with the hook's two guards taken out (2026-10-29). The toast is added once the result commits, not as the action returns: added from the action, before the commit, it left the first blocked word added without its view transition (`e2e/blocked-words.test.ts`, 2026-10-29), since Base UI keeps its toasts in a store read through `useSyncExternalStore` (`@base-ui/utils` 0.5.0, `store/useStore.js:25`), whose updates React renders synchronously (inferred). Its commit then waits for the list's view transition to end, so the toast enters right after the words' animations (a throwaway Playwright probe that logged each frame, 2026-10-29). A failure's message stays in the alert region, since a toast leaves after 5 s and that message says what to do next, and « Envoi en cours… » stays in the status region, which the blocked words' list reads for a removal and a change of action alike since #326, where it read « Retrait en cours… » (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #227). The toast is shadcn/ui's Toast on Base UI, which `pnpm exec shadcn add toast` copied with no new package, rather than Sonner, which `shadcn add sonner` would bring with `next-themes` (#227). `src/app/(staff)/layout.tsx` renders its `Toaster`, the provider and its viewport, after the page, so that a toast outlives a move between sections (`e2e/blocked-words.test.ts`), and the login pages have none. Base UI's settings stay: a toast leaves after 5 s, and 3 show at most (`node_modules/@base-ui/react/toast/provider/ToastProvider.js:23-24`, 1.9.0). The viewport is a `region` named « Notifications », already French, in `aria-live="polite"` (`toast/viewport/ToastViewport.js:180-184`), which F6 focuses, and focusing or hovering it pauses the toasts' timers (`:61-67`, `:129-130`). Each toast is a `dialog` named by its title, the message (`toast/root/ToastRoot.js:423-427`), after the copied `CircleCheck` of its `success` type, which takes no color, and before « Fermer », which Base UI hides from assistive technologies until the toast is expanded or the button focused (`toast/close/ToastClose.js:49`). Base UI renders the title as an `h2` (`toast/title/ToastTitle.js:36`), which would put a heading in the system's font where **Fonts and icons** gives headings EB Garamond, so the copied `ToastList` renders it as a `<p>`, through `render={<p />}`: the description would name no dialog, which 4.1.2 of **Accessibility** asks of one, and the copied classes draw it in `muted-foreground`, where the captures of #239 draw the message in `foreground` (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #227, for Gilles to review). A toast bears no `view-transition-name`, so a write's view transition stays the words' (`e2e/blocked-words.test.ts`).
- **Keeping what was typed.** React resets a `<form action>` on every submission, a failed write included, once the commit has updated the DOM (`node_modules/react-dom/cjs/react-dom-client.development.js:9326-9344`, `:16128-16129`, react-dom 19.3.1). So a failed write hands back each field as typed (**Write results**), and the field takes it as its `defaultValue`. The reset should then put the word back after a failure and empty the field after a success: that is inferred from the order above, and the blocked-word flow of **End to end** checks it.
- **Errors in forms.** An action that throws under `useActionState` rejects its state, which React throws again while rendering (`react-dom-client.development.js:8832-8845`, `:8948-8966`), so `error.tsx` takes it like a failed read.
- **No browser validation.** No `required`, `maxLength` or `pattern`: the browser would show its own bubbles instead of the domain's French message, and `maxLength` would silently cut a pasted word. The domain is the one validator (**Errors**). Nothing is built to work without JavaScript.
- **Suspense fallbacks** are skeletons in the shape of what loads, from shadcn/ui's Skeleton, one in each domain folder (#139): `BlockedWordListSkeleton`, a bar for « La liste » and one for its count, beside one for the filter's field and button (#229), over six tiles in the list's grid, each a bar for its word over a shorter one for its action, beside a square for its cross (#325), and `BlockedWordCapacitySkeleton`, the capacity gauge's overline, number and bar, `aria-hidden` and no `role="status"`, since the list's skeleton already says that the blocked words are loading: the page reads them once, through one promise that the `<Suspense>` of the gauge, of the list and of the other AutoMod rules share, as the layout shares the member's (#228); `AddBlockedWordFormSkeleton`, the form's card, a bar for its label and one for its field, then, beside a bar for its button, a bar for the label « Action » over one for its choice, which holds no field, so that nothing typed is lost when the form replaces it, and which the form needs since the actions it offers depend on the staff member's roles (#325), and which wears the form's layout classes, so that it stacks and grows as the form does (**Responsive**, #358); `AuditLogSkeleton`, which hands `LogSkeleton` of `src/ui/` its widths and its form, in the shape of the audit log's filter by type, a pill for each type (#231), then of its form, `AuditLogFilterFormSkeleton`, a bar for each of its two labels and its two fields and one for its button, with nothing to type in, laid out by the form's own classes (#321), under `md` the bar of the button that folds them in their place (#359), then of its days (#230): two days, each a bar for its date before its line, over a card of rows, each a bar for its time, the tile of its icon, a bar for its title, and, for two of them, one for a sanction's account and reason, then a circle for the member's avatar beside the bar of their name (#141), laid out by the classes of `src/ui/log-layout.ts` that `LogDay`, `LogRow` and `LogSkeleton` take, and by `FILTER_FIELD_CLASSES` of `src/audit-log/audit-log-layout.ts` for the filter's fields, each written in a `cn()` so that Prettier sorts it (**Classes**), so each of its rows takes the real row's grid, and the skeleton's time, tile, title and staff member sit on one axis from `sm` up and its tile, time and staff member on a first line above the title under it (#319): a throwaway Playwright probe over `holdDiscord` measured its five rows at 1,280, 640, 639 and 360 px (2026-11-02), and from 640 px the centers of the time's cell, the tile, the title's block, the staff member's cell and the circle all sat on the row's middle, while at 639 px and 360 px those of the time, the tile, the staff member and the circle shared one axis, with the title's block starting 10 px under the tile's bottom, though no test of the repository measures them, the skeleton sharing the real row's classes (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #319, for Gilles to review); and `StaffMemberIdentitySkeleton`, a circle for the member's avatar and a bar for their name (#224), with the shorter bar for the line under it drawn by `StaffHeader`, as the fallback of that line (#320), in the sidebar's foot, in the bar, which shows the circle alone (**Navigation**), and in its Sheet, which hold the name in a `<div>` rather than a `<p>`, since the Skeleton is a `<div>` (#140); `AccountSearchFormSkeleton`, the shape of the form of the list of accounts, the label and the field of the search, as tall as the field of #232, then a label and a select for each of the six choices, wrapping as the form does, then the button, until the staff check and the roles' read are done: it holds no field, so that nothing typed into a fallback is lost when the form replaces it, as a fallback form lost it in 1 end-to-end run of 60 (2026-10-28, #324); `FoundAccountsSkeleton`, a bar for the count, then a card of rows, each a circle for an account's avatar beside two bars and a pill for its standing badge, which goes under them under `sm` as the badge does (#232), and which stands for the list, a count over a card of rows, with nothing for the navigation under it (#322, #324); `SupportTicketArchivesSkeleton`, in the shape of the support tickets' page, a pill for each of the five links of the kinds, then the bars of the filters' form, `TicketArchiveFilterFormSkeleton`, a label over a field for each of the seven controls and one for « Filtrer », or one bar for the button that folds them under `md`, which wear the pills' size and a control's height from the constants of `src/ui/filter-layout.ts` that the pills and the form take too (#386), then a bar for the count over a card of three entries, each a bar for its request kind, two pairs of a bar for the label, a bar for the name, 44 px high under `touch`, and a bar for the date, then a bar for the reason, from the layout constants of `support-ticket-archives-layout.ts` and the card's `TICKET_ARCHIVE_ENTRIES_CLASSES`, so that the content replaces it with the same layout, though not at the same height, which follows the reasons (#385); and `OpenSupportTicketsSkeleton`, in `src/support-tickets/`, the shape of the tickets in progress, a bar for the count over two cards of the list, each a bar for its request kind and one for its message count, three pairs of a bar for a label and a bar for a name or a date, 44 px high under `touch`, a bar for the last message and one for the link, from the layout constants of `open-support-tickets-layout.ts` that the cards take, so that the content replaces it with the same layout, though not at the same height, which follows the extract (#388); and `RecordDetailsSkeleton`, in the shape of a record's header, a circle for its avatar, 68 px and 56 px under `sm`, a bar for its name, a pill for its standing badge and a bar for its `@username`, over the rule, then, in `RecordColumns`, its card « Détails », a bar for its title and one for its overline over the bars of its entries, each a bar for its term beside a bar for its value, or above it under `sm`, and the heading of its history over two entries of its timeline, each with its diamond and two bars, which every record shows (#177, #233), then the heading of the tickets over a card of two rows (#197), in the record's two columns where both fit, with nothing in the right one, since the skeleton cannot know whether the record will offer a sanction, and in one column below, but not « Sanctions », which only the record of a member shows, or, to a staff member who bans, that of an account absent or banned (#176, #179, #181, #227). Each but `BlockedWordCapacitySkeleton` is a `role="status"` element whose one text, `sr-only`, names what loads: « Chargement des mots interdits… », « Chargement du formulaire… », « Chargement du journal d’audit… », « Chargement de ton nom… », « Chargement de la recherche… », « Chargement des comptes… », « Chargement des tickets en cours… », « Chargement des archives de tickets… », « Chargement de la fiche… ». Its pulse, the copied Skeleton's `animate-pulse`, stops under reduced motion through the `motion-reduce:animate-none` each skeleton passes, as the Sheet's offset does (**Copied code**, **Motion**). `SuspenseWithSkeleton`, in `src/ui/`, wraps the skeleton in a `<ViewTransition>` whose `exit` is `fade-exit` and the content in one whose `enter` is `content-enter`, both with `default` set to `none`, so that the content enters in place of the skeleton (**Motion**): on a full load of `/blocked-words`, of `/audit-log`, of `/support-tickets` and of a record (#233, #385), the skeleton fades out and the content fades in, rising by `0.5rem` only with motion, and the pulse runs only with motion. `e2e/skeletons.test.ts` proves it while `holdDiscord` holds Discord's answers back, and checks the skeletons under reduced motion with axe and at 360 px (**End to end**): it failed with the `<ViewTransition>`s of `SuspenseWithSkeleton` or a skeleton's `motion-reduce:animate-none` taken out (2026-10-28). The pages use it; the navigation's member does not, and replaces its skeleton with no animation, since a `<ViewTransition>` in the layout leaves an animation behind at every navigation (**Gotchas**), where #129 and #139 had planned one around every fallback and its content (Gilles, 2026-10-27, on #139). This replaced #53's text alone, with no skeleton and no spinner. The groups « Modération » and « Support », the other AutoMod rules, the blocked words' count in the navigation and the numbers of the support tickets' pills are the four fallbacks that are no skeleton: the first three are nothing, since most staff members never see the first group and the second waits with it, so that it does not move when the first arrives (#176, #224, #385), the rules come last in their column, so that nothing moves when they arrive (#228), and the count delays nothing, showing once Discord answers (#226); the fourth is the pills themselves without their numbers, which the counts' searches, read after the list, fill in when Discord answers (#386). An empty list says so in one sentence, such as « Aucun mot interdit pour l’instant. ».
- **Motion**, as Gilles decided while grilling #129 on 2026-10-27, takes no library (**Framework**). React's `<ViewTransition>`, which React 19.3.1 exports and the App Router runs with no configuration (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/view-transitions.md:50`), and which renders from a Server Component as `<Suspense>` does (the staff pages, and the crossfade test of `e2e/staff-header.test.ts`, 2026-10-27), animates what a transition changes. Each staff page wraps its `<main>` in one whose `enter` and `exit` are `section-crossfade` and whose `default` is `none`, so moving between sections fades the old content out and the new one in, and a `refresh()` leaves the page alone (a throwaway test of a write ran no view transition, 2026-10-27). The page holds it rather than `src/app/(staff)/layout.tsx`, which stays mounted across sections, so that `enter` and `exit` fire (the same guide, `:268`). React keeps the rest of the page, the sidebar included but for the current section's bar, which its own `<ViewTransition>` names with `share` set to `slide` and `default` to `none` (#137, #224), out of the transition: it sets the root's `view-transition-name` to `none` and hides the root's snapshot (`node_modules/next/dist/compiled/react-dom/cjs/react-dom-client.development.js:16392-16411`). `src/ui/globals.css` holds, after the theme, the rules of the `::view-transition-*` pseudo-elements for six classes a `<ViewTransition>` can name: `section-crossfade`; `content-enter`, which fades in and rises by `0.5rem`, for what a `<Suspense>` streams in place of its skeleton (#139); `highlight-enter`, which fades in over a tint of `primary` at 15%, held for half its length so that it shows (Playwright screenshots of a throwaway page, light and dark, 2026-10-27), for a blocked word just added, and `fade-exit`, which fades out, for one removed (#138) and for a skeleton that leaves (#139); `slide`, which times the group that moves the sidebar's bar to the current section (#137, #224); and `slide-into-place`, which times as `slide` does the group that moves a blocked word to its new place, and shows the word's new snapshot at once, with neither a fade nor the browser's default crossfade, so that a word React names without moving it does not animate (**Forms**, **Gotchas**, #166). `tw-animate-css`, which `globals.css` imports, and Base UI's `data-starting-style` and `data-ending-style` animate the Sheet, and any dialog or popover, as the copied components write them (https://ui.shadcn.com/r/styles/base-nova/sheet.json, read 2026-10-27), and `tw-animate-css` alone brings in a failed write's message (**Forms**, #138). The Toast's copied classes slide a toast in from below and out by 150% of its height, and restack the others, through `transition: transform 500ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1), opacity 500ms, height 150ms` (`src/ui/toast.tsx`, #227). CSS transitions animate hover and press. Each lasts 150 to 250 ms and eases out, but for the Toast's copied 500 ms, for the spinner of a pending button, which turns as Tailwind's `animate-spin` does, `spin 1s linear infinite` (`node_modules/tailwindcss/theme.css:438`, Tailwind 4.3.3), and only under `motion-safe` (#136), and for the pulse of a skeleton, the copied Skeleton's `animate-pulse`, `pulse 2s cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.6, 1) infinite` (the same file, `:440`), which stops under reduced motion (**Suspense fallbacks**, #139). Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, what moves only fades, or does not animate: there, no `::view-transition-group` animates, so an element named in both renders swaps in place, and `content-enter` only fades, since it rises under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference` alone; on a throwaway page holding the five classes, Chromium then ran only opacity, `highlight-enter`'s tint and the blend mode of a swapping pair, where with motion it also moved `slide`'s group and raised `content-enter` (2026-10-27). Between sections, the bar's group moves down or up the sidebar only with motion, through its `transform` alone since every bar has the same size, and under reduced motion the bar swaps in place (the crossfade test of `e2e/staff-header.test.ts`, 2026-10-27, then on the sidebar, 2026-10-29, #224). So does a blocked word that a write moves: it slides only with motion, and under reduced motion swaps in place (`e2e/blocked-words.test.ts`, #166). The bar's Sheet, whose copied classes slide it in and out by `2.5rem`, only fades under reduced motion, through `motion-reduce:translate-x-0!`, important since the copied offset sits behind `data-[side=right]` and `data-starting-style` (end to end, 2026-10-27; #137). The audit log's filter Sheet, which slides up by `2.5rem` from the bottom, only fades under reduced motion the same way, through `motion-reduce:translate-y-0!` on `FilterSheetContent` (#359), which also takes `motion-reduce:translate-x-0!`, since the Sheet of `/records` slides in from the right from `sm`, by `2.5rem`, through `sm:` overrides of the bottom Sheet's classes, all important since the copied classes sit behind `data-[side=bottom]` and `data-starting-style` (#360; the `!` overrides are decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #360, for Gilles to review): `e2e/audit-log.test.ts` lists the properties that its transitions run when it opens, `opacity` alone under reduced motion and `opacity` and `translate` with motion, as the bar's menu does, and `translate` came too, in 4 runs of 4, on a throwaway build without the class (`pnpm test:e2e e2e/audit-log.test.ts -g "fades in and slides up" --repeat-each 4`, 2026-11-03). `e2e/account-filters.test.ts` proves the same for `/records`, at 639 px, from the bottom, and at 700 px, from the right, and `translate` came under reduced motion at 700 px on a throwaway build without `motion-reduce:translate-x-0!` (`pnpm test:e2e e2e/account-filters.test.ts -g "slides in only with motion"`, 2026-11-03). The confirmation of a kick or a ban, whose copied classes fade it in and out and zoom it from and to 95% through `tw-animate-css`'s `animate-in` and `animate-out`, only fades under reduced motion, through the `motion-reduce:data-open:zoom-in-100` and `motion-reduce:data-closed:zoom-out-100` that `SanctionConfirmationDialog` passes, and `BlockedWordActionDialog`, which changes a word's action, with it (#326), both from `DIALOG_ONLY_FADES_WHEN_REDUCED` in `src/ui/dialog-motion.ts`, as `e2e/sanctions.test.ts` and `e2e/blocked-words.test.ts` prove by the dialog's opacity and scale at the start of its animation, which `e2e/dialog-opening.ts` reads for both, red without them for the sanctions' (2026-10-29, #235; the constant and the file are a move out of the component and out of that test, with no change to what it proves, #326). Under `sm` the dialog docks (**Responsive**) and slides up from the bottom edge instead of zooming, only with motion, and fades alone under reduced motion, which the same file reads as a `lift`, the distance in pixels between the dialog's center at the start of its opening and at its end: 0 at 1,280 px, where it zooms, positive at 360 px with motion and 0 under reduced motion (`e2e/sanctions.test.ts`, #357). Its fade and its zoom last the 100 ms its copied `duration-100` writes, under the 150 ms above, since this section has any dialog animate as the copied components write it, and #235 says nothing of its length (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #235, for Gilles to review). A toast only fades under reduced motion, through four classes the panel gives each toast of the copied `ToastList` (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #227): `motion-reduce:[transition:opacity_500ms]` has its transforms and its height change at once, `motion-reduce:data-starting-style:opacity-0` and `motion-reduce:data-ending-style:opacity-0` fade it in and out over the copied 500 ms, and `motion-reduce:data-ending-style:transform-none!`, important since the copied ending transforms sit behind `:not()`s and data attributes, keeps it in place as it fades out, where it would otherwise jump below the screen. With motion, a toast's enter and exit run only `transform`, and under reduced motion only `opacity` (`e2e/blocked-words.test.ts`, #227). A toast that leaves from behind another, under reduced motion, jumps under the frontmost one as it fades (inferred from the copied classes). A browser without the View Transitions API shows the change without the animation (the same guide, `:53`).
- **Avatars**, as Gilles decided while grilling #129 on 2026-10-27. A member shows with their avatar on the Discord server, else their Discord avatar, else their initials on `muted`, through shadcn/ui's Avatar on Base UI, which also shows the initials while the image loads and when it fails; an animated avatar shows still. The navigation's comes from the member `requireStaffMember` reads: `StaffMember` carries its `avatarUrl` (ADR 0005), which `memberAvatarUrl`, in `src/discord/`, builds from `DiscordApi`'s `guildId` and the member, `null` for a member without an avatar, and `MemberAvatar` draws it, or the first letter of at most two words of the name, in capitals, skipping the words that start with neither a letter nor a digit, so that « Lyra | Garde de Blancherive » gives « LG » where it gave « L| », while a name with no such word keeps the rule it had (decided by the agent on 2026-10-29, on #230, for Gilles to review, where the comment that raised « L| » on #230 counted only the words that start with a letter). The name sits beside it, so `MemberAvatar` is `aria-hidden`, initials included, and its image's `alt` is empty (#140). It sits in `src/ui/`, since the audit log draws it too, in every row, at 24 px, beside the member's name, after it at the row's right edge, under `sm` too, in the rows' first line, where it stood before the name under `sm` until #319 (#141, #230, #319; the side under `sm` was decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #319, for Gilles to review, as **Small screens** says), and so does the moderation section, beside each account the list shows, whether a search or a filter kept it, and its record's name, where an account that is not a member, banned or absent, shows its Discord avatar, which `userAvatarUrl` builds (#176). Every avatar of an account links to that account's record for a staff member who moderates, and to nothing for one who does not, in every section and every feature to come, but on that record itself: the link holds the avatar and the name beside it, since `MemberAvatar` is `aria-hidden`, and takes `prefetch={false}` (**Navigation**). The list's rows do, so do the audit log's rows, for the member who made the change, and so does the navigation's identity, in the sidebar, the bar and the menu (Gilles, 2026-10-30, while grilling the account list and the automatic sanctions; #320). `recordHrefForModerator`, in `src/records/record-href.ts`, is the one place of the rule: it gives `/records/{id}` to a staff member who moderates and an account with an ID, and `null` to anyone else, so that an author with no ID, such as the « AutoMod » of an automatic timeout (#327), gets no link; the list's rows take their path from `recordHref` beside it, as they are shown to those who moderate alone. `AccountLink`, in `src/ui/`, draws a `next/link` with `prefetch={false}` from the href it is given, and a `div` with the same classes from `null`, and knows nothing of records or rights, since `src/ui/` holds no domain logic; the audit log's author cell, and the navigation's identity in the sidebar and in the bar, are `AccountLink`s, and the audit log's account `TextLink` takes its href from `recordHrefForModerator` too, as does `ownRecordOnceRead`, which gives the navigation's (**Navigation**) (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #320, for Gilles to review). « AutoMod », the author of an automatic timeout in the audit log, has no avatar at all, not even its initials: its row draws the name alone, where `MemberAvatar` would draw a member, so that it does not pass for one, and the audit log looks up no avatar for it, its change carrying a `memberId` and an `avatarUrl` that are both `null`, where the entry's `user_id` is the member timed out and never the author (#327; decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #327, for Gilles to review, since #327 leaves « sans avatar » open between no image and no avatar). The audit log's comes from one `getMember` per distinct member of the page, made in parallel once the page's 50 changes are chosen: for a change made through the panel, which names its staff member only in its reason, `Name (ID) : …` (`src/audit-log/audit-log-reason.ts:12`, ADR 0004), the ID that `panelReasonOf` reads there when the entry's `user_id` is the application's (#210), and for a change made in Discord, the entry's `user_id`, since the audit log's `users` hold only the global avatar (#141). The name shown stays the reason's, as it was at the change, or for a change made in Discord the audit log's, and a member no longer on the Discord server, or whose lookup throws a `DiscordError`, shows the initials (**Read failures**). Images come straight from Discord's CDN, as `.webp` at `?size=64` without `animated=true`, which Discord's docs name only for the animated version (https://docs.discord.com/developers/reference, **Image Formatting**, read 2026-10-27), but for a record's, which is asked at `?size=128` (#246). `memberAvatarUrl` and `userAvatarUrl` take an `AvatarSize`, `64` or `128`, as their last parameter, `64` when it is left out so that no other caller changes, and `readRecord`, in `src/records/`, passes its `RECORD_AVATAR_SIZE`, 128, for a member, a banned account and an absent one (the union of two sizes and the default decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #246, for Gilles to review). Discord's docs say "Image size can be any power of two between 16 and 4096" (the same section, read 2026-11-02; the page shows no date of modification), which both sizes are within. A record's header draws its avatar at 68 px (#233), so a 68 px avatar on a screen of twice the density draws 136 device pixels, which the 128 leaves enlarged by a factor of 1.06 where the 64 gave 2.13, and 256 is the smallest size that never enlarges it, while the 56 px avatar of #361 draws 112 device pixels at that density, which the 128 never enlarges, and 168 at three times the density that many phones have, which it enlarges by a factor of 1.31 (inferred; #246 asks for 128, and the size a record's avatar is drawn at is out of its scope). Base UI's `Avatar.Image` renders the `<img>` once the image has loaded (`node_modules/@base-ui/react/avatar/image/AvatarImage.js:26`, `:49`, `:129`), so the panel writes none and needs no `next/image`, neither Vercel's image optimization nor a Route Handler of the panel's serves them, and the Content Security Policy allows `https://cdn.discordapp.com` in `img-src` (**Headers**). The server's render shows the initials only, so `src/discord/member-avatar-url.test.ts` proves the URL of each size, `src/staff/staff-member.test.ts` at the staff check, `src/audit-log/read-audit-log.test.ts` in the audit log and `src/records/read-record.test.ts` in a record, at 128 px where the others keep 64, and the image is proven end to end (**End to end**), but a record's, whose flows pose no avatar (`grep -i avatar e2e/records.test.ts`, 2026-11-02), so only its URL is proven.
- **Accessibility.** WCAG 2.2 AA (https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/, Recommendation of 2024-12-12, read 2026-10-24), in light and in dark: text at 4.5:1 (1.4.3), and at 3:1 against adjacent colors what identifies a control and its state, such as an input's border or the focus indicator (1.4.11). The palette (**Theme**) sits closest to those thresholds in these pairs: in light, `ring/50` on `muted`, the theme toggle's fill, at 3.06:1 (#225), `input` on `warning/6` over `card`, the border of the lift's reason in a running timeout's notice, at 3.07:1 (#234), `sidebar-ring/50` on `sidebar` at 3.10:1, `ring/50` on `warning/6` over `card` at 3.13:1 (#234), `input` on `background` at 3.14:1, `ring/50` on `background` at 3.15:1, and on `card` and `popover` at 3.19:1 (#238), `input` on `card` at 3.32:1 (#238), `primary` on `sidebar-accent`, the current section's icon, which needs 3:1, at 4.43:1 (#224), `primary-foreground` on the default Button's hover fill, `primary/90` on `card`, « Ajouter » in the blocked words' form, at 4.56:1 (#228), and on `background` at 4.60:1, `destructive-foreground` on the red buttons' hover fill, `destructive/90`, on `card` and on `background` at 4.56:1 and 4.60:1 (#234), `warning` on `background` and on `card` at 4.66:1 and 4.93:1 (#232), `primary` on `muted`, the border of the theme chosen in the toggle, at 4.80:1 (#225), `muted-foreground` on `sidebar-accent`, the blocked words' count on the current section's fill, at 5.31:1 (#226), `destructive` and `success` on `background` at 5.32:1 and 5.34:1, `destructive-foreground` on `destructive` at 5.40:1 (#234), `muted-foreground` on `accent`, an account found on hover, at 5.49:1 (#232), `primary-foreground` on `primary`, as `sidebar-primary-foreground` on `sidebar-primary`, at 5.56:1, `destructive` and `success` on `card` at 5.63:1 and 5.64:1 (#238), `primary` on `card` at 5.63:1 (#225), and `muted-foreground` on `muted` at 5.74:1, on `sidebar` at 6.01:1 (#224), on `background` at 6.37:1, and on `card` and `popover` at 6.74:1 (#238); in dark, `input` on the field's `input/30` fill over `card` at 3.03:1, the closest of all (#238), `ring/50` on `muted` at 3.19:1 (#225), and on `warning/6` over `card` at 3.19:1 too (#234), `ring/50` on `card` and `popover` at 3.30:1 (#238), `ring/50` on `background`, `sidebar-ring/50` on `sidebar` and `input` on the field's `input/30` fill over `background` at 3.32:1, `input` on `warning/6` over `card` at 4.07:1 (#234), `input` on `card` at 4.52:1 (#238) and on `background` at 4.86:1, `muted-foreground`, the placeholder, on the field's `input/30` fill over `card` at 5.20:1, in the blocked words' form (#228), and over `background` at 5.70:1, in the search (#232), `destructive` on `card` at 6.32:1 (#238), `destructive-foreground` on the red buttons' hover fill, `destructive/90`, on `background` and on `card` at 5.66:1 and 5.72:1 (#234), `muted-foreground` on `accent` at 6.50:1 (#232), and on `sidebar-accent` at 6.55:1 (#226), `destructive-foreground` on `destructive` at 6.77:1 (#234), `destructive` on `background` at 6.80:1, `foreground` on the outline button's hover fill in a running timeout's notice, `input/50` over `warning/6` over `card`, at 6.95:1 (#234), `muted-foreground` on `muted` at 6.98:1, on `card` and `popover` at 7.76:1 (#238), on `sidebar` at 8.03:1 (#224) and on `background` at 8.34:1, `primary` on `sidebar-accent` at 8.00:1 (#224), `primary-foreground` on the default Button's hover fill at 7.57:1 over `background` and 7.65:1 over `card` (#228), `foreground` on `input/50` at 8.05:1, `primary` on `muted` at 8.52:1 (#225), and `success` on `card` at 8.65:1 (#238). Every other pair the theme test proves holds 9.20:1 or more (a scratch port of the measure of `src/ui/globals.test.ts`, run on `src/ui/globals.css`, 2026-10-29). The ring must be darker than `primary` in light, since the Button, the Input and the base layer of `globals.css` draw it at 50% (`ring-ring/50`, `outline-ring/50`), so the ring's copied classes stay as the CLI wrote them (**Copied code**). **Testing** proves it (**The theme**, **End to end**).
- **Accessibility lint.** `eslint.config.mjs` adds the rules of `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y`'s `recommended` config after `eslint-config-next`'s configs (**Gotchas**), so they take its severities: error for all but the three it turns off, the six `eslint-config-next` sets as warnings included (`node_modules/eslint-config-next/dist/index.js:176-191`). #55 kept them so, and `pnpm lint` fails on a warning anyway (**Warnings**).

## Responsive

How every page holds from a 360 px phone to a 1,440 px desktop, as Gilles asked on 2026-11-03, from mockups made with Claude Design, with the three options he chose that day, recorded in [`docs/responsive.md`](docs/responsive.md) (#354). That file holds the patterns, the per-page specification and the checklist that review runs; this section holds the rules a change must not break. Like **Architecture**, it was written before the code: #355 delivers the `touch:` variant, the lint rule, the viewport and the end-to-end test, and each other ticket of #354 makes true the rules that name it, #356 to #362, so that the passages of **Interface** that those rules contradict stay as they are until the ticket that changes them, as #356 changed the sidebar from `md` up and the bar that scrolled away with the page. Any ticket that adds or changes UI says, under `Terminé quand`, what each page it touches shows at 360, 768 and 1,024 px (`docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`, **Interface tickets**).

- **Breakpoints.** Tailwind's and no other: base under 640 px, `sm` from 640, `md` from 768, `lg` from 1,024 and `xl` from 1,280. Classes are written mobile first: the unprefixed class is the phone's, a prefix adds what a wider screen changes. `max-*` variants are kept for the few phone-only overrides that would otherwise need three prefixes, and for the navigation's rail, whose `max-lg:` overrides spare the sidebar a second set of `lg:` classes (`docs/responsive.md`, **Navigation**). No arbitrary `min-[…]` breakpoint on the viewport; a component that must adapt to the room it is given, rather than to the screen, uses a container query (`@container` and `@min-[…]:`), as `blocked-words-view.tsx` and `record-columns.tsx` already do.
- **Navigation (#356).** Below `md`, the bar at the top of the page, which sticks while the page scrolls (#356), and its Sheet, which holds every section under its group titles, then the theme, the member, « Se déconnecter » and « Confidentialité » (option 1a): tabs fixed to the bottom were set aside, since the number of sections and sub-sections to come is unknown (Gilles, 2026-11-03). A new section joins the sidebar, the rail and the Sheet, never a bar of its own. From `md` to `lg`, a rail of 64 px, the same `<aside>` and `<nav>` « Sections du panel » with icons alone, each section keeping its name in `sr-only` and in `title`, the badge of `BlockedWordCountBadge` pinned to its icon, and the theme toggle in its `rail` form, stacked. From `lg`, the sidebar of 252 px of #224, unchanged. So the sidebar starts at `lg` where #224 started it at `md`, and the flow of `e2e/staff-header.test.ts` that fixed 768 and 767 px for the sidebar and the bar fixes 1,024, 1,023 and 767 px, with 768 px kept for the rail by `e2e/responsive.test.ts`, whose every `setViewportSize` goes through `resize` (#356, #348).
- **Touch targets (#357 for the shared controls, #358 to #362 for the pages).** Every control a finger can reach is at least 44 by 44 px: buttons, links that stand alone, fields, selects, tabs, pills, the rows of a list, each radio and checkbox with its label, the Sheet's and the toast's close buttons. `src/ui/globals.css` defines `@custom-variant touch`, which holds under 1,024 px and under `(pointer: coarse)` (`src/ui/touch-variant.test.ts`, #355), and the copied components carry the touch size beside their own, such as `h-8 touch:h-11` and `size-8 touch:size-11`, so that the desktop with a mouse keeps today's density and a tablet in landscape gets the touch sizes; a desktop window narrower than 1,024 px gets them too, with a mouse, which costs nothing (Gilles, 2026-11-03). These 44 px are WCAG 2.5.5's, Target Size (Enhanced), a level AAA criterion (https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/, Recommendation of 2024-12-12, and https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/target-size-enhanced.html, read 2026-11-03), beyond the AA of **Accessibility**, which the panel takes for the finger alone (Gilles, 2026-11-03). The touch sizes are a kind of line that **Copied code** allows in a copied component, beside the colors and the motion, so that no caller has to remember them (Gilles, 2026-11-03). A link inside a sentence is exempt, as WCAG 2.5.5 exempts a target "in a sentence or block of text" and 2.5.8, Target Size (Minimum), a target "within a sentence" (https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/target-size-minimum.html, read 2026-11-03). The 24 px of **Accessibility**, 2.5.8's, stays the floor everywhere else. #357 delivered it for `src/ui/`: every `Button` size, `touch:h-11` on the four text sizes and `touch:size-11` on the four icon sizes, which the close buttons of the Sheet, the Dialog and the Toast follow, then `Input`, `NativeSelect`, a Tab's trigger (`touch:min-h-11`), `ThemeToggle`, `AccountLink` and `TextLink`. `ThemeToggle` takes `touch:h-11` on every choice and `touch:w-11` on an icon-only one, where #357 writes `touch:size-11`, which gives the same box (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #357, for Gilles to review). The Toast's close takes `touch:-my-2 touch:-mr-2`, so that a toast keeps its height and its glyph its place. The Sheet's and the Dialog's close buttons keep their glyph where it was, through `touch:top-1 touch:right-1` and `touch:top-0 touch:right-0` (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #357, for Gilles to review). `TextLink` stands alone by default (`touch:inline-flex touch:min-h-11 touch:items-center`) and takes `inSentence` for a link inside a sentence, which stays content-sized, as the account of an audit log line, its one such caller, since three of its four callers stand alone, and a caller that forgets the prop is left with a larger target, where the opposite default leaves it a small one (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #357, for Gilles to review). `AccountLink` gives `touch:flex touch:min-h-11 touch:items-center` to its link and to the `div` it draws without an address, so that a row's height does not follow the staff member's rights, and `touch:flex` so that the content of a caller that is a `block`, the sidebar's and the bar's identity, sits in the middle of the 44 px, where `items-center` alone does nothing for a `block` (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #357, for Gilles to review). A height that a caller sets still beats `touch:h-11` when its selector is more specific, as the `[&>select]:h-9.5` of `src/records/account-search-form-layout.ts` did until #360, so that `BlockedWordActionSelect`, which sets the same, adds `[&>select]:touch:h-11` for the select of the dialog that changes an action to reach 44 px, and the six choices of the list of accounts add it since #360 (`/records` at 768 px failed the 44 px check on a throwaway build without it, 2026-11-03). #356 delivered it for the navigation: the sidebar's section links take `touch:h-11` and the Sheet's `h-11`, the rail's are 44 px squares, the bar's logo, avatar and menu button are 44 px, as are the member's link, « Confidentialité » and the theme toggle's choices in every form, so that the 44 px test of every page, which measures the navigation with the page, has none of its controls to report (a scratch copy of `e2e/responsive.test.ts` with every `fixme` lifted, 2026-11-03: `/blocked-words` passed at 360, 390, 768 and 1,024 px, and the controls it reported on the other pages were their own).
- **Type and fields (#357).** A field or a native `<select>` under `md` takes `text-base`, 16 px, so that iOS does not zoom on focus (inferred from iOS Safari's usual behavior, not tried on a device); `Input` and `Textarea` already did, and `NativeSelect` takes `text-base md:text-sm` since #357. A page's `h1`, `PageHeading`, takes 32 px under `sm` and 38 px from `sm` since #357; the login pages' 46 px title takes 38 px under `sm` since #362. No text is smaller than 11 px at any width, so the overlines, 10 and 10.5 px before, take 11 px since #357, and the badge of the rail takes it with #356 (Gilles, 2026-11-03). `Overline` is 11 px and no caller sets a smaller size. The brand label « Panel du staff » under « Shor », which **Navigation** calls an overline, was 9.5 px and takes 11 px with them, which makes the panel's link 1.5 px taller and its text 118 px wide where it was 102 (a Playwright probe of every box of six pages at 1,280 px, before and after, 2026-11-03; decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #357, for Gilles to review, since #357 names the overlines of `text-[0.625rem]` and not that class). `src/ui/globals.css` sets `text-size-adjust: 100%` on `html` beside the `-webkit-` one, which Tailwind's preflight already sets (`node_modules/tailwindcss/preflight.css:31`): the property controls the text inflation that some smartphones and tablets apply, and MDN marks the unprefixed form as of limited availability (https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/text-size-adjust, read 2026-11-03), so what it adds to the prefixed one is inferred to be the browsers that read only the standard name (#355).
- **Layout (#355).** No fixed width above 20 rem without a breakpoint or a container variant, no grid of more than two columns without one, no length in `vh`, whose `svh`, `dvh` and `lvh` stay allowed: `eslint-rules/responsive-classes.mjs`, which `eslint.config.mjs` registers as the rule `admin-panel/responsive-classes` on `src/**/*.{ts,tsx}`, refuses all three in `className` and in the strings given to `cn`, `cva` and `clsx`. It reads a width in `w-*`, `min-w-*` and `size-*`, on Tailwind's scale or in `px` or `rem`, and in `w-*` and `min-w-*` by Tailwind's container names, `w-xl` being 36 rem; a `vh` in any arbitrary value, `top-[50vh]` and `py-[10vh]` included; and an `!` on either side of the utility. A breakpoint prefix, `sm:` to `2xl:`, or a container size or minimum, `@sm:` or `@min-[…]:`, counts as a guard, while `max-sm:`, `@max-md:`, `dark:` and `hover:` do not, 20 rem itself passes, and `repeat(auto-fill,minmax(…,1fr))` is no column count (`test/responsive-classes.test.mjs`). It does not see the keys of an object given to `cn` or `clsx`, as in `cn({ "w-96": open })`; a class held in a constant outside a `cn()`, which the ticket that changes each file wraps in one, where **Classes** of **Code style** sorts them too, as #356 did for `MEMBER_LINK_CLASSES` (`src/staff/staff-header.tsx:48`), #358 for the two of `src/blocked-words/word-tiles.ts` and #360 for `NUMBER_CLASSES` (`src/records/account-pagination.tsx:11`); a string given to a prop other than `className`; `basis-*`; a width in `calc()`, `em` or `%`; and it takes the arbitrary `min-[…]:` for a guard, though **Breakpoints** refuses it, which review checks. A flex or grid child that holds text takes `min-w-0`, a grid track that holds text takes `minmax(0,1fr)`, and a word that may be long, such as a name, an ID, a reason or a blocked word, takes `wrap-anywhere` or `truncate` with its whole value in `title`. Nothing may scroll the page sideways at 360 px; a wide table, should one come, scrolls inside its own box.
- **Forms and filters (#359, #360).** Under `sm`, a form stacks its fields and its main action takes the full width. Under `md`, the filters that are not the search, the six choices of `/records` and the author and account of `/audit-log`, fold behind one outline button that opens a Sheet, from the bottom under `sm` and from the right from `sm` for `/records` (#360), and from the bottom at every width under `md` for the audit log, as #359 asks, which wins over this sentence for that page (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #359, for Gilles to review), and the filters that are set show under the search as chips, each a link to the same address without that filter, so that removing one needs no client logic. The Sheet holds a GET form of its own, with its choices and, in a hidden field, the search text of the URL, since its portal renders outside the page's form; the page's form keeps its choices, hidden under `md`, so that a search sent from it keeps the filters set. A field of the Sheet joined to the page's form through its `form` attribute would be sent beside the hidden one of the same name, and a repeated parameter counts as absent (**Validation**). A search text typed and not sent is lost when the Sheet's form is sent (Gilles, 2026-11-03). The copied Sheet keeps Base UI's open state, as the bar's menu does, so it needs no client leaf of the panel's. The audit log's type pills stay visible and wrap. #359 delivered the audit log's (**Navigation** of **Interface** says how), and #393 adds its text and its period to the Sheet, which then holds five fields. #360 delivered `/records`'s, with the same `FilterSheet` and `FilterChips`, which moved to `src/ui/` when a second domain needed them, and which split into a trigger and a content so that the trigger sits in the page's form and the content outside it (**Gotchas**; the move and the split are decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #360, for Gilles to review): a flow fails on a build that nests them, its « Appliquer » landing on the page form's address (`e2e/account-filters.test.ts`, throwaway build, 2026-11-03).
- **Dialogs and sheets (#357).** Under `sm`, `DialogContent` docks (option 2b) to the bottom of the screen across its whole width, with its top corners rounded and its bottom padding raised by `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`, and its two buttons share one row in two equal columns; from `sm`, the dialog stays centered, at the copied `DialogContent`'s `sm:max-w-sm`, or at the `sm:max-w-110` that the panel's two dialogs, the sanctions' confirmation and the blocked words' change of action, pass it. A Sheet takes at most 384 px from `sm`, and the whole width under it when it opens from the bottom. #357 delivered it: the Dialog carries `max-sm:inset-x-0 max-sm:top-auto max-sm:bottom-0 max-sm:max-w-none max-sm:translate-0 max-sm:rounded-b-none max-sm:pb-[calc(1.5rem+env(safe-area-inset-bottom))]`, with no zoom when docked (`max-sm:data-open:zoom-in-100` and its exit), and its slide up from the bottom edge, `max-sm:motion-safe:data-open:slide-in-from-bottom` and its exit, runs only with motion, so that under reduced motion it fades alone, as it does from `sm` (**Motion**). The two confirmation footers, `SanctionConfirmation`'s and `BlockedWordActionChange`'s, are `grid grid-cols-2 gap-2` under `sm` and `sm:flex sm:justify-end` from it.
