A Sheet that opens from the bottom takes `max-h-[88svh]`, and what scrolls in it is a body, `SheetBody`, which `shadcn add` does not write: `min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto px-4` inside the popup, which is already a flex column, between the header and the footer, so that the close button and the footer stay in view on a tall sheet, and a caller wraps its fields in it (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #357, for Gilles to review, since #357 asks for « a scrolling body » and the copied Sheet has none). Every `SheetFooter` pads its bottom by `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`, whatever the side, since the footer of the bar's menu, opened from the right, sits at the screen's bottom edge as much (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #357, for Gilles to review, since #357 pads the footer of the bottom Sheet alone). The first Sheet to open from the bottom is the audit log's (#359), which a flow at 360 px and one at 360 and 390 px measure docked to the bottom edge, across the whole width, once its slide has ended, and every control of it at 44 px (`e2e/audit-log.test.ts`, `e2e/responsive.test.ts`); its scroll, with a body taller than 88svh, is proven by no test, since the audit log's body holds two fields (inferred from the classes, not tried).
- **The blocked words (#358).** `/blocked-words`, which `docs/responsive.md`, **Pages**, specifies, is the first page brought to the finger. What a later change to it must not break: the form's choice and its button stack under `sm`, each at the form's full width, and share a row from `sm`; each skeleton of the page wears the classes of what it stands for, from the layout constant that it shares with its component (`add-blocked-word-form-layout.ts`, `blocked-word-capacity-layout.ts`, `blocked-word-list-layout.ts`), so that the content replaces it at the same size, the gauge's height excepted, which follows the heading's font; a tile keeps its 48 px around its two 44 px buttons; the rows of « Autres règles AutoMod » and their `<summary>` reach 44 px under `touch:` and keep the spacing a mouse gives them; and what the staff type, such as a filter's text, wraps inside its column (`wrap-anywhere`). `e2e/blocked-words-touch.test.ts` proves it at 360, 639, 640, 768 and 1,024 px, the last with a touch screen, and at 1,280 px with a mouse. The page's entry in `e2e/responsive.test.ts` is out of `fixme` since #358, which waited for #356: before the navigation was delivered, every control of the page's `<main>` passed at 360, 390, 768 and 1,024 px and the only small targets left were the bar's logo link and identity link at 360 and 390 px and the sidebar's links at 768 and 1,024 px (`pnpm test:e2e e2e/responsive.test.ts -g "blocked-words gives"` with the entry's `fixme` off, 2026-11-03).
- **Lists (#360, #361).** The rows of the audit log keep the two grids of #319, and its pagination takes the whole width under `sm`, its two links side by side in two equal columns when both show, each wrapping its text within its 44 px, where « Revenir aux plus récents » takes two lines in 156 px (#359; a Playwright measure at 360 px, `e2e/audit-log.test.ts`, 2026-11-03). The rows of `/records` put the badges under the name under `sm`, as today, and take 56 px at least under `touch`. A record's terms and values stack under `sm`, the term above its value, and its avatar takes 56 px there. A record's breadcrumb link, durations and choices of its sanctions' forms take 44 px under `touch`, the messages a ban deletes in a grid of two columns under `sm` (#361). `AccountPagination` shows, under `sm`, only the first page, the current one and its neighbours and the last, between two arrow buttons whose text goes `sr-only`, so that it fits one row of 44 px targets (#360 delivered it, and the rows' 56 px: **Interface** says how « … » takes no width at 360 px).
- **The support tickets' page (#385).** An entry stacks its request kind, its two events and its reason, the events side by side from `sm`; a name is a block of 44 px under `touch` and the width of its column, where a mouse gets a link the width of its name, and `docs/responsive.md` says why. The filters (#386) follow the audit log's pattern: from `md`, the pills of the kinds, then the form with its seven controls and « Filtrer » in line; under `md`, the pills, then « Filtres », a button as wide as the column that opens a sheet from the bottom with the same controls, « Réinitialiser » and « Filtrer », and a chip for each filter set, which removes it. The pills and the chips take 44 px under `touch`, the kind rides in a hidden field of both forms, « Réinitialiser » clears it too, and no link prefetches.
- **Fixed to an edge (#356, #357).** What sticks to the top or the bottom of the screen, the bar, a toast and a dialog docked to the bottom, adds `env(safe-area-inset-*)` to its padding, and the root layout's `viewport` sets `viewportFit: "cover"` for it, as `src/app/layout.tsx` does since #355. #357 pads the docked dialog, the footer of a Sheet and the toasts' viewport (`max-sm:bottom-[calc(1rem+env(safe-area-inset-bottom))]`), none of which a test proves, since Playwright's insets are 0 (inferred), and #356 pads the bar at the top, left and right, the rail and the sidebar at the left, by their width, and at the bottom, by their foot, and the page's column at its sides (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #356, for Gilles to review, for the rail and the sidebar, whose paddings #356 leaves open), which a flow proves by setting the insets through the Chrome DevTools Protocol's `Emulation.setSafeAreaInsetsOverride`, in Playwright 1.64.1's Chromium 156, where `env()` then reads 47, 44, 40 and 34 px and holds across a change of the viewport (`e2e/responsive.test.ts`, 6 runs of 6, 2026-11-03; `docs/responsive.md`, **Known limits**).
- **Checks (#355).** `pnpm lint` runs the rule above. `e2e/responsive.test.ts` crosses nine pages at 360, 390, 768 and 1,024 px: `/blocked-words`, `/audit-log`, `/records` and one record, `/records/<id>` of a member, for a staff member who moderates and bans, and `/support-tickets`, bare, on its second page and with filters set so that their chips show, over 28 archives (#385, #386), then `/privacy`, `/login/logged-out`, `/login/refused` and `/login/failed`, which need no session (#362), over a Discord server with a role of each kind, a member with a role, a banned account and two blocked words. It does not cross the error pages, which a staff session reaches only through an unknown URL or a failed read, and which a flow of their own measures (below); a page that joins them joins `STAFF_PAGES` or `PUBLIC_PAGES` in the same change. That flow, `the login, error and privacy pages at <width> px` (#362), logs in once per width and reads, in one test, `/login/refused`, an unknown URL, the audit log with `getAuditLog` failing once, whose « Réessayer » it clicks so that React's #419 comes, as in the flow of the error pages, then `/privacy`: under 640 px the title of the refused page takes 38 px, and 46 px from it; its « Se reconnecter » and « Se déconnecter » take the room of the column, 328 px at 360 px starting 16 px from the screen's edge, one under the other 8 px apart, where from 640 px they share a row at under 250 px each; the not-found page's « Revenir à l’accueil » and the error page's « Réessayer » follow the same width rule and measure no target under 44 px, the theme toggle's three buttons on the refused page measure 44 by 44 px, and a word of 300 characters written into the error's reference and into the policy's `<code>` scrolls nothing sideways. On the build before #362 it failed at 360 px on the 46 px title and, at 768 and 1,024 px, on a reference of 300 characters (`span.font-mono` the wider element, a `scroll` of 1805 and 1677 px), and the 44 px check of the four public pages failed on the footer's links, 77×16 and 67×16 px; a throwaway build without the `<code>`'s `wrap-anywhere` failed it at 360 px on a `scroll` of 2133 px, and one without the error notice's column on a « Revenir à l’accueil » 154 px wide where 328 were expected (`pnpm test:e2e e2e/responsive.test.ts`, 2026-11-03). The reference of the error page, a digest of Next.js, is short, so the test writes the long word itself rather than provoke one. The 1,024 px screen alone has a touch screen, since it is the one width where `touch:` holds through the pointer alone (the test asserts it). For each page and width, one test fails on a page that scrolls sideways and on axe, and one on a control smaller than 44 px. The 44 px check of a page is a `test.fixme` while the ticket of that page has not delivered it: its entry in `STAFF_PAGES` or `PUBLIC_PAGES` carries `fixme: true`, which that ticket sets to `false`, as #362 did for the public pages and #361 for a record, so that the sideways scroll and axe checks run for every page from today (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #355, for Gilles to review, since #355 asks for each page in `test.fixme` and its other checks pass today). The flow of the navigation's three forms runs since #356, as the flow of a dialog docked to the bottom does since #357: at 767 px, the bar stays at the top after a scroll of 400 px, its controls and its menu's are 44 px and the menu's links, and the bar's avatar, carry their accessible names, then at 768 and 1,023 px the `<aside>` is 64 px wide, names the same links alike, shows the blocked words' badge in the top right of its link, stacks the three icons of the theme toggle in a column, keeps its controls at 44 px and its group titles at 1 px, and can be scrolled to « Se déconnecter » on a 300 px screen, and at 1,024 px it is 252 px wide, names them alike and shows its group titles; a second flow, at 767, 768 and 1,024 px, overrides the screen's safe-area insets and reads that the bar, the rail, the sidebar and the page keep clear of them (#356). A second kind of flow, `the shared controls at <width> px`, logs in once at 360, 390, 768 and 1,024 px and at 1,280 px with a mouse, crosses `/blocked-words`, `/audit-log`, `/records`, a record and the bar's menu, and measures the shared components (#357): the heading's text at 32 px under `sm` and 38 px from it, the overlines' at 11 px, « Capacité », a sidebar group's title and a record's « Heures de Paris » among them, the fields and buttons, the links that stand alone, the toast's close button and its height, a tab, a dialog's button, the Sheet's close and the theme toggle at 44 px on a touch screen and at their desktop size with a mouse, and a field's and a select's text at 16 px under `md` and 14 px from it. It also reads that a link inside a sentence, the account of an audit log line, stays inline and under 44 px, and that the dialog of a kick is docked to the bottom of the screen under 640 px and centered from it, at 768 and 1,024 px as at 1,280 px. A control is an `a[href]`, a `button`, a field, a `select`, a `textarea`, a `summary` or a `[role=tab]`, and the `<label>` of a radio or a checkbox, whose box is under 44 px either way, but for one that is `sr-only`, `aria-hidden`, `inert` or invisible, and a link inside a sentence. The label is the target of a radio or a checkbox, and the native `<input>` is not, since a native checkbox is 16 px wide, its label is what a finger hits, and a timeout's radio button covers its label (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #355, for Gilles to review). A link is inside a sentence when it is inline, in a `p`, `li`, `dd` or `span` that holds more text than the link: of 12 states of those six pages at 360 px, with and without a filter, it exempts one link, the audit log's account in « Compte : <name> · Motif : <reason> », and none that stands alone (a throwaway probe, 2026-11-03), so the heuristic stays; a link that stands alone in a `<p>` with a count would escape it (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #355, for Gilles to review). A third kind of flow, `the record at <width> px`, one at each of those five widths, opens a record of a member for a staff member who moderates and bans (#361): its avatar is 56 px under 640 px and 68 px from it, its name 32 and 38 px, each term of its details sits above its value under 640 px, in 12.5 px, and beside it from there, in 14 px, the breadcrumb's link, the seven durations, the label « Prévenir en message privé » and each of the seven messages a ban deletes are 44 px high on a touch screen and keep their desktop size, 30 px for a duration and 19 px for the others, with a mouse, the messages stand in two columns under 640 px and, from 640 px on a touch screen, in as many columns of 170 px at least as their card holds, three at 1,024 px, and every control of `main` is 44 px at least on each of the three tabs, measured within `main`, since `smallTargets` takes the root to scan, `body` by default, so that the navigation's own targets, which #356 delivered, are left to the page-wide 44 px test, which the record has passed since #361 (decided by the agent on 2026-11-03, on #361, for Gilles to review, since #361 names no flow of its own and the navigation's targets kept the page-wide test red until #356). The two columns and the card « Sanctions » that sticks at 1,280 px stay proved by `e2e/records.test.ts`, whose flow changed at 360 px alone (#361). A page is measured once `settle`, of `e2e/settle.ts`, says it is complete (**Gotchas**), and every change of the viewport in `e2e/responsive.test.ts` and in the navigation's flow of `e2e/staff-header.test.ts` goes through `resize`, of the same file, which settles first, since Chromium aborts a view transition that a change of size cuts, with a `pageerror` that the console check refuses (#348). `/audit-log` is crossed twice, bare and with a type and an author set so that its chips show, and its filter sheet, which exists under 768 px alone, has a test of its own at 360 and 390 px that opens it and measures its box, its controls at 44 px, its overflow and axe (#359), and `/records` is crossed twice too, bare and with a state and a sort set so that its chips show, its sheet taking the same test (#360), as does `/support-tickets` with its sheet « Filtres » (#386). The 44 px check of both entries left its `test.fixme` once #356 had merged: before it, with the `fixme` off, the only controls under 44 px on those pages were the navigation's, the bar's logo link at 28×30 and its avatar link at 32×44 at 360 and 390 px, and at 768 and 1,024 px the sidebar's « Mots interdits », « Journal d’audit » and « Modération » at 227×36 and « Confidentialité » at 77×16, and none of the page's own (`pnpm test:e2e e2e/responsive.test.ts -g "audit-log"` on a694641, 2026-11-03, #359), and the 18 tests of the audit log pass on the merge with #356 (`-g "audit"`, 2026-11-03). A fourth kind of flow, `the tickets in progress at <width> px`, opens `/support-tickets` at those five widths over two tickets, one with a last message of 300 characters, and measures that the cards stack one to a row across the page's column under 1,024 px and sit two to a row from it, above the archives, that the card's three facts share a row when its content holds 448 px, that the link to the channel is 44 px high on a touch screen, and that nothing scrolls sideways (`e2e/responsive.test.ts`, #388). Review reads the checklist of `docs/responsive.md` on any change to the interface.

## Security

How the panel protects the staff's session, its secrets and its writes, and how it reads its configuration, as Gilles decided while grilling #51 on 2026-10-24. Like **Architecture**, it was written before the code: #59 sets up the configuration, Discord's variables and `server-only`, #10 the staff's variables, the session and the login, #63 `E2E_TEST_PROXY`, #76 the headers, and #9 what lives in Vercel's dashboard. The options set aside, and why, are in #51's decisions.

- **The session** is a cookie that iron-session 9 seals, encrypted and signed, holding only the member's Discord ID (ADR 0005); no session lives on the server, since the panel stores nothing (ADR 0004). Auth.js and Better Auth are set aside: each brings its own OAuth flow, which would replace `DiscordOAuth` and the two Route Handlers. It is named `__Host-session`, with `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Lax` and `Path=/`, in development too, since Chromium takes a `Secure` cookie on `http://localhost` (end to end, 2026-10-26). `Lax`, not `Strict`: the return from Discord to `/login/callback`, and a link to the panel clicked in Discord, are navigations that start on another site, which a `Strict` cookie skips. `vercel.app` is on the Public Suffix List (https://publicsuffix.org/list/public_suffix_list.dat, line 16331, read 2026-10-24), so another Vercel project is another site, which can neither set the panel's cookies nor send them on a POST; `__Host-` costs nothing, and would matter under a domain of the panel's own. iron-session 9.0.1 needs Node `>=22.13.0` and is ESM only (`npm view iron-session`, 2026-10-24).
- **Its lifetime** is 7 days from the login, written into the seal's `ttl` and the cookie's `Max-Age`, and never extended: a Server Component cannot set a cookie (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/04-functions/cookies.md:81`), so extending it would mean writing the cookie from `proxy.ts` or from every Server Action. A session ends when it expires, through « Se déconnecter », or, for every member at once, when `SESSION_SECRET` changes. None can be revoked on its own (iron-session 9.0.1's README, « How to invalidate sessions? », `npm view iron-session readme`, 2026-10-24): the staff check on every request is what cuts a member's access (ADR 0005), so a stolen cookie is worth at most 7 days, and only while its member holds the staff role.
- **OAuth2.** `/login` draws 32 random bytes as the `state`, in base64url, keeps them in a `__Host-oauth-state` cookie with `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Lax` and a `Max-Age` of 10 minutes, and sends `prompt=consent`: Discord documents no default for `prompt`, and `none` would send a member who clicks « Se reconnecter » back as the same account (https://docs.discord.com/developers/topics/oauth2, modified 2026-10-16). The callback compares the `state` in constant time, deletes its cookie whatever the outcome, and goes to `/login/failed` when it is missing or differs, so of two logins started in two tabs, only the later one can succeed, and only if it returns first. A login always lands on `/`, with no return path in the URL, so no redirect is open. PKCE with S256, which Discord's metadata lists (`code_challenge_methods_supported`, https://discord.com/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server, read 2026-10-24) but its OAuth2 page does not document, stays: on 2026-10-26, against « Shor Panel Dev », Discord refused a code exchanged with a wrong `code_verifier` (`400 invalid_grant`, « Code challenge failed. ») or with none (`400 invalid_request`, « Missing "code_verifier" »), and accepted it with the right one (#10). So `/login` also draws 32 random bytes as the `code_verifier`, in base64url, sends their S256 challenge, and keeps them in the same cookie, after the `state` and a dot, for the callback's exchange. `state` stays all the same, as #51 decided, since RFC 9700 relies on PKCE against CSRF only once the server is known to support it (§4.7.1). The member's token is not revoked after `/users/@me`: it never leaves the callback's memory, and revoking it would revoke every token of that authorization (the OAuth2 page), for one more call and one more failure. `iss`, which Discord has returned since 2026-10-14 (https://docs.discord.com/developers/change-log), goes unchecked: Discord advises the check for a client of several providers.
- **CSRF.** Nothing beyond what Next.js and the `Lax` cookie give: no token, and no `serverActions.allowedOrigins`. A Server Action takes only POST, and its `Origin` must match `Host` (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/data-security.md:548-550`). A POST without `Origin` passes with a warning (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/05-config/01-next-config-js/serverActions.md:13`), but a browser sends `Origin` on a POST, and a client that does not carries no session cookie (inferred). `/login` and `/login/callback` are GETs that write nothing to Discord, and `state` covers the login itself.
- **Headers.** `next.config.ts` sets, on every route, a Content Security Policy without nonces (ADR 0006), `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: blob: https://cdn.discordapp.com; font-src 'self'; connect-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; upgrade-insecure-requests`, with `'unsafe-eval'` added to `script-src` in development only, when `NODE_ENV` is `development` (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/content-security-policy.md:548`); then `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, all through `headers()` on `/:path*`, and `poweredByHeader: false`, so no response carries `X-Powered-By`. `img-src` allows `https://cdn.discordapp.com` for the members' avatars (**Avatars**, #140). `next.config.test.ts` proves both policies, and an end-to-end test reads the headers on `/login/logged-out` (#76). `upgrade-insecure-requests` holds in every environment: under `next start` on `http://localhost:3100`, Chromium still loaded every script and stylesheet over `http:`, and every end-to-end flow passed (throwaway test, 2026-10-27); only the target of a redirect that a `fetch` follows went to `https:` (**Gotchas**). Under `next dev`, the same flows logged no violation (throwaway Playwright config, 2026-10-27), though two failed on Next.js's own development warning « Route … is rendering with server caches disabled », which `next start` does not print, and the headers test, as it should, on the development policy. The real `pnpm dev`, logged in through « Shor Panel Dev », logged no violation either, on `/`, on `/blocked-words` and around a blocked word added then removed (Gilles, 2026-10-30, #119). HSTS is Vercel's: `max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains; preload` on `*.vercel.app` (https://vercel.com/docs/cdn-security/encryption, updated 2026-09-15). Since scripts may run inline, React's escaping is the defense against XSS, and `react/no-danger` and `react/jsx-no-script-url` are errors, the latter on `<a>` and on `next/link`'s `Link`, which it skips without being named (#76, 2026-10-27). The Vercel Toolbar, on by default on previews, is turned off there (#9), so previews serve production's policy rather than one opened to `vercel.live` (https://vercel.com/docs/vercel-toolbar/managing-toolbar, updated 2026-08-11).
- **Rate limiting.** One rule of the Vercel Firewall, the only one Hobby allows per project (https://vercel.com/docs/vercel-firewall/vercel-waf/rate-limiting, updated 2026-08-28), set in the dashboard by #9: every path, keyed on the IP, 600 requests over a fixed window of 10 minutes, then deny. A Hobby project past an included limit waits 30 days (https://vercel.com/docs/plans/hobby, updated 2026-09-14), and a function invocation counts "regardless of request success or failure" (https://vercel.com/docs/functions/usage-and-pricing, updated 2026-06-16), so a script from one IP could take the panel down; traffic the Firewall denies counts toward no usage (https://vercel.com/docs/vercel-firewall/vercel-waf/usage-and-pricing, updated 2026-06-16). Attack Mode, free on every plan, answers a flood from many IPs (https://vercel.com/docs/vercel-firewall/attack-mode, updated 2026-08-11). The panel's code limits nothing: a write needs a session whose member holds the staff role, and Discord's 429 is handled (**Retries**).
- **The application's permissions.** Since 2026-11-03, the panel's application is the Bot's too, ShorBot connecting to the gateway with its token, from the switch of ADR 0008 on, and the Bot's permissions join its role then. The panel's application never holds Administrator. Each feature adds the Discord permissions and privileged intents it needs, and no more, in the same change as the README's list of them, and the panel's role sits just above the roles it manages. On Shor's Discord server, that role, "Shor Panel", holds exactly Manage Server, View Audit Log, Timeout Members, Kick Members and Ban Members, at position 13 of 38, between "Ko-fi Bot" (14) and "🎥| Streameur" (12): under it sit only the roles of the members outside the staff and the Patreon bot, and above it "Ko-fi Bot", "Divin" and "Shor - Bot", then "Staff" (17) and every role of the staff (`GET /guilds/{guild.id}/roles` and `GET /guilds/{guild.id}/members/{user.id}`, 2026-10-29, #183). Gilles put it there, not right under the staff's highest role as #183 had planned, so that the panel reaches only the members outside the staff and a leak of its token touches neither the staff nor the bots above it (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #183), though the Patreon bot sits under the role all the same. He also ticked the three moderation permissions on it himself (Gilles, 2026-10-29, on #183), where this rule had a staff member who holds Manage Server grant them, since Gilles did not (#8) (dropping that rule decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #249, for Gilles to review). He is inferred to hold Manage Roles and those three on Shor, probably through Administrator (#249). "Shor Panel Dev" gets a wave's permissions on Gilles' test Discord server ahead of its features, through a ticket of its own, for `pnpm dev` and the contract suite, as #178 gave it the moderation's (#143). #192 gave it Manage Roles and Manage Channels for the interactions wave (#191), and #206 took them back, since no feature of the panel needs them any more (ADR 0003). Whoever holds the application's token holds all of them, so each one widens what a leak costs (Gilles, 2026-10-27, while grilling the features that follow the MVP). Both applications also hold the Server Members Intent, `GUILD_MEMBERS`, which Gilles turned on in the Developer Portal on 2026-11-02 for the list of accounts: `GET /applications/@me` read `flags` of 32768, `1 << 15`, for « Shor Panel Dev » and for « Shor Panel », and `GET /guilds/{guild.id}/members?limit=1` answered 200 for each on its Discord server, which counted 4 members for the test one and 69 for Shor's (`approximate_member_count`, #318). It is no permission of the role: it lets the application call List Guild Members, which requires it (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/guild, read 2026-11-02) and answered « Shor Panel Dev » `403 50001` before, and the panel opens no connection to the gateway (ADR 0003), so it receives no event about any member (#322). A leaked token now also lists every member at once, where it read one by its ID before (inferred). The Message Content intent is on for both too since 2026-11-05: `GET /applications/@me` read `flags` of 557056, `1 << 19` and `1 << 15`, for each (#382), and the search of the Discord server's messages needs it, with Read Message History on the channels it searches (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/message, **Search Guild Messages**, read 2026-11-05; #383). The panel uses it through REST alone, which the intents an application passes when it connects to the gateway do not touch (https://docs.discord.com/developers/events/gateway, **HTTP Restrictions**, read 2026-11-05; ADR 0010). A leaked token now also reads the content of the channels the application views (inferred). Reading the ticket archives (#197) asks View Channel and Read Message History on the archives channel (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/message, read 2026-11-04), which is private: on Shor, « 📃・transcriptions » is synced with its category « ・TICKETS PRIVÉS ・ », which hides it from @everyone, and the application's role holds Read Message History through @everyone and a permission of its own on that category, which ShorBot's own posting there needs (ShorBot's README, **Archiving a closed support ticket**, and its issue #25, comment of 2026-11-04), so the application is inferred to hold both on the channel already, which the panel did not try (the manual try of #197 tells). A record paged the channel's history until #387, which needs no privileged intent; it now searches the channel, as the page `/support-tickets` does, which needs the Message Content intent that Gilles turned on (ADR 0009, ADR 0010). Reading the tickets in progress (#388) asks View Channel and Read Message History on each ticket's channel: ShorBot writes View Channel for the application's role in every channel it creates (`TICKET_OWN_ROLE`, `create_support_ticket_channel`, `shorbot/discord_adapter.py:41`, `:478` at 9915eb7), and « Shor Panel » holds Read Message History on « ・TICKETS PRIVÉS ・ » and at role level on Shor (Gilles, 2026-11-05, on #382), so the application is inferred to read every ticket's channel already, which the check of the page on Shor tells (#380, not tried). The conversations it reads there need the Message Content intent (ADR 0010). Reading the game log asks View Channel and Read Message History on its channel, which Gilles gave « Shor Panel » on the category « 🛠️・LOG » on 2026-11-05 (#390, #394; `.claude/rules/game-log.md`). Listing the Discord server's channels (#384) is inferred to ask for no permission and no intent: Discord's page for Get Guild Channels names none (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/guild, read 2026-11-05), and « Shor Panel Dev » read the 13 channels of the test Discord server with a 200 (a read-only probe of `GET /guilds/{guild.id}/channels`, 2026-11-05), though its role holds View Channel, which the probe cannot tell from a requirement. From 2026-11-16, though, Discord lists only the channels the application can view (**The Discord seam**), so a page that reads a channel through `canViewChannel` needs View Channel for the application on that channel itself, and a grant on its category reaches the channel only while it is synced to it (**Channel visibility**; inferred, not tried).
- **Secrets.** Only `NEXT_PUBLIC_` variables reach the browser (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/environment-variables.md:156-164`), and the panel has none. Every module that reads a secret from `process.env` or calls Discord starts with `import "server-only"`, so a client component that reaches it fails the build. Next.js resolves `server-only` itself (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/01-getting-started/05-server-and-client-components.md:602`), but Vitest does not, and the package's default entry throws, only its `react-server` condition leading to an empty module (`npm view server-only exports`, 2026-10-24): `server-only` is a dependency, and `vitest.config.mts` aliases it to that empty module. React's taint APIs stay off, being experimental (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/data-security.md:219-236`).
- **Personal data.** [`docs/personal-data.md`](docs/personal-data.md) says what the panel processes about people, why, where and for how long, and what the GDPR and Discord's Developer Terms ask before the store opens. A change that starts or stops processing a personal datum updates it, and the privacy policy at `/privacy`, which says the same in French, with the date it shows, in the same change (**Privacy policy**, #186) (Gilles, 2026-10-28, while grilling #27).
- **CI secrets.** `ci.yml` holds none. "Shor Panel Dev"'s token is CI's one secret, in the GitHub environment `discord-contract`, which only `contract.yml` uses (**Delivery**, #102). On Gilles' test Discord server, its application held only Manage Server and View Audit Log (#10, comment of 2026-10-23), and since #178 also holds Timeout Members, Kick Members and Ban Members, through a role right under the staff role and, since #192, above the test Discord server's Membre role, beyond what `@everyone` gives every member (`GET /guilds/{guild.id}/roles` and `GET /guilds/{guild.id}/members/{user.id}`, 2026-10-30), and since 2026-11-02 the Server Members Intent, which the contract suite's list of members needs (#318, #322). GitHub Free offers environments to public repositories only (https://docs.github.com/en/actions/managing-workflow-runs-and-deployments/managing-deployments/managing-environments-for-deployment, read 2026-10-25), and this one has been public since 2026-10-27 (ADR 0007, #100).
- **Configuration.** Each folder validates its own variables with zod, in a module without `server-only` that exports a function parsing the environment it is given; `src/discord/env.ts` has one per client, since `contract.yml` holds only `DiscordApi`'s variables (Gilles, 2026-10-25, on #59), and a third, `parseTestProxyEnv`, for `E2E_TEST_PROXY`, the switch that lets the HTTP adapter answer for Discord end to end (Gilles, 2026-10-25, on #63). The folder's factories call it on `process.env` when they first run, in development as in production, and a failed parse is then a panel error (**Four kinds of failure**). When `VERCEL` is set, `next.config.ts` also calls every folder's at build, so a missing or invalid variable fails the build and Vercel keeps the previous production deployment: "Your production domains don't serve a deployment until a production deployment succeeds" (https://vercel.com/docs/deployments/environments, updated 2026-09-17). Next.js loads `.env*` before `next.config.ts` (`node_modules/next/dist/server/config.js:1263`), and Vercel gives a project's variables to its build (https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables, updated 2026-09-17). Elsewhere the build checks nothing, since `pnpm typecheck` loads `next.config.ts` too (**Gotchas**): a fresh clone or a worktree, without `.env.local`, still typechecks and builds, and `ci.yml` needs no placeholder values. `E2E_TEST_PROXY`, which `next.config.ts` parses on every load, may be absent. A failed parse's message names the variable, never its value: zod 4 leaves the input out of its issues (https://zod.dev/error-customization, read 2026-10-24), and the panel turns on no `reportInput` and adds no error map that would put it back.
- **Variables.** `src/discord/` reads `DISCORD_CLIENT_ID`, `DISCORD_CLIENT_SECRET` and `DISCORD_REDIRECT_URI` for `DiscordOAuth`, and `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` and `DISCORD_GUILD_ID` for `DiscordApi`; `src/discord/`'s contract suite also reads `CONTRACT_MEMBER_ID`, through `parseContractEnv`, the member of the test Discord server it times out and sends a direct message (#179), and `CONTRACT_ABSENT_USER_ID`, the account absent from it that it bans by its ID, then lifts the ban of (#181); `src/staff/` reads `STAFF_ROLE_ID`, `MODERATION_ROLE_IDS`, `BAN_ROLE_IDS` and `SESSION_SECRET`; `src/ticket-archives/` reads `TICKET_ARCHIVES_CHANNEL_ID` and `TICKET_CATEGORY_ID`; `src/game-log/` reads `GAME_LOG_CHANNEL_ID` and `GAME_LOG_WEBHOOK_ID`; `src/monitoring/` reads `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL`, and Vercel's `VERCEL_ENV` and `VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_SHA` (**Monitoring**); `next.config.ts` reads `E2E_TEST_PROXY`, and the `NODE_ENV` that Next.js sets, for the Content Security Policy (**Headers**). IDs are snowflakes of 17 to 20 digits (inferred from Discord's 64-bit IDs). `MODERATION_ROLE_IDS` and `BAN_ROLE_IDS` each list one or more of them, split on commas, with spaces allowed around each (ADR 0005, #176). `DISCORD_REDIRECT_URI` is a URL, `https:` except on `localhost`, set per environment, and on a preview only on demand (**Delivery**, **Previews**). `SESSION_SECRET` holds at least 32 characters (the same README, `password`). `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` is a URL under `https://discord.com/api/webhooks/`, required when `VERCEL_ENV` is `production` and left unset elsewhere, where `Monitor` only writes to the console; an empty one counts as unset, so that a copy of `.env.example` sends no alert rather than failing its parse (Gilles, 2026-10-25, on #78, where `E2E_TEST_PROXY` refuses an empty value and stays out of `.env.example`). `TICKET_ARCHIVES_CHANNEL_ID` is the ID of the channel where the Bot posts the ticket archives, a snowflake, « 📃・transcriptions », `1562505523490848881`, on Shor, optional, an empty one counting as unset as `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL`'s does: the test Discord server has had one since 2026-11-05, « transcriptions », `1567881753266229298`, in a ticket category, `1567881446415278191`, which Gilles created and which « Shor Panel Dev » views (#382, closing comment of 2026-11-05), and a build on Vercel never fails for want of it, so that a record then says its archives are not configured. A value that is no snowflake fails the build on Vercel, as the others do. Production needs it set in Vercel's dashboard, which only Gilles does, and a variable reaches a deployment at its build (**Gotchas**) (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #197, for Gilles to review, for the ticket says the configuration holds the channel and not how). `TICKET_CATEGORY_ID` is the ID of the category where the Bot creates the channel of each support ticket, « ・TICKETS PRIVÉS ・ », `1559574907426439229` on Shor and `1567881446415278191` on Gilles' test Discord server, a snowflake, named as ShorBot names it (`TICKET_CATEGORY_ID`, `shorbot/config.py:20` at 9915eb7), as `TICKET_ARCHIVES_CHANNEL_ID` is (`:21`), while the code says « tickets category » and « archives channel », the terms of `GLOSSARY.md` (`ticketsCategoryId`, `archivesChannelId`) (decided by the agent on 2026-11-05, on #388, for Gilles to review), optional as the archives channel's is, an empty one counting as unset: left out, `/support-tickets` says that the tickets category is not configured, and a build on Vercel never fails for want of it, but for a value that is no snowflake (#388, `src/ticket-archives/env.ts`, which the epic's decision keeps beside the archives channel's). Production needs it set in Vercel's dashboard too, which only Gilles does. `GAME_LOG_CHANNEL_ID` and `GAME_LOG_WEBHOOK_ID` are the snowflakes of the game log's channel and of the webhook that posts there (never its URL), optional together, an empty one counting as unset, and one without the other refused by name; their values and what each state does are in `.claude/rules/game-log.md` and the README (#394). `VERCEL_ENV` and `VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_SHA` are system variables, which Vercel sets once the project exposes them (#9), so they are absent outside Vercel and stay out of `.env.example`. The others are non-empty. `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` and `DISCORD_GUILD_ID` take Discord's names, as `src/discord/` does (`docs/agents/domain.md`): Discord files that token under its application's Bot tab and sends it as `Authorization: Bot`, though the docs call it the panel's application token (ADR 0003). `STAFF_ROLE_ID` names one role, the only one that makes a member staff on Shor's Discord server (the POs, relayed by Gilles on 2026-10-29, on #13). `E2E_TEST_PROXY` is absent or `1`, and the build refuses it when `VERCEL` is set, as Vercel does at build and at runtime (https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables/system-environment-variables, updated 2026-07-15) once the project exposes its system variables, which #9 checks. `.env.example` lists them all, empty, but Vercel's two and `E2E_TEST_PROXY`, which only Playwright's `webServer` sets and which an empty value copied from it would break (Gilles, 2026-10-25, on #63), and the README documents each one as the code that reads it arrives.

## Monitoring

What the panel logs, what it never logs, and how its maintainer learns that it broke, as Gilles decided while grilling #50 on 2026-10-24, with everything kept free. Like **Architecture**, it was written before the code: #78 wrote `src/monitoring/` and `instrumentation.ts`, #59 gave `DiscordError` its route's pattern, #10 makes the login callback report what it catches, and #9 sets the webhook's variable in Vercel's dashboard. The options set aside, and why, are in #50's decisions.

- **Logs and the audit log.** Logs say why the panel failed, for whoever maintains it; the audit log says which staff member changed what (`GLOSSARY.md`, ADR 0004). So logs hold failures only: no staff action and no success, neither a write that lands, which Discord's audit log records with the panel's reason, nor a login.
- **What is logged.** Next.js prints every error thrown in a render, a Server Action or a Route Handler itself, through `console.error`, before it calls `onRequestError` (`node_modules/next/dist/server/route-modules/route-module.js:320-327`), with its message, its stack and its enumerable properties, `digest` included (`node_modules/next/dist/server/patch-error-inspect.js:437-442`); `redirect()` and `notFound()` never get there (`node_modules/next/dist/server/app-render/create-error-handler.js:52`, `:68-71`). The panel adds no line to those. An error of `proxy.ts` takes another way to `onRequestError`, with no such `console.error` on it (`node_modules/next/dist/build/templates/middleware.js:108-118`), and that way looks for the instrumentation the edge runtime loads, `_ENTRIES.middleware_instrumentation` (`node_modules/next/dist/server/web/globals.js:27-30`, `:48-57`), while `proxy.ts` runs on Node.js only (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/proxy.md:255`). On 2026-10-25, a throwaway `proxy.ts` that threw, under `next start`, never reached `onRequestError`, and Next.js printed its error once, with its stack (inferred for Vercel, where it was not tried). So `proxy.ts` catches what it throws and hands it to `Monitor.error`, which writes the one line and sends the alert, then answers without throwing it again (Gilles, 2026-10-25, on #78), by letting the request through: `proxy.ts` authorizes nothing, and each page runs its own check, which fails the same way and alerts a second time (Gilles, 2026-10-26, on #10). Beyond that, the panel writes only what the login callback catches, since the callback throws nothing (**Error pages**): a panel error at `error`, Discord unavailable and a missing or differing `state` at `warn`. Refused input and refused access leave nothing, nor does a login the member cancels on Discord (Gilles, 2026-10-26, on #10), nor a write that returns `{ ok: false }` because Discord is unavailable, nor a 429 retried with success. `info` and `debug` go unused.
- **What is never logged**, nor sent in an alert: a secret, such as `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`, `DISCORD_CLIENT_SECRET`, `SESSION_SECRET` or `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL`; a member's OAuth2 token; the callback's `code` and `state`; a cookie, or the `Authorization` and `Cookie` headers; a member's Discord ID, name or IP, since the panel stores no personal data (ADR 0004); and what the staff typed, such as a blocked word. A member who reports a failure names themself in the staff's channel. That is why a `DiscordError` carries its route's pattern, never the path filled in (**`DiscordError`**).
- **Log lines** go through `console.error` and `console.warn`, called with the error object as Next.js does, with no logging library and no JSON. Vercel's own pages disagree on whether the fields of a JSON line are searchable (https://vercel.com/docs/logs/runtime, updated 2026-08-28, against https://vercel.com/kb/guide/add-structured-application-logs-to-vercel-functions, updated 2026-05-28), and Hobby keeps runtime logs one hour (the first page), so the alert carries what matters.
- **Alerts.** Every panel error notifies Gilles, the maintainer, alone, but one that a lookup of an avatar in the audit log turns into initials, or that the read of the blocked words' count in the navigation turns into no badge (**Read failures**, #226), and stays findable for at least 7 days, one thrown in `proxy.ts` included, which `proxy.ts` reports itself (**What is logged**); Discord unavailable notifies no one, since it passes; the counts of the request kinds on the support tickets' page hide only that, so a panel error there alerts (**Read failures**, #386). The staff get no alert: they report what they see in their channel (**Messages**), `error.tsx` with its reference, or `/login/failed`, which shows none. Alerts go through a Discord webhook into a private channel of Gilles' test Discord server (#7): it is free, needs no other account, and the panel writes every word it sends. Discord keeps a channel's messages with no expiry, which covers the 7 days (inferred, not checked against Discord's docs). Executing a webhook is a call to Discord's REST API (ADR 0003), and the one call to Discord outside `DiscordApi` and `DiscordOAuth` (**The Discord seam**). Nothing is grouped: ten failing pages send ten messages, and an alert sent during a Discord outage is lost.
- **What an alert says**, in English like the logs (**Language**): the environment, from `VERCEL_ENV`, `local` outside Vercel, and the short commit, from `VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_SHA`; the context's `routeType` and `routePath`, the route's file path (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/instrumentation.md:109`), when Next.js gives one; the `digest`, when there is one; the error's name and message, which for a `DiscordError` hold its method, its route's pattern, its status, and Discord's `code` and `message` (`src/discord/discord-error.ts`); then the stack's frames. It is one `content`, with no embed, of at most Discord's 2,000 characters (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/webhook, modified 2026-06-25): those lines, then the error and its frames in a code block, whose end is cut, ending on `…`, when it does not fit. Never `request.path`, which holds the query string (`instrumentation.md:103`), and so the callback's `code` and `state`, nor anything else **What is never logged** names. It goes with `allowed_mentions: { parse: [] }`, so that no error text mentions anyone, where a webhook's default parses users (the message page). It holds no time of its own: Discord shows when the message arrived.
- **Sending.** An alert never changes what the staff see. Next.js awaits `onRequestError` before it answers a Route Handler or an error that escapes a render (`node_modules/next/dist/build/templates/app-route.js:338`, `node_modules/next/dist/build/templates/app-page-runtime.js:1602`), but not for an error thrown while streaming (`create-error-handler.js:125`), and `after()` is documented for Server Components, Server Functions, Route Handlers and `proxy.ts` only (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/04-functions/after.md:8`). So the alert runs in the background, gives up after 2 s through `AbortSignal.timeout`, and never throws: a failed send is one `console.error` line. It is never retried, a 429 included: in a burst the same error repeats, so the first alerts carry it (inferred). Discord gives no figure for the rate limit of webhooks (https://docs.discord.com/developers/topics/rate-limits, modified 2026-02-06). What can throw is the factory: `createRequestErrorReporter()` parses the variables on every error, and so does `createMonitor()`'s `error`, once its line is written, so an invalid `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` makes `onRequestError` throw, which Next.js catches and prints as `Error in instrumentation.onRequestError:`, with the variable's name and not its value (`node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/router-utils/instrumentation-globals.external.js:138`; **Configuration**). A failed send's line reads `Alert not sent: ` then Discord's status, or the name of what `fetch` threw, never its message, which can hold the webhook's URL, as a `Failed to parse URL from …` would. `onRequestError` returns before the send ends, and `waitUntil` from `@vercel/functions` hands the send to the `waitUntil` of the request context Vercel's runtime keeps under `Symbol.for("@vercel/request-context")`; without that context, as under `pnpm dev` and `next start`, it does nothing, and the send runs on in a server that stays up (`node_modules/@vercel/functions/wait-until.js`, `get-context.js`, 3.10.1). Under `next start`, an error thrown while streaming and one thrown by a Route Handler each sent their alert, to a well-formed URL that Discord answered with a 404 (2026-10-25), and under `pnpm dev`, with `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` set by hand, a Route Handler's panel error reached the private channel of Gilles' test Discord server (Gilles, 2026-10-25). On a preview, the send of an error thrown while streaming reached its end too: on 2026-10-30, a throwaway page under `/login/`, `◐` in the build's list, whose component under `<Suspense>` threw after `connection()`, put an alert in that channel with `preview`, the commit, `render /login/probe-alert`, the `digest` and frames under `/var/task/` (Gilles, on #78). That its response had already left is read under `next start`, where `curl` got the page's `200` and its shell in 27 ms, the boundary flagged in the stream, and inferred on Vercel, and whether the send would have ended without `waitUntil` was not tried. Each render that fails sends its own alert: that trial's « Réessayer » sent a second one with the same `digest`, as a load and a click on it sent two in a browser under `next start` (2026-10-30).
- **`src/monitoring/`** sits outside the domain, like `src/discord/` and `src/ui/`. It exposes `Monitor`, one method per level, which `createMonitor()` builds, in `monitor.ts`, without reading its variables: `error` writes the line, then reads them and sends the alert, `warn` writes the line only. So a request that reports nothing, through `proxy.ts` say, never fails on `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` (#10). `isPanelError`, in `panel-error.ts`, tells whether a thrown error alerts: a `DiscordError` that says Discord is unavailable does not, anything else does. It knows a `DiscordError` by its `name` and its `unavailable`, not by `instanceof`, since `instrumentation.ts` gets a copy of the class of its own (**Gotchas**). `src/instrumentation.ts` only wires: its `onRequestError` hands the error, the request and the context to the reporter that `createRequestErrorReporter()` builds, in `request-error.ts`, which sends the alert when `isPanelError` says so, and writes no line, since Next.js has written it. The login callback's domain function takes a `Monitor`, as it takes `DiscordApi` and `DiscordOAuth`, and so does what `proxy.ts` calls. `alert-message.ts` lays out the alert, and `alert.ts` sends it, over a `fetch` and a `waitUntil` taken as parameters, like **The real implementations**, which the two factories fill with `globalThis.fetch` and `@vercel/functions`' `waitUntil`, behind `import "server-only"` (**Secrets**). `env.ts` reads `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL`, `VERCEL_ENV` and `VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_SHA` (**Variables**). `InMemoryMonitor`, in `in-memory-monitor.ts`, keeps each report with its level for the tests, and the `no-restricted-imports` rule that keeps the fake Discord in `*.test.*` files (**The Discord seam**) refuses it elsewhere too.
- **Stacks** stay as Next.js builds them, minified, since `experimental.serverMinification` defaults to `true` (`node_modules/next/dist/server/config-shared.js:203`): the panel adds neither `serverSourceMaps` nor `--enable-source-maps`. The message, the route and a `DiscordError`'s fields place most failures in a panel this small. This is revisited when a stack proves unreadable.
- **No uptime probe.** The failures that matter, such as a revoked token or a removed permission, show only when a page reads Discord, so when the staff use the panel, and the alert fires then. A failed deployment notifies its creator, on the web and by email, by default on every plan (https://vercel.com/docs/notifications, updated 2026-09-14), and Vercel keeps the previous production deployment (**Configuration**).
- **The browser reports nothing.** Client leaves hold no logic (**Components**), an action's error is thrown on the server before the browser sees it, and `connect-src` stays `'self'` (**Headers**).

## Code style

How the code is laid out, named and typed, as Gilles decided while grilling #48 on 2026-10-24. Like **Architecture**, it was written before the tooling: #80 set up Prettier as described below, and #81 sets up the lint rules and `erasableSyntaxOnly`. It lives here rather than in a `CODING_STANDARDS.md`, since code-review reads every file that documents how code is written (its `SKILL.md:36`, mattpocock/skills 1.3.1). The options set aside, Biome, oxfmt and ESLint Stylistic among them, and why, are in #48's decisions.

- **Prettier lays out** every file it knows: TypeScript, JavaScript, CSS, JSON, YAML and Markdown. It and the two plugins below are devDependencies, `prettier.config.mjs` is the source of truth for layout, and `.prettierignore` keeps out `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `.claude/skills/groom-backlog/`, the copy shared with EsportCards, which commit e885352 means to differ only by `config.json`, and `.claude/skills/deliver-epic/`, shared with EsportCards the same way (#257). `pnpm-workspace.yaml` stays in: in two tries with pnpm 12.9.1, a plain `pnpm add` left it alone, and a `pnpm add` of a version under a day old wrote its `minimumReleaseAgeExclude` entry in double quotes, which Prettier accepts (tried, then reverted, 2026-10-24). If a later `pnpm add` undoes Prettier's layout there, the file joins `.prettierignore` (#48's decisions). ESLint stays the linter and sets no layout rule (`pnpm exec eslint --print-config src/app/page.tsx`, 2026-10-24), so no `eslint-config-prettier`.
- **shadcn/ui's style.** Prettier's defaults with `semi: false` and `trailingComma: "es5"`, which are the registry's own: with them and the two plugins below, Prettier 3.9.10 leaves `src/ui/button.tsx` and the sources of 20 base-nova components unchanged (run on scratch copies of https://ui.shadcn.com/r/styles/base-nova/<name>.json, 2026-10-24). `shadcn add --diff` compares lines (`diffLines`, `node_modules/shadcn/dist/index.js:121`), so this style keeps it to the lines the panel changes (**Copied code**), where single quotes would add every line that holds a string. The width stays 80: at 100, Prettier changes 287 lines across 15 of those 20 components.
- **Classes** are sorted by prettier-plugin-tailwindcss, in Tailwind's recommended order, against `src/ui/globals.css`, in `className` and inside `cva()` and `cn()` calls. It drops duplicate classes and extra whitespace by default, and loads last among Prettier's plugins (https://github.com/tailwindlabs/prettier-plugin-tailwindcss, README, read 2026-10-24).
- **Imports** are sorted by `@ianvs/prettier-plugin-sort-imports`, in the registry's order: `node:` modules, `react`, `next`, other packages, then, after a blank line, `@/` and relative paths. A side-effect import stays where it is and the imports after it are sorted, so `import "server-only"` stays first (**Secrets**; tried on a scratch file, 2026-10-24).
- **Copied code** stays as `shadcn add` wrote it, beyond its French strings and stripped comments (**Copied code** of **Interface**), but for six kinds of line, which `--diff` then shows. A class the panel uses whose colors miss **Accessibility**'s level on the panel's theme takes one that holds it, as the default Button's hover does (**Copied code** of **Interface**). The Toast's list gives each toast the classes that have it only fade under reduced motion, and renders its title as a `<p>` (**Motion**, **Forms**, #227). A copied component carries beside its own the classes that adapt it to a phone and to a finger, so that no caller repeats them (**Responsive**, #355, #357; Gilles, 2026-11-03): the touch size, `h-8 touch:h-11` or `size-8 touch:size-11`, and the offset that keeps a close button's glyph in its corner; the 16 px of a field under `md`; the `env(safe-area-inset-*)` paddings; the Dialog's docking and slide under `sm`; and a Sheet from the bottom's `max-h-[88svh]` and its `SheetBody`. Prettier reformats what the CLI's own transforms leave unformatted: its icon transform writes an icon's JSX over two lines and its `lucide-react` import after the `@/` ones, so 3 to 8 lines change in each of `combobox`, `dialog`, `select` and `sheet` (`pnpm exec shadcn add <name> --dry-run --view`, 2026-10-24). A line the lint refuses carries an `eslint-disable-next-line` for its rules, and one the typecheck refuses a `@ts-expect-error` (**Directives**). Of the 20 that #55's decisions list, copied into a scratch worktree, `label` trips `jsx-a11y/label-has-associated-control`, and `input-group` `jsx-a11y/click-events-have-key-events` and `jsx-a11y/no-noninteractive-element-interactions` (`pnpm exec eslint .`, 2026-10-24). The Toast, which the panel took over Sonner (#227), trips neither the lint nor the typecheck (`pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, 2026-10-29).
- **Enforcement.** `pnpm format:check` runs in CI, right after the comment check, and a `PostToolUse` hook of `.claude/settings.json` on `Edit|Write`, `.claude/hooks/format-after-edit.mjs`, formats the file the tool touched; those two are the whole of it, with no git hook and no editor setting. The hook calls Prettier from the project's root, `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, since Prettier resolves a plugin's package name from the working directory (`loadPlugin`, `node_modules/prettier/index.mjs:18619-18624`). That root is where the session started, and a session that enters a worktree keeps it (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, read 2026-10-24), so such a session's edits in the worktree, which the main checkout's `.gitignore` lists, go unformatted until `pnpm format` (inferred from the code). It leaves alone a file outside the project, one that `.gitignore` or `.prettierignore` lists, and one Prettier does not know, and exits 2 on a file Prettier cannot parse, so that Claude reads why (`test/format-after-edit.test.mjs`). It never sees what a `Bash` command rewrites, such as `pnpm add` or `shadcn add`: `pnpm format` and CI catch that. Code that Prettier lays out badly gets reshaped, since `prettier-ignore` is a comment like any other (**No comments**), and `pnpm check:comments` reads `.prettierignore` and `.git-blame-ignore-revs` as files of `#` comments. #80's reformatting is a commit of its own, `Tooling: format the repository with Prettier`, whose SHA `.git-blame-ignore-revs` holds for GitHub's blame (https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/working-with-files/using-files/viewing-and-understanding-files, read 2026-10-24), and `git blame` too once `blame.ignoreRevsFile` points to it (https://git-scm.com/docs/git-blame; the command is in the README). A line the reformatting created, such as a closing parenthesis split off a call, has no earlier commit, so blame still names the reformatting for it: 6 lines of `scripts/check-comments.mjs`, with `blame.markUnblamableLines` (2026-10-24). A merge commit keeps that SHA (**Commits, PRs, issues**); a rebase of a reformatting branch before its merge redoes the file with the new one.
- **Names.** Files and folders are kebab-case, as in Next.js's file conventions (`not-found.tsx`, `global-error.tsx`) and shadcn/ui's registry (`alert-dialog.tsx`, `dropdown-menu.tsx`). Outside the files whose names Next.js sets, a component lives in the file named after it: `blocked-word-list.tsx` exports `BlockedWordList`, beside `blocked-word-list.test.tsx`. Values and functions are camelCase; components, types and classes PascalCase; a module-level constant that holds fixed data SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE, such as `DIRECTIVES` in `scripts/check-comments.mjs`, while other `const`s stay camelCase. An acronym is a word, `DiscordApi`, `userId`, `webhookUrl`, but for `OAuth`, as in `DiscordOAuth`, and a type takes no `I` prefix. The terms come from `GLOSSARY.md`, but in `src/discord/` (`docs/agents/domain.md`). Review checks names, since `@typescript-eslint/naming-convention` would need an exception for components; a slip that review misses becomes a check.
- **Exports** are named and written inline, `export function …`, while a copied component keeps the registry's `export { … }` at its end. A default export lives only where Next.js or a tool requires one, the files of `src/app/` and config files, the only ones `import/no-default-export` lets through. An import names the module itself, such as `@/blocked-words/add-blocked-word`, and review checks that no barrel sits in between: a barrel that re-exports a client leaf beside a `server-only` module would pull that module into the client graph (inferred, not tried).
- **TypeScript.** `type` for every object type, `import type` for what is imported as a type only, and `as const` as the one type assertion, except on the line that mints the `StaffMember` brand (ADR 0005), which carries an `eslint-disable-next-line`. Lint rules enforce the three at `error`, which #55 kept (**Warnings**), and refuse nothing in the code of 2026-10-24 (`pnpm exec eslint --rule …`). The syntax stays erasable, with no `enum`, `namespace` or parameter property, which `erasableSyntaxOnly` refuses; `tsc --noEmit --erasableSyntaxOnly` passes today.
- **Module size** is a judgement call: a module holds one idea, with no figure and no `max-lines`, and code-review's smells, Divergent Change and Shotgun Surgery, watch it.

## Warnings

What fails on a warning, and how one is silenced, as Gilles decided while grilling #55 on 2026-10-24; the options set aside, and why, are in #55's decisions. Like **Architecture**, it was written before the code: #89 sets up ESLint's side, #86 TypeScript's options, #87 CI's checks, #88 the tests' console, #23 the directives, and #63 the browser's console end to end.

- **ESLint.** `pnpm lint` runs `eslint --max-warnings 0`, at the desk as in CI, so a rule a config sets to `warn` blocks like one set to `error`. The severities stay as the configs set them: the merged config sets 20 rules to `warn` on `src/app/page.tsx`, `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` and `@next/next/no-img-element` among them (`pnpm exec eslint --print-config src/app/page.tsx`, 2026-10-24), jsx-a11y's stay `error` (**Accessibility lint**), and so do #81's (**TypeScript**). A copied line that a `warn` rule refuses carries its `eslint-disable-next-line` as one an `error` rule refuses does (**Copied code** of **Code style**). `linterOptions.reportUnusedDisableDirectives` is `"error"`, where ESLint 10 leaves it at warn (the same command). `no-console` is an error in `src/`, but in `src/monitoring/`, the one folder that writes lines (**Monitoring**); scripts and hooks print by design.
- **Type-aware lint.** typescript-eslint's `recommendedTypeChecked` and `@typescript-eslint/no-deprecated` run at `error` on `ts`, `tsx` and `mts` files, through `projectService`. The `.mjs` files stay untyped: `tsconfig.json` leaves them out, and `projectService` refuses all 13 (scratch worktree, 2026-10-24). Set after `eslint-config-next`'s configs, `recommendedTypeChecked` raises `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` and `no-unused-expressions` from the `warn` that `eslint-config-next` gives them (`node_modules/eslint-config-next/dist/typescript.js:36-37`) back to `error`, which `--max-warnings 0` makes moot (inferred from the flat config's order, **Gotchas**). `no-deprecated` catches a deprecated API, React's, Next.js's or zod's, and `no-floating-promises` and `no-misused-promises` a Server Action's forgotten `await`. `typescript-eslint` becomes a devDependency, at the version `eslint-config-next` uses, since it does not resolve from the root (`ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`; 8.72.0 through `eslint-config-next`, 2026-10-24). On `main`, and on 20 base-nova components copied into a scratch worktree, these rules found nothing, and took the lint from about 9.2 s to 13.4 s, about 1 s of it `no-deprecated`'s (medians of five runs, and `TIMING`, 2026-10-24). `Response.json()` returns `Promise<any>` (`node_modules/typescript/lib/lib.dom.d.ts:5127`), Discord's responses are typed, not validated (**Validation**), and `as` is refused (**TypeScript**), so `src/discord/` reads Discord's JSON in one helper, whose line carries an `eslint-disable-next-line` for the `no-unsafe-*` rule it trips (inferred, not tried). Should `pnpm lint` on a fresh clone, before `next typegen` has written the route types, fail for want of them, `lint` runs `next typegen` first, as `typecheck` does: #89 tries it.
- **TypeScript's options.** `tsconfig.json` adds `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, `noImplicitOverride`, `noImplicitReturns` and `noFallthroughCasesInSwitch` to `strict`, since they cost nothing today and would touch more code the later they came: on `main` each gives no error, and on the scratch worktree's components only `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` gives one, TS2375 on `sonner.tsx`'s `theme={theme as ToasterProps["theme"]}`, with `skipLibCheck` on or off (`pnpm exec tsc --noEmit --<option>`, 2026-10-24). `noUnusedLocals` and `noUnusedParameters` stay off, since `@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars` checks the same, and so does `verbatimModuleSyntax`, since `consistent-type-imports` does: one check, one tool. `noPropertyAccessFromIndexSignature` stays off too: it would have `next.config.ts` write `process.env["VERCEL"]`, and catches nothing `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` misses (inferred). `erasableSyntaxOnly` is #81's (**TypeScript**). `ignoreDeprecations` is never set: TypeScript 6 makes a deprecated option an error, TS5107, unless it is (`pnpm exec tsc --noEmit --target ES5`, 2026-10-24).
- **CI's output.** Next.js prints a warning through `Log.warn`, behind `⚠` (`node_modules/next/dist/build/output/log.js:64`), and Node prints a process warning as `(node:<pid>) [DEP0040] DeprecationWarning: …` or `(node:<pid>) Warning: …` (Node 24.22.0, `require("punycode")` and `process.emitWarning`, 2026-10-24). A script of CI reads the output of `pnpm build`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm typecheck`, and fails on every `⚠` line of the build and every such Node line of the three that its list of exceptions does not hold. The commands themselves stay as they are, so Vercel never fails a deployment over a warning. The list starts with « No build cache found », which CI printed on run 40124210901 and which leaves with the build cache of #103; the local build of 2026-10-24 printed no `⚠`. A dependency's own `console.warn` follows no format, so it stays advisory: `baseline-browser-mapping` 2.11.30 writes one, with no `⚠`, once its data is two months old (`node_modules/.pnpm/baseline-browser-mapping@2.11.30/node_modules/baseline-browser-mapping/dist/index.cjs:1`). The output is no API either, so a Next.js that changed its glyph would pass the check unseen (inferred). A warning that follows the date rather than the code can still turn an unrelated PR red, and is then fixed like any other, by an update or an exception (**Reasons**), since no PR merges red (**Feedback loops**).
- **pnpm.** CI's `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` skips resolution when the lockfile is up to date, and so prints no peer or deprecation warning (« Lockfile is up to date, resolution step is skipped », 2026-10-24): those come at `pnpm add` and `pnpm update`. CI runs `pnpm peers check` right after it, which reads the lockfile without resolving (inferred) and answered « No peer dependency issues found » on 2026-10-24, with the three `peerDependencyRules` of `.claude/rules/dependencies.md`. A deprecated package is handled by **Dependencies**.
- **The tests' console.** Under Vitest and `node --test`, a test fails when it writes to the console, through any method, or when Node emits a process warning, a deprecation included, unless the test captures that line and asserts it, as the test of a failed alert send does with its `console.error` (**Testing monitoring**). React's development warnings, such as a missing `key`, go through `console.error`, and `NODE_ENV` is `test` under Vitest, so they fail the test that renders them (inferred). Neither tool fails on the console by default (inferred), and `pnpm test` writes nothing to it today (2026-10-24).
- **The browser's console.** End to end, a browser console message at `error` or `warning`, or a `pageerror`, fails the test, a Content Security Policy violation included: Chromium logs one at `error`, and a throwaway test that loaded an image from another origin failed on « Loading the image '…' violates the following Content Security Policy directive » (#76, 2026-10-27). An automatic fixture of `e2e/fixtures.ts` collects them and asserts there are none once the test ends; throwaway tests showed each of the three failing a test, and a `console.log` failing none (#63, 2026-10-25). A test that expects a line declares it, by its exact text, through the fixture's `expectProblems`, and then fails on any other line and on a declared one that never comes, in whatever order they come and however many times each comes (#61). Chromium logs a document that answers 404 as « Failed to load resource: the server responded with a status of 404 (Not Found) ». The flow where Discord is unavailable while the audit log is read declares two more, which React and Next.js raise by design (react-dom `19.3.0-canary-278794d7-20261002`, Next.js 16.4.2, #61). It fails `getAuditLog` once, a read that only the page makes: the AutoMod rules' read, which the layout's count of blocked words makes too, raced the count, whose call came first took the one failure, and the count swallowed it while the page rendered (`e2e/staff-header.test.ts:407`), and the fake keeps one failure per method, so two cannot queue (`src/discord/fake-discord.ts:302`, `:334`, `:843-851`). The first line is « Error: Minified React error #441 » in `console.error`, once, as the error page appears. #441 is the production error that React's Flight client builds for an error row of the RSC stream, whose message the server leaves out, and sets the row's digest on (`node_modules/next/dist/compiled/react-server-dom-turbopack/cjs/react-server-dom-turbopack-client.browser.production.js:1690-1693`, `:1826-1831`); its full text, « An error occurred in the Server Components render. The specific message is omitted in production builds… », is in the node build of the same client (`react-server-dom-turbopack-client.node.production.js:1906`). Next.js hands `hydrateRoot` an `onCaughtError` (`node_modules/next/dist/client/app-index.js:227`) that prints what an explicit error boundary, here `error.tsx`, catches, in production through `console.error` (`node_modules/next/dist/client/react-client-callbacks/error-boundary-callbacks.js:103`), so every read that fails brings the line. The other is « Minified React error #419 », a `pageerror`, right after « Réessayer », which comes twice or once. #419 is « The server could not finish this Suspense boundary, likely due to an error during server rendering. Switched to client rendering. » (`node_modules/next/dist/compiled/react-dom/cjs/react-dom-client.development.js:11500`, `react-dom-client.production.js:7393`). React raises it where it hydrates a Suspense boundary that the server flagged for client rendering, `$RX("B:4","<digest>")` in the streamed HTML (`react-dom-client.development.js:11479-11512`), and queues it as a hydration error (`:5736`); Next.js's `onRecoverableError` (`app-index.js:226`) hands it to `reportError` (`on-recoverable-error.js:57`, `report-global-error.js:11-12`, in `react-client-callbacks/`), which fires the `window`'s `error` event. Each carries the digest of the server's error, the one `error.tsx` shows. The server flags one boundary on `/audit-log` and three on `/blocked-words`, and the client raised two on each in every local run, also under a CPU 8 times slower, but one on CI's runner (run 40977975608, where this flow failed on the count), so the count follows no boundary's count and no fixed number, and the fixture takes each declared line once, however many times it comes. The two of a local run share one call stack, the `reportError` call of the loop that reports a commit's recoverable errors, 0.4 ms apart, so they are two entries of one queue of hydration errors (a throwaway probe of the streamed HTML, of `reportError` and of its stack, 2026-10-30). Why they come after the click and not at load, and why the queue holds two entries or one, is inferred, not traced: none comes in the 700 ms that follow the load, the DOM holds no `<!--$!-->` marker at the error page nor after the retry, and React clears its queue of hydration errors only where a hydration starts and drains it where a boundary or the root completes (`react-dom-client.development.js:5724`, `:11567`, `:12302`, `:12917`, `:13263`), so the entries that the render which threw to `error.tsx` queued probably wait for the render that « Réessayer » starts, and how many times that render reached the flagged boundary probably depends on when the `$RX` instruction came, against the hydration. Nor is #419 bound to the click: a first version of #362's flow of the error page, which never clicked « Réessayer » and declared #441 alone, got it once on CI's runner, at 390 px, and none in 60 local runs, 30 of them under a CPU slowdown of ×6 (run 41683657341, 2026-11-03), so a flow that shows the error page declares #419 only where it clicks « Réessayer », whose click brought it in each of the 60 runs of the corrected flow, and the list of accounts' flow, which declares #441 alone, may meet the same (inferred, not tried). The console of `next start` fails nothing, since the flow where Discord is unavailable during a read has Next.js print the error, by design (**What is logged**).
- **The runner.** What CI's runner and actions print themselves fails nothing: `actions/checkout`'s `hint:` about the default branch name, `pnpm/action-setup`'s `[WARN] Detected a pnpm v10 installation layout`, and the notice that `ubuntu-latest` moves to Ubuntu 26, all on run 40124210901. CI is **Delivery**'s, whose jobs run on `ubuntu-24.04` once #95 lands, which should end that notice (inferred), and the actions' versions are **Dependencies**', where #95 takes `pnpm/action-setup`'s line on.
- **Silencing.** A warning's cause is fixed first. A warning is silenced only when it is wrong for that code, or when it comes from code the panel keeps as its source wrote it: copied code (**Copied code** of **Code style**), or a dependency.
- **Directives.** On a line, the one ESLint directive is `eslint-disable-next-line` followed by one or more rule names, separated by commas, with no `-- …` and no other text. `eslint-disable` and `eslint-enable`, whether they span a block or a whole file, `eslint-disable-line`, and a directive that names no rule are refused by `pnpm check:comments`, which has accepted none of them since #23 (`test/check-comments.test.mjs`). It takes the block form, `/* eslint-disable-next-line … */`, which JSX needs, as it takes the line form. Directives exist only in script files, `.js`, `.mjs`, `.cjs`, `.jsx`, `.ts`, `.mts`, `.cts` and `.tsx`: in a `.json` or `.jsonc` file `pnpm check:comments` reports every comment, a directive's form included, since no tool of the repository reads one there (#289). A scratch project (2026-10-30) showed that `tsc` 6.0.3 reports an unused `// @ts-expect-error` as TS2578 in a `.ts` file and nothing in a `.json` file that a `.ts` file imports, and `pnpm exec eslint tsconfig.json` answers « File ignored because no matching configuration was supplied », as it does for a throwaway `.json` file under `src/` and under `e2e/` (2026-10-30). The extension globs of `eslint.config.mjs` name code files only (`eslint.config.mjs:13`, `:23`, `:28`), which is inferred to be why, since its folder globs (`:36`, `:63`, `:85`) did not select those two files. That `/// <reference`, `@vite-ignore` and `webpackIgnore` are read in JavaScript and TypeScript modules alone, and that no tool reads a directive in a `.jsonc` file, which shares the `json` family with `.json` and was not tried, are inferred. A rule name is segments joined by `/`, each starting with a letter or a digit and going on with letters, digits, `_` and `-`, with an optional leading `@`, and the names are separated by commas, so that a run of dashes or underscores alone names no rule. ESLint reads a disable directive's list through `parseListConfig`, which splits it on commas, trims each name and keeps one with a space in it whole (`node_modules/eslint/lib/linter/linter.js:243`, `node_modules/.pnpm/@eslint+plugin-kit@0.7.3/node_modules/@eslint/plugin-kit/dist/esm/index.js:183-200`), so that `a b` would be one name that matches no rule (inferred) (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #23, for Gilles to review). `@ts-expect-error` stands alone, and `@typescript-eslint/ban-ts-comment` takes `"ts-expect-error": false`, since its default refuses one without a description of 3 characters or more (scratch file, 2026-10-24); `@ts-ignore` and `@ts-nocheck` stay refused, by that rule and by the comment check. `eslint.config.mjs` sets the option in an object of its own, `admin-panel/ban-ts-comment`, with no new dependency, and `test/eslint-config.test.mjs` lints a virtual `src/probe.ts` through ESLint's API to prove the three cases (decided by the agent on 2026-10-30, on #23, for Gilles to review, since no file of the repository holds a lone `@ts-expect-error` for `pnpm lint` to prove it). That object has to stay after every config that sets the rule: `recommended-type-checked` sets `@typescript-eslint/ban-ts-comment` too (`typescript-eslint` 8.72.0, `@typescript-eslint/eslint-plugin/dist/configs/flat/recommended-type-checked.js:25`), and in flat config the last object to set a rule replaces its options (**Gotchas**), so #89, which adds that config, puts it before this object, and the test fails otherwise (inferred, #89 being open).
- **Reasons.** A directive's why goes in the message of the commit that adds it, and code-review refuses a new directive whose commit gives none. A silence that waits on something, such as a `peerDependencyRules` entry, an exception of CI's list (**CI's output**) or an option set until an upstream fix, gets a line in the rule of its area, under `.claude/rules/`, that says what lets it go, as the three `peerDependencyRules` have in `dependencies.md`. A standing choice, such as `ban-ts-comment`'s option or `no-console`'s exemption for `src/monitoring/`, is recorded in the section that makes it, as this one does.

## Dependencies

How the panel's dependencies move to new versions, as Gilles decided while grilling #56 on 2026-10-25; the options set aside, and why, are in #56's decisions. Like **Architecture**, it was written before the code: #92 sets up exact versions and the release age, #93 the audits and the duplicate check, #94 GitHub's alerts, #95 the actions, #96 Node 24, and #97 the monthly session.

- **What it covers.** npm packages, direct and transitive; the actions of `ci.yml`; pnpm itself, through `packageManager`; the Node major the panel runs on; the code copied from shadcn/ui; adding a dependency; and copying code from another project. The copies of `groom-backlog` and `deliver-epic` shared with EsportCards follow their own commits (e885352, #257), and Claude Code's plugins live outside the repository.
- **Who and when.** No bot. Once a month, in its first week, Gilles starts a session of `/update-dependencies`, a skill of `.claude/skills/update-dependencies/` (#97), which runs the updates and the monthly check of **Costs**; he starts one out of cycle too when the daily audit fails (**Advisories**) or a ticket needs a newer version. Its commits are his, and he merges. The rules live here, the procedure in the skill. Dependabot and Renovate were set aside: neither re-reads the citations that name a version (**Docs that name a version**), so each of their PRs would need a session anyway; #56's decisions give the other reasons (Q9).
- **Exact versions.** Every direct dependency names an exact version, TypeScript included, and `savePrefix: ""` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` makes `pnpm add` write one, where pnpm's default is `^` (`pnpm help add`): without a version, `pnpm add is-number` wrote `"^7.0.0"` before the setting and `"7.0.0"` after it, in a throwaway copy of the repository's package files (2026-11-02, #92).
