CI installs the lockfile as it is (`--frozen-lockfile`), so a range acts only when pnpm resolves again; with exact versions, `package.json` says what runs, and every update shows in its diff. `engines.node` keeps its range, `^24.0.0`, since it names the Node major rather than a dependency (**Node**), though #92 asks that `package.json` hold no `^` (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #92, for Gilles to review, since #92's decisions name the direct dependencies alone).
- **Release age.** `pnpm-workspace.yaml` sets `minimumReleaseAge: 10080`, 7 days. Written down, the setting makes pnpm strict (**Gotchas**): on a version younger than that, it asks at a TTY, writing the exclusion only if told yes, and fails without one, instead of excluding the version from the rule by itself (https://github.com/pnpm/pnpm/blob/v12.9.1/pnpm/crates/package-manager/src/minimum_release_age.rs#L146-L191). It also checks every entry of the lockfile, at `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` too, and refuses one under 7 days old (**Gotchas**), so CI's install should fail on a lockfile that a version slipped into past the rule (inferred from a throwaway copy): CI runs that check, which its 712 entries passed on 2026-11-02 (« ✓ Lockfile passes supply-chain policies (712 entries in 3.5s) », run 41514393274, #92). An exclusion goes in only for a security fix that touches the panel and that an advisory attests, written by hand or approved at that prompt, on the exact version, with a line of `.claude/rules/dependencies.md` saying it goes once that version is 7 days old; any other prompt is answered no. The repository's age "never reaches `self-update`" (https://github.com/pnpm/pnpm/blob/v12.9.1/pnpm/crates/config/src/registry_options.rs#L200-L212), so the session reads a pnpm version's date, `npm view pnpm time`, before taking it (inferred from that comment; #97 checks it).
- **Majors.** A major, or a minor of a 0.x package, such as `cn`, `class-variance-authority` and `prettier-plugin-tailwindcss` today (`package.json`), comes in once what depends on it declares it, and at the latest when the current major reaches its end of life. One that must come in earlier gets a `peerDependencyRules` entry and its line in `.claude/rules/dependencies.md`, as ESLint 10 did. Each major has a PR of its own: its release notes and migration guide are read and its codemod run, if it has one, the PR says what they changed, and every page is opened under `pnpm dev`.
- **Checks.** Every update passes CI on its PR, end to end included once #63 lands. An update of Prettier or of one of its plugins also passes `pnpm exec shadcn add <component> --diff` on every copied component (**Code style**), and its reformatting is a commit of its own, `Tooling: reformat with Prettier <version>`, whose SHA joins `.git-blame-ignore-revs` in the next commit of the same PR, which the merge commit keeps (**Enforcement**). An update of Node also passes `pnpm test:contract`, since the `fetch` of the real implementations is Node's (**The real implementations**), and its pull request's Vercel build log still shows « Detected ENABLE_EXPERIMENTAL_COREPACK=1 » and the pnpm `packageManager` names (**Gotchas**).
- **Docs that name a version.** On 2026-10-25, `CLAUDE.md` and the ADRs held 42 `node_modules/…:line` citations, 33 of them into Next.js (`grep -oE 'node_modules/[^ ]+:[0-9]+' CLAUDE.md docs/adr/*.md`), and, by an agent's count, 12 short back-references to the same files and about 42 sentences that name a version. A patch moves lines too, so the commit that moves a package re-reads that package's citations, patches included: a citation whose text moved gets its new `path:line`, a claim whose behaviour changed is rewritten or deleted, and a line of `.claude/rules/` tied to a version is tried again. A sentence that reports a dated trial stays while its conclusion holds, and one that describes the present follows the update. A script of `scripts/` lists a package's citations (#97).
- **Holds.** A version held back is one line of `.claude/rules/dependencies.md`: the package, the range it stays in, why, and what lets it go, as for `@babel/parser` 7.x and TypeScript 6.0 today; nothing else configures it. The session reads those lines first and tries again each hold, and each `peerDependencyRules` entry (**Majors**), whose condition it can check, through `npm view <package> peerDependencies` or `engines`. That `@types/node` follows the Node major (**Node**) is a standing rule, not a hold.
- **Node.** The panel runs on one Node major, the same on Vercel, in CI's main job and in `@types/node`: 24, since #96. Vercel offers 24.x, its default, and 22.x; `engines.node` wins over the project's setting, and `>=22.0.0` deploys the latest 24.x (https://vercel.com/docs/functions/runtimes/node-js/node-js-versions, updated 2026-02-27, read again 2026-10-29). So `engines.node` names that major, `^24.0.0`, within the ranges the toolchain declares (**Gotchas**): the range it replaced, `^22.13.0 || ^24.0.0 || >=26.0.0`, would have deployed 26 without a PR the day Vercel offered it (inferred from that page's table). This reopened the decision of 2026-10-23 that `engines.node` is the intersection of those ranges (Gilles, 2026-10-25, while grilling #56). `@types/node` names an exact 24.x (**Exact versions**): 24.19.2, the latest on 2026-10-29 (`npm view @types/node time`). A new major comes in one PR that moves `engines.node` and `@types/node` together, which moves Vercel and CI with them, once Vercel offers the next LTS, and at the latest when the current one reaches its end of life, 2028-04-30 for 24 (`schedule.json` of https://github.com/nodejs/Release, read 2026-10-25). CI tests only the latest version of that major, which `actions/setup-node` reads from `engines.node` (**CI**).
- **pnpm itself** follows a devDependency's rules, through the exact version of `packageManager`.
- **Actions** name their full version tag, such as `pnpm/action-setup@v6.1.0`: it reads without a comment (**No comments**), and an update shows in the diff, where a major tag can lag, as `pnpm/action-setup`'s `v6` still pointed to v6.0.10, which predates its pnpm 12 support (pnpm/action-setup#288 and #289, 2026-10-25). A workflow whose job holds a secret pins its actions by SHA instead, as `contract.yml` does, while `ci.yml` keeps its tags (**Delivery**). `pnpm outdated --include-github-actions` lists them with the packages (`pnpm help outdated`).
- **Advisories.** GitHub's dependency graph reads no lockfile of two YAML documents, which pnpm 12 writes, so it raises no Dependabot alert (https://github.blog/changelog/2026-10-20-dependabot-now-supports-npm-12-pnpm-11-and-12-and-bun-1-4/); #94 turns the graph and the alerts on all the same, for the day it does, but not Dependabot's security updates. CI runs `pnpm audit` and `pnpm audit signatures` after the install, on every PR and every push to `main`, and once a day on `main`, which notifies "the user who initially created the workflow" (https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/workflows-and-actions/notifications-for-workflow-runs, read 2026-10-25). `pnpm audit` covers every dependency, devDependencies included, and fails on any advisory at its default level, `low` (https://pnpm.io/cli/audit, read 2026-10-25; https://github.com/pnpm/pnpm/blob/v12.9.1/pnpm/crates/cli/src/cli_args/audit.rs#L392-L402). An advisory is fixed by an update. It is ignored, through `--ignore <GHSA>`, which writes it under `auditConfig.ignoreGhsas` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (pnpm 12.9.1, 2026-10-25), only when the panel cannot reach the vulnerable code, with its line in `.claude/rules/dependencies.md`, and the `overrides` that `pnpm audit --fix` writes are a last resort, with theirs (**Reasons**). An advisory published while a PR is open should turn it red with no change of code, as a warning that follows the date does (**CI's output**; inferred).
- **One copy.** CI runs `pnpm dedupe --check` after the install, which fails when two copies of a package could be one (`pnpm help dedupe`). `typescript-eslint`, which #89 makes a direct devDependency at the version `eslint-config-next` uses (**Type-aware lint**), moves in the same commit as `eslint-config-next`, which declares `^8.56.0` (`npm view eslint-config-next@16.4.3 dependencies.typescript-eslint`, 2026-10-25).
- **Transitive dependencies** are refreshed at each session by `pnpm update`, in a commit of its own. With exact direct versions, it should move only the transitive ones, within their ranges, since its `--depth` defaults to unlimited (`pnpm help update`; inferred, #97 tries it first). The release age guards it.
- **Deprecated packages.** pnpm prints a deprecation only at `pnpm add` and `pnpm update` (**Warnings**), and records it as a `deprecated:` field of `pnpm-lock.yaml`, which held none on 2026-10-25. The session reads both: a deprecated direct dependency becomes a ticket to replace it, and a transitive one waits for its parent to move, unless `pnpm audit` flags it or it raises a warning in CI.
- **The registry.** Each session runs `pnpm exec shadcn add <component> --diff` on every copied component. What the registry changed comes in like a minor update, and the panel's own lines stay (**Copied code** of **Interface** and of **Code style**).
- **Adding a dependency.** Before `pnpm add`, its `engines`, which must hold `engines.node` (**Gotchas**), its `peerDependencies`, and whether it is deprecated, are read, and one that reaches a hosted service when it installs or runs first gets its entry in `docs/costs.md` (**Costs**). After it, the version is exact, its build script stays refused in `allowBuilds` unless it cannot work without one, which its commit then explains, `pnpm-workspace.yaml` is read again, and `pnpm audit` and `pnpm dedupe --check` pass.
- **Copying code from another project.** Before code comes into the repository from another project, by hand or through a tool such as shadcn/ui's CLI, its license is read (#106). Code under a permissive license, such as shadcn/ui's MIT, adds the notice that its license asks every copy to carry to `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` in the same commit, in a section of its own that says which files it covers, as shadcn/ui's does (the section decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #106, for Gilles to review, since #106 says only that the notice goes there). A notice the file already holds covers its project's later copies, as shadcn/ui's covers every component the CLI copies into `src/ui/` (#101). Apache-2.0, permissive too, asks more: "You must cause any modified files to carry prominent notices stating that You changed the files" (its section 4(b), https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0.txt, read 2026-11-02), which only a comment in each file could do, against **No comments** (inferred), so its terms are read again the day such code comes in. Code under a copyleft license, such as skymp-heavy-rp's AGPL-3.0 (`gh api repos/vinicius3232/skymp-heavy-rp/license`, 2026-11-02), is not copied: the panel would become a derivative work to be published under that license, against the absence of a license that ADR 0007 keeps (inferred from the license, not a lawyer's reading). Code from a project with no license, which default copyright reserves to its authors (README, **License**), comes in only when the panel's maintainer wrote it alone (decided by the agent on 2026-11-02, on #106, for Gilles to review, since #106 names the permissive and copyleft cases alone). EsportCards, Gilles' private repository, has no license (`gh api repos/PothieuG/EsportCards`, 2026-11-02) and a second contributor, whose 296 commits outnumber Gilles' 289 (its `contributors`, the same day), while the 21 commits of its `.claude/skills/groom-backlog/`, the copy shared with the panel (**What it covers**), are all Gilles' (its `commits?path=.claude/skills/groom-backlog`, the same day), so that copy may come in and the rest of EsportCards only where Gilles wrote it alone (inferred from the commits' authors). The licenses of npm dependencies, which `pnpm add` installs without copying them into the repository, stay out of this rule (#106).
- **The session's PR.** One per session for all but the majors, on `dependencies/<yyyy-mm>`. It holds a commit per package, or per set that must move together, such as Next.js and `eslint-config-next`, or React and its types, with the docs that package changes: `Dependencies: update next and eslint-config-next to 16.4.3`. The refresh of transitive dependencies follows, as `Dependencies: refresh the lockfile`. For each package, the PR gives the versions it moves between, what was read, what changes for the panel, and the citations re-read.

## Delivery

How code goes from a branch to production, where it runs on the way, and what CI checks, as Gilles decided while grilling #57 on 2026-10-25; the options set aside, and why, are in #57's decisions and in ADR 0007. Like **Architecture**, it was written before the code: #101 and #100 open the repository, #96 and #95 set CI's Node and runner, #103 the build cache, #102 `contract.yml`, #9 the Vercel project and #13 the first production deploy.

- **The repository** has been public since 2026-10-27, because Vercel's Hobby plan deploys no private repository of a GitHub organization, and ADR 0007 lists what going private again would break. #100 published it once a secret scan of the whole history, pull requests' refs included, found nothing; once the passages Gilles chose to withhold from the issues were rewritten and their earlier versions deleted (#100), since "anyone with read access to a repository can view a comment's edit history" (https://docs.github.com/en/communities/moderating-comments-and-conversations/tracking-changes-in-a-comment, read 2026-10-27); and once the POs and loanspac, owners of the organization like Gilles (`gh api orgs/ShorRP/members?role=admin`, 2026-10-25), agreed. It carries no license, so its code can be read but not reused (https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/licensing-a-repository, read 2026-10-25): `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` carries shadcn/ui's MIT notice for the code copied into `src/ui/` (https://github.com/shadcn-ui/ui/blob/main/LICENSE.md, read 2026-10-25), and `SECURITY.md` asks for vulnerabilities through GitHub's private reporting rather than in issues (#101). A public repository gets secret scanning, push protection and private vulnerability reporting with no license (https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/managing-repository-settings/setting-repository-visibility, read 2026-10-25), but GitHub left the first two off at the switch, and #100 turned all three on (`security_and_analysis` of `gh api repos/ShorRP/Admin_Panel`, read right after it, 2026-10-27). Gilles' commits carry his GitHub `noreply` address, set in this repository's `.git/config`, and his account keeps his other address private and refuses a push that would expose it (Gilles, 2026-10-27), while the earlier commits keep theirs, since rewriting them would change every SHA the docs and the issues cite.
- **Outside contributions.** Issues and pull requests from outside follow `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`, **Outside contributions**. A fork's workflow runs wait for a maintainer's approval, at GitHub's strictest setting, `all_external_contributors`, which #100 set in place of the default `first_time_contributors` (`gh api repos/ShorRP/Admin_Panel/actions/permissions/fork-pr-contributor-approval`, 2026-10-27), and get no secret (https://docs.github.com/en/actions/security-for-github-actions/security-guides/using-secrets-in-github-actions, read 2026-10-25), and Vercel deploys a fork's pull request only once it is authorized (https://vercel.com/docs/git, updated 2026-09-18).
- **CI** is `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: one job, `Check`, on `ubuntu-24.04` (#95), run on every pull request, every push to `main`, and once a day on `main` (**Advisories**). It reads Node from `engines.node`, through `actions/setup-node`'s `node-version-file: package.json` with `check-latest: true` (its README and `docs/advanced-usage.md`, read 2026-10-25), so it runs the latest version of the panel's one major, as Vercel does, and names no version of its own (#96). That version comes from the manifest of `actions/node-versions` first, which can lag Node's own releases: on 2026-10-29, CI ran 24.22.1 (run 40898070133), while https://nodejs.org/dist/index.json listed 24.22.2, of 2026-10-27. The floor of `engines.node` goes untested, since no deployment runs it, and no job tries the next major, which would either block merges or be ignored. The monthly session (**Dependencies**) moves the runner to the next Ubuntu in a pull request, once GitHub offers it and Playwright supports it. A public repository's standard runners cost nothing (https://docs.github.com/en/billing/managing-billing-for-your-products/managing-billing-for-github-actions/about-billing-for-github-actions, read 2026-10-25).
- **Caches.** `actions/setup-node`'s `cache: pnpm` keeps the pnpm store, and `actions/cache` keeps `.next/cache`, keyed on `pnpm-lock.yaml` and the sources as Next.js's guide does (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/ci-build-caching.md:73-89`), so the build stops warning that it found no cache (#103). Playwright's browsers are downloaded on every run, since Playwright advises against caching them in CI (https://playwright.dev/docs/ci, read 2026-10-25). No cache spares `pnpm audit` or `pnpm audit signatures` their call to the registry (inferred from https://pnpm.io/cli/audit, read 2026-10-25). GitHub evicts a cache unused for 7 days, and a repository holds 10 GB of them (https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/dependency-caching, read 2026-10-25).
- **The contract suite in CI.** `contract.yml` runs `pnpm test:contract` on a pull request that touches `src/discord/**` or `contract.yml` itself, once a week on `main`, and on demand through `workflow_dispatch`, and skips a pull request from a fork. Its job reads the GitHub environment `discord-contract`: `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` as a secret, which Gilles sets himself through a masked prompt, and `DISCORD_GUILD_ID`, `CONTRACT_MEMBER_ID` and `CONTRACT_ABSENT_USER_ID` as variables (#179, #181). The environment has no reviewer, who would have Gilles approve each run of his own pull requests, and allows every branch, since pull requests run it. The workflow pins its actions by SHA (**Actions**), restores no cache, since a cache written by `ci.yml`, whose actions follow tags, could carry code into the job that holds the token (inferred), and keeps two runs from overlapping on the test Discord server through one concurrency group. A scheduled run that fails notifies Gilles, who created the workflow (**Advisories**). Running the suite by hand stays possible (**The contract suite**).
- **The rules of `main`.** `main` carries a ruleset named `main` that nobody bypasses, Gilles included: a pull request, with no approval, since Gilles works alone, merged with a merge commit; `Check` green on a branch up to date with `main`, so the merge commit has the tree CI tested, and Vercel's status, `Vercel`, which #9 added on 2026-10-27 (`gh api repos/ShorRP/Admin_Panel/rulesets/25946856`; **Previews**); no force push and no deletion. The repository allows merge commits only. #100 set both on 2026-10-27. The organization's other owners could lift the ruleset (inferred), so that only Gilles, or an agent running `deliver-epic` or `deliver-backlog`, merges stays a convention (**Commits, PRs, issues**). GitHub's page on rulesets names only GitHub Team and Enterprise (https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/configuring-branches-and-merges-in-your-repository/managing-rulesets/about-rulesets, read 2026-10-25), yet the organization's `free` plan (`gh api orgs/ShorRP`) took this one once the repository was public, after the 403 of **Feedback loops** (`gh api repos/ShorRP/Admin_Panel/rulesets`, 2026-10-27).
- **Environments.** Gilles' password manager holds every value. `.env.local`, which he types through a masked prompt, holds those of "Shor Panel Dev" and of his test Discord server (#7), for `pnpm dev` and `pnpm test:contract`. CI holds none but `discord-contract`'s, and end to end runs on the placeholders of Playwright's `webServer` (**End to end**). On Vercel, Preview gets "Shor Panel Dev" and the test Discord server, Production "Shor Panel" and Shor's Discord server (#9, comment of 2026-10-23), each with a `SESSION_SECRET` of its own, and Production alone `ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` (**Monitoring**). Production and Preview also hold `ENABLE_EXPERIMENTAL_COREPACK=1` (Gilles, 2026-10-27, on #9), so that Vercel installs with the `pnpm@12.9.1` of `packageManager` rather than the pnpm 9 or 10 it infers from `lockfileVersion: 9.0` (https://vercel.com/docs/package-managers, updated 2026-08-11): the preview build of dfd4ddd logged « Detected ENABLE_EXPERIMENTAL_COREPACK=1 and "pnpm@12.9.1" in package.json », then « Done in 67ms using pnpm v12.9.1 » (2026-10-27). Vercel's Development environment stays empty, since nothing runs `vercel dev` or `vercel env pull`.
- **Previews.** Vercel deploys every pushed branch as a preview, under Standard Protection, which on Hobby lets in Gilles, one external user and one shareable link per account (https://vercel.com/docs/deployment-protection/usage-and-pricing, updated 2026-09-15). The first, of 600cbce on 2026-10-27, succeeded (`gh api repos/ShorRP/Admin_Panel/deployments/7359753181/statuses`), and its URL answered a visitor without a Vercel session with a `302` to Vercel's login (`curl`, 2026-10-27). A preview has no login by default, since Discord documents neither a wildcard redirect URI nor an endpoint that edits them (https://docs.discord.com/developers/resources/application, read 2026-10-25). A pull request that touches the login, the session or the headers gets it on demand: Gilles registers its branch URL, `<project>-git-<branch>-<scope>.vercel.app` (https://vercel.com/docs/deployments/generated-urls, updated 2026-09-08), in "Shor Panel Dev", sets `DISCORD_REDIRECT_URI` for that branch alone, as Preview variables allow (https://vercel.com/docs/environment-variables, updated 2026-09-17), tries by hand what differs only for real, cookies over `https` and the headers Vercel serves, and removes both once it merges. Every pull request also waits for Vercel's commit status (https://vercel.com/docs/git/vercel-for-github, updated 2026-09-17), which proves the build with Preview's variables, on Vercel's Node and the pnpm `packageManager` names (**Configuration**, **Environments**). #9 turns Vercel's bot comments off, and keeps its statuses and the deployment events that put the preview's link on the pull request.
- **Production.** Every merge into `main` deploys to production, Vercel's default (https://vercel.com/docs/git, updated 2026-09-18). With the up-to-date branch the ruleset requires, Deployment Checks would prove nothing more, and their page does not say Hobby has them (https://vercel.com/docs/deployment-checks, updated 2026-08-11). A merge that only changes docs deploys too, since a build the Ignored Build Step cancels still counts toward the deployment quotas (https://vercel.com/docs/project-configuration/project-settings, updated 2026-09-21). There is no version number and no tag: a deployment goes by its short commit, which an alert carries (**What an alert says**).
- **Rollback.** A bad production deployment is first undone with Instant Rollback, which on Hobby reaches only the deployment right before it, and which turns off the automatic assignment of production domains (https://vercel.com/docs/instant-rollback, updated 2026-07-07). A revert then goes through a pull request like any change, and promoting its deployment, from the dashboard or with `vercel promote`, turns that assignment back on. When the deployment before is bad too, the revert alone fixes it.
- **Scheduled workflows** of a public repository stop after 60 days without activity in it (https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/events-that-trigger-workflows, read 2026-10-25), which would stop the daily audit and the weekly contract run. The monthly session's pull request keeps the repository active (inferred), and `.claude/rules/ci.md` holds the line.
- **The trial.** Vercel's limits page says a Hobby project connects to no repository of a Git organization, without telling public from private (https://vercel.com/docs/limits, updated 2026-09-16), while its Git page offers a public repository as the way out (https://vercel.com/docs/git, updated 2026-09-18). The Git page held: on 2026-10-27, Gilles' Hobby project `shor-admin-panel`, under the scope `gilles-pothieus-projects` (the `target_url` of Vercel's commit statuses), connected to this repository through Vercel's GitHub App, which ShorRP holds for selected repositories only (`gh api orgs/ShorRP/installations`), and built a preview of each pushed commit (`gh api repos/ShorRP/Admin_Panel/deployments`; #9). Its production builds of 779396b failed (same command), one with « Invalid environment variables » for `DISCORD_GUILD_ID`, thrown by `parseDiscordApiEnv` from `next.config.ts`, which calls it only once `VERCEL` is set: the project exposes its system variables (Gilles' build log, 2026-10-27). Production got its variables with #13, and the first production deployment to succeed was 8dabf13's, at 15:12 UTC on 2026-10-29, after three failed attempts (GitHub deployment 7415200822).

## Feedback loops

- Tests: `pnpm test` runs the tooling's tests, `node --test` over `test/**/*.test.mjs`, then the panel's, Vitest over `src/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}` and `next.config.test.ts`. Vitest runs in Node, without a DOM: a test renders a component with `react-dom/server`, as the server does. One file: `node --test test/<name>.test.mjs` or `pnpm exec vitest run <path>`. No test of `pnpm test` calls the real Discord: an in-memory fake replaces it (**Testing**).
- Contract: `pnpm test:contract` runs the `DiscordApi` contract suite against the real Discord, through `vitest.contract.config.mts`, which loads `.env.local` through `process.loadEnvFile`, which leaves a variable the environment already sets alone (tried with Node 24.22.0, 2026-10-25), and which `pnpm test`'s config leaves out (**The contract suite**). It deletes the AutoMod rules it creates, all named `Contract …`, and the direct message it sends `CONTRACT_MEMBER_ID`, lifts the timeouts it gives that member and the ban it gives `CONTRACT_ABSENT_USER_ID`, which must not be banned when it starts, needs room on the Discord server for one keyword rule and at least one category, which its list of channels reads (#384; the test Discord server held 4 on 2026-11-05, a read-only probe), and fails before calling Discord when a variable is missing. It waits out a 429 and tries again, up to three times, since creating a rule allows 5 in a row (`X-RateLimit-Limit`, reset after 6 s, 2026-10-25), past the 2 s the client waits (**Retries**).
- End to end: `pnpm test:e2e` runs Playwright over `e2e/*.test.ts`, on Chromium, against `next start` serving the last `pnpm build`, so build first; install Chromium once with `pnpm exec playwright install chromium`. One test: `pnpm test:e2e <path>`. The calls the server makes for a test's `page` go to that test's fake, through the HTTP adapter, never to the real Discord (**End to end**).
- Typecheck: `pnpm typecheck` runs `next typegen`, which writes what a fresh clone lacks, the gitignored `next-env.d.ts` and the route types behind `PageProps` and `LayoutProps`, then `tsc --noEmit`.
- Lint: `pnpm lint`, ESLint with `eslint-config-next`'s Core Web Vitals and TypeScript configs, then the rules of `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y`'s `recommended` config, `react/no-danger` and `react/jsx-no-script-url` (**Headers**), the rule `admin-panel/responsive-classes` of `eslint-rules/`, which refuses in `src/**/*.{ts,tsx}` a fixed width above 20 rem and a grid of more than two columns that no breakpoint or container variant guards, and a height in `vh` (**Responsive**), and a `no-restricted-imports` that refuses, in `src/ui/`, any relative import and any `@/` import but `@/ui/*` (**Interface**), and anywhere in `src/` outside `*.test.*` files an import of the fake Discord or its HTTP adapter (**The Discord seam**) or of the tests' helpers, `src/discord/answering-fetch.ts`, the contract suite, `src/ticket-archives/archive-message.ts`, `src/support-tickets/support-ticket-channel.ts` and `src/game-log/game-log-message.ts`, or of the in-memory `Monitor` (**Monitoring**), and in `e2e/` any import of `@playwright/test` or `next/experimental/testmode/playwright` but in `e2e/fixtures.ts` (**End to end**), over the whole repo.
- Build: `pnpm build` (`next build`), which type-checks again. Dev server: `pnpm dev`.
- Comments: `pnpm check:comments`.
- Format: `pnpm format` lays out the repository with Prettier, `pnpm format:check` checks that it is laid out (**Code style**). One file: `pnpm exec prettier --write <path>`.
- Components: `pnpm exec shadcn add <component>` copies a shadcn/ui component into `src/ui/`, as `components.json` sets, and installs the packages it needs: read `pnpm-workspace.yaml` after (**Adding a dependency**), and `src/ui/globals.css` when the component brings theme variables, since the CLI looks for their dark values in a `.dark` rule (`selector:".dark"`, `node_modules/shadcn/dist/index.js`, shadcn 4.22.1), which the panel no longer has: those go under `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` (**Theme**; inferred from the code, not tried). Then strip its comments and turn its English strings French (**Interface**, **Copied code**). `pnpm exec shadcn add <component> --diff` shows how a copied file differs from the registry's.
- CI: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the comment check, the format check, the typecheck, the lint, the tests, the build, then end to end, after installing Chromium. Its `Check` job must pass before a PR merges, through the ruleset of **The rules of `main`**, which GitHub refused while the repository was private (HTTP 403 « Upgrade to GitHub Pro or make this repository public », checked 2026-10-23) and took once #100 made it public (2026-10-27). Never merge a PR whose CI is red.

## Commits, PRs, issues

- Commit messages read `Area: what changed`, in English.
- A branch is named `<area>/<subject>`: the area of the commits it carries, in lower case, such as `docs`, `tooling`, `interface`, `repo` or `dependencies` (**Dependencies**), then a few English words in kebab-case, with no issue number, since the PR gives it. A session renames its branch before its first push, since the desktop app names the branches of new sessions after a prefix of its own. Review checks it (Gilles, 2026-10-25, while grilling #57).
- The user is the sole author: commits carry no `Co-Authored-By` trailer, PRs carry no "Generated with" footer.
- Only the maintainer, Gilles (`docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`, **People**), or an agent running `deliver-epic` or `deliver-backlog`, which only he starts, merges into `main`, with a merge commit, never a squash. The repository allows merge commits only, since #100 turned squash and rebase off (**The rules of `main`**). Vercel's Hobby plan has no team collaboration, and deploys no private repository of a GitHub organization (https://vercel.com/docs/git, updated 2026-09-18), hence a public repository (ADR 0007). Its check that a commit's author owns the Hobby team is written for private repositories (same page, **Deploying private Git repositories**), and Gilles authors every commit anyway.
- Issues, PR descriptions and comments read like a teammate wrote them: direct, specific, complete, no filler.
- A PR that only advances a ticket names it once, as `Part of #<n>`. GitHub closes, at merge, every issue that a PR description or commit message puts right after `close`, `fix` or `resolve` (any form), even inside a negation.
- Once a PR merges, delete its local branch with `git branch -d` and run `git fetch --prune`. When `-d` refuses, run `git merge-base --is-ancestor <branch> origin/<default branch>`: `-d` also refuses a merged branch that is ahead of its upstream, or whose upstream is gone while `HEAD` does not hold it, as five branches of #34's run were (exit code 0, 2026-10-30, #272; git 2.55.0, throwaway repositories, 2026-10-30). A branch that fails the check holds unmerged work: ask the user, or, under `deliver-backlog`, list it in the run's report. Under `deliver-epic`, `finish-pbi.mjs` runs that check and deletes the branch with `-D` once it passes, and lists a branch it keeps.

## Gotchas

A trap of one area sits in that area's rule, under `.claude/rules/`, which Claude Code loads when a matching file is read or edited: `dependencies.md` (`package.json`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, held versions, `minimumReleaseAge`, Corepack on Vercel), `eslint.md`, `shadcn.md`, `discord.md` (tokens, the test Discord server, the contract suite in a worktree), `nextjs.md` (`next dev`, `next typegen`), `monitoring.md`, `next-link-prefetch.md`, `view-transitions.md`, `react-components.md` (Base UI, portals, `next/script`), `e2e.md` (Playwright, axe, the favicon, `load`), `claude-hooks.md`, `node-tests.md`, `ci.md`, `env.md` (variables on Vercel), `audit-log.md` (the period and the text of the audit log's filters, which are no trap) and `game-log.md` (the game log's page, reading, configuration and access, which are no trap either). The traps of the project board are in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`, **Gotchas**. Here stay the traps of the machine and the tooling that no path scopes:

- Under Git Bash, write a path inside a script as `C:/Users/…`: Node reads `/c/Users/…` as `C:\c\Users\…`, since MSYS converts only arguments.
- A U+00A0 typed into `Write` or `Edit` can land as an ordinary space, and `Read` shows one as a space too: after writing French text, count them with `grep -c $'\xc2\xa0' <file>`, or write a placeholder and replace it with `sed 's/<placeholder>/\xc2\xa0/g'`; the escape `\u00a0` typed into a JavaScript string lands as the character itself. The audit log's reason format holds one before its colon, so a test builds a reason through `auditLogReason`, never by hand.
- Several sessions can share this folder: before switching branches here, read `git status` and `git reflog -3`; a change that needs its own branch goes in a `git worktree`.
- The hooks of `.claude/settings.json` need `pnpm install` first: without `node_modules`, `gh pr create` and `gh pr new` are refused, and edits go unformatted. A hook added during a session may not run before the next one.
- On Windows, `git worktree remove` fails, exit code 255 and `Filename too long`, on a worktree that has run `pnpm install`, whose `node_modules` holds paths over 260 characters, and unlists it while its folder stays. Remove the folder, with `rm -rf <worktree>` from Git Bash, `Remove-Item -LiteralPath '\\?\C:\…\<worktree>' -Recurse -Force` in PowerShell, the path written in full, or Node's `fs.rmSync(<worktree>, { recursive: true, force: true })`, then run `git worktree prune`. A worktree still locked needs `git worktree unlock` first.

Issues live as GitHub issues in `ShorRP/Admin_Panel`, on the org project board **Admin Panel**, driven by the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` for the commands and for writing an issue in French.

The five canonical roles, each label string equal to its name. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.
